Issue:  VERSION 1 OF THE REALIZED PEAK TRAILING FLOOR IS AUTHORIZED IN DESIGN AND BLOCKED ON DEPLOYMENT BY THE D4 GATE. The ruling set D1 through D8 is FINAL in DECISIONS of 2026-08-24 and the design spec that accompanies it is complete, so nothing about the mechanism itself is open. What is open is the gate the owner attached to it: NO VERSION 1 BINARY REACHES MT5 while `P3-1` or `P49`/`P53` is open on the currently deployed build.
Action: TWO THINGS, AND ONLY THE SECOND IS BLOCKED. FIRST, implementation and vectors MAY PROCEED IN PARALLEL RIGHT NOW, which the ruling states explicitly, and the design spec names the exact surface so no later session has to re-derive it: a new `AgRealizedRunFold` in `Pnl.mqh` producing the running maximum from the existing single sorted walk, with `AgRealizedFold` reduced to a forwarder so that its FINAL frozen signature and its two outputs are preserved and no second history walk is added; a peak model block in `Persist.mqh` mirroring the floor block at lines 557 to 830 with `AgPeakPath`, `AgPeakSerialize`, `AgPeakSave`, `AgPeakResetModel`, `AgPeakQuarantine`, `AgPeakLoad` and `AgPeakUpdate`, differing from the floor block in ONE deliberate respect, that a corrupt peak RECONSTRUCTS FROM HISTORY rather than reseeding from the live limit; and in `AgEvaluateActive` one added call plus one changed comparison at `AccountGuardian.mq5:628-630`, placed inside the breach tail so that the PRE BREACH ONLY constraint and the Q10, Q10 amendment, Q8 and Q9 guards above it are all inherited rather than restated. Four acceptance rows are drafted and ready to be captured: profit then give back locking on full equity with the position still open, restart reconstruction with no re-inflation including the file deleted leg, stricter wins with both mechanisms live across a raise attempt, and rollover resetting peak and base together on one anchor value. Each closes only on quoted journal evidence plus the `.ex5` build hash, and each depends on four new journal lines that must exist before the row can be run, `realized peak reconciled`, `realized peak raised`, `realized peak reset at rollover` and `lock level`, the last being the only artifact that can show which of the two mechanisms won. SECOND, THE GATE ITSELF IS NEITHER THIS SESSION'S NOR THIS BRANCH'S WORK. `P3-1` and `P49`/`P53` live on `phase3-defect-fixes` and were explicitly out of scope for the session that took this ruling, which touched neither them nor that branch.
Status: UNBLOCKED, THE D4 GATE IS CLEARED AS OF 2026-08-26. `P3-1` CLOSED 2026-08-25 (Run A) and `P49`/`P53` CLOSED 2026-08-26 (Run B), both on the deployed build `1C88C4A1` and both on artifacts rather than on reports, so the gate condition that no version 1 binary reaches MT5 while those rows are open is satisfied. Design and implementation were never blocked and may run in parallel; deployment is now open as well.

Issue:  NEXT BEST ACTION. DEFECT 1 OF 4 IN THE FIX ORDER RULED 2026-08-19. A DISCONNECT OR A FROZEN QUOTE THAT STRADDLES THE LOCK EXPIRY LEAVES THE FIRST POST EXPIRY PASS UNGATED. The chain is three facts, each checkable on its own. `g_ag_resyncing` is set in exactly one place, the `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` guard at the head of `AgEvaluateActive`, which does not run while LOCKED. Lock expiry runs `AgTransition(AG_STATE_ACTIVE, "lock expired", "")`, entering ACTIVE DIRECTLY rather than through SYNCING, so the SYNCING stability polls do not run either. The Q10 coherence gate is written `if(g_ag_resyncing)`, so with that flag false the first post expiry pass takes no history stability check at all before it is eligible to decide a breach. LIVE EVIDENCE, `docs/evidence/journal-20260819-stage7-lock-expiry.txt`, 2026-08-19, build `74D666E9`: the server clock sat frozen for 61 minutes 32 seconds straight across the anchor, `AG|2026.08.18 23:57:59|LIFE|state=LOCKED|seconds_in_state=32283|waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until|quote_age=3691s|server=2026.08.18 23:57:59|local=2026.08.19 00:59:30`, then advanced an hour in one tick and expiry fired one second later, `AG|2026.08.19 01:00:01|TRANSITION|LOCKED->ACTIVE|lock expired|`. THE WHOLE FILE CARRIES EXACTLY THREE NON LIFE LINES, the expiry INFO, that TRANSITION and the ratchet reseed, and not one of them is a SYNCING or a RESYNC line, which is the artifact form of the gate never arming.
Action: IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE 2026-08-20 AS SHAPE A, commit `0a4c86b`, under the owner ruling of that date which is FINAL in DECISIONS. THE Issue LINE ABOVE DESCRIBES THE BUILD AS FOUND AND THE SOURCE NO LONGER MATCHES IT: expiry now runs `AgTransition(AG_STATE_SYNCING, "lock expired", ...)` at `AccountGuardian.mq5:489`, so the SYNCING stability polls run before the first pass eligible to declare a breach, unconditionally and therefore on both straddle routes, the disconnect and the frozen quote while connected. The expiry block also resets `g_ag_stable_polls` and `g_ag_last_history_total` at `:487-488`, which is not decoration: those globals advance only inside `AgHistoryStable`, which never runs while LOCKED, so without the reset the first SYNCING poll takes the count-unchanged branch and increments straight past `HistoryStablePolls`, leaving SYNCING on the tick it was entered. Shapes B and C of the plan are not taken and the ruling closes that choice. DEPLOYED AND COMPILED, CORRECTED 2026-08-24 ON OWNER RULING, ARTIFACTS GOVERN: this fix was BUILT AS `1C88C4A1` ON 2026-08-20 and that build has been DEPLOYED AND RUNNING SINCE 2026-08-23 22:25:57, md5 `1C88C4A155CF09AC38D71C3E5BFEF1D9` MEASURED BY HAND BY THE OWNER with `Get-FileHash` on the Experts path on 2026-08-24; the retired `74D666E9`, which carries the defect, is no longer the running binary, and per the ruling of 2026-08-20 this fix does not ship alone, it reaches the terminal in one build with defect 3.

THE BUILD IS TIED FIRST so the rest is about a known binary: `AccountGuardian.ex5` md5 `1C88C4A155CF09AC38D71C3E5BFEF1D9`, 78676 bytes, mtime 2026-08-20 20:33:18, hashed read only at the real data folder Experts path whose first component after `Terminal\` is `D0E8209F77C8CF37AD8BF550E51FF075`, per the check ruled on 2026-08-23. THE LOCK: `AG|2026.08.24 14:32:15|TRANSITION|ACTIVE->LOCKED|DAILY_BREACH|pnl=-111.70|limit=106.65|locked_until=2026.08.25 01:00:00` at `docs/evidence/journal-20260825-runa-lock-expiry.txt:33`, the owner having closed the losing position by hand at 14:32:30 and 14:32:33 per `docs/evidence/terminal-20260825-runa-lock-expiry.txt`, far ahead of the anchor. THE WINDOW is lines 33 to 1500 of the banked journal, opening at that lock and terminating at `AG|2026.08.25 02:41:10|INFO|deinit|reason=9|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=101707` at `:1500`, which is 1468 banked lines of which `:1172` is the file's own CONTINUES marker, leaving 1467 journal lines. THE THREE PREDICTED LINES, quoted from the banked file with line numbers and each found EXACTLY ONCE in that window. P25 at `:1293`, `AG|2026.08.25 01:00:05|INFO|lock expired|locked_until=2026.08.25 01:00:00|server=2026.08.25 01:00:05`, PASS. P26 at `:1294`, `AG|2026.08.25 01:00:05|TRANSITION|LOCKED->SYNCING|lock expired|history stability required before the first post-expiry breach decision|polls=0/3`, PASS, and its companion condition holds, ZERO `TRANSITION|LOCKED->ACTIVE` lines in the window. P27 at `:1295`, `AG|2026.08.25 01:00:08|TRANSITION|SYNCING->ACTIVE|history stable|polls=3/3`, PASS, landing THREE SECONDS after P26 on the server stamp, 01:00:05 to 01:00:08, and 3.001 seconds on the log local stamp, 01:00:03.720 to 01:00:06.721, which is the predicted three seconds at the one second timer. THE FOUR COUNTS, each a count over that window and not over the day file: `TRANSITION|LOCKED->ACTIVE` ZERO in window; `RESYNC entered` ZERO in window; `RESYNC exited` ZERO in window; `waiting_on=RESYNC: polls=` ZERO in window. P28 is also satisfied at ZERO `LIFE|state=SYNCING` lines in window, and it is recorded as satisfied rather than as evidence, exactly as the prediction demands. Issue:  DEFECT 2 OF 4 IN THE FIX ORDER RULED 2026-08-19. THE GV LOCK MIRROR ERASES ITSELF ON EVERY COLD BOOT, BEFORE ITS OWN WITNESS CAN READ IT. The mechanism is three lines of source in one callback. `OnTimer` writes the mirror at `AccountGuardian.mq5:881`, `GlobalVariableSet(AgGvLock(), (double)(long)g_ag_locked_until)` followed by `GlobalVariablesFlush()`, guarded only by `if(g_owns_mutex)`, and the boot derivation that READS that mirror runs at `AccountGuardian.mq5:926`, in the SAME `OnTimer`, AFTER the write. On a cold boot `g_ag_locked_until` is 0, because the only thing that sets it is `AgEnterLockFromBoot`, which does not run until the SYNCING exit several ticks later, so the first ticks flush a zero over the persisted mirror and the witness then fails the `(datetime)(long)gv_raw > now` test. LIVE EVIDENCE, `docs/evidence/journal-20260818-stage7-forced-kill.txt`, 2026-08-18, build `74D666E9`, three boots in one file and the split is exact. Cold boot one, `AG|2026.08.18 13:46:41|TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s`, fired FILE and DERIVED at 13:46:44 and NO GV line. Cold boot two, `AG|2026.08.18 16:01:24|TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s`, fired FILE and DERIVED at 16:01:27 and NO GV line. The ONLY GV firing in the whole day is `AG|2026.08.18 15:36:27|INFO|boot witness GV fired|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, on the one boot whose transition reads `LOCKED->SYNCING` and which therefore preserved its memory.
Action: FIX SECOND, per the ruled order, and the fix itself is not written in this session. Fix shape named and NOT RULED, since the owner reserves the design: the write is unconditional on state and could be gated, or ordered after the dispatch, or the boot read could be taken once in `OnInit` before any timer tick. SEVERITY, stated so the fix is scoped against what is actually lost: the GV witness fires only when the EA RETAINED ITS MEMORY, which is exactly the case where a mirror is redundant, and it is guaranteed zeroed in the case it was built for, a genuine restart. So the independent third recovery path does not exist in practice, and with the state file deleted a restart recovery rests on the derived history witness ALONE, making the strictest wins OR over three witnesses an OR over two. This does NOT weaken any lock the file and derived witnesses carry, and it does weaken the deletion resistance argument that justified building three.
Status: OPEN

Issue:  P2-H IS OPEN AND IS NOT CLOSABLE BY CODE OR BY CODE READING, ruled 2026-08-19 and FINAL in DECISIONS of that date. The row's claim is that a limit raised while locked produces a loud WARN while the Q6 snapshot holds. LIVE EVIDENCE, `docs/evidence/journal-20260818-stage7-p2h-input-change.txt`, 2026-08-18, build `74D666E9`: the owner raised `DailyLossPercent` from 5 to 10 while the account was locked at 15:36 and there are ZERO `input changed while LOCKED` lines in the journal, so the row is UNTESTED rather than passed or failed. The cause was published before the row ran and is now ruled intended behaviour: MT5 reloads the EA on an input change, `INFO|deinit|reason=5`, the reload is visible as `AG|2026.08.18 15:36:26|TRANSITION|LOCKED->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s`, and `AgEnterLockFromBoot` reseeds the Q6 input witness from the LIVE inputs at `AccountGuardian.mq5:386` to `:388`, so the witness captured percent 10 and had nothing left to warn about.
Action: WAIT FOR THE LIVE WINDOW THE RULING NAMES, the coming weekend market close, and do nothing else with this row. A substitute test stays FORBIDDEN under the standing instruction of 2026-08-18, since a substitute would prove something else while carrying this row's name. Recorded so the two entries are not read as contradicting each other: the reseed ruling of 2026-08-19 settles that the reseed behaviour is correct and is not a defect, and it does NOT close this row, because what P2-H measures is whether the WARN fires on a LOCKED evaluation pass that observes a divergence, which no input change through the dialog can produce while the reload always reseeds first. The row may prove unreachable through the terminal UI altogether, and that outcome is the owner's to declare rather than the executor's to infer.

Stated precisely so no later session miscounts: a plain search for LOCKED returns exactly one hit in each of Saturday and Sunday and BOTH are the substring inside `locked_until` in the boot line `INFO|lock state file loaded|reason=-|locked_until=1970.01.01 00:00:00|weighed by the boot derivation at the SYNCING exit`, which is the epoch value a file with no lock carries. There was no exposure to lock on: every LIFE line reads `realized=0.00|floating=0.00|base=2133.00|limit=106.65` and the terminal journal confirms `0 positions, 0 orders` at each authorization. THE REASON THE WEEKEND DID NOT SUPPLY THE ROW HAS NOTHING TO DO WITH THE MARKET BEING SHUT: the row needs a lock, a lock needs a losing position, and there was none. So the market close was necessary for the row's setting and was never sufficient for the row itself, which is worth writing down because the 2026-08-19 ruling named the weekend as the window and a later session could otherwise read this harvest as the window having failed. Issue:  `.gitignore` SILENTLY SWALLOWED THIRTEEN EVIDENCE JOURNALS AND `git add -A` REPORTED SUCCESS EVERY TIME. Found 2026-08-19 while preparing the Stage 7 merge, by reading `git diff --stat main HEAD` and seeing FOUR files where the ledger's own narrative claims a dozen banked journals. The mechanism is one line: `.gitignore` carries a bare `*.log` under its "Junk" heading, intended for build and tool noise, and every journal this session copied out of the Terminal folder was named `.log` because that is what MT5 names them. So thirteen `git add -A` calls across two days each matched nothing, each exited 0, and each was followed by a commit that appeared to succeed. THE FAILURE MODE IS THE DANGEROUS SHAPE: not an error, not a warning, a silent no-op behind a successful command, and the executor asserted in ledger entries and to the owner that files were banked when they were not. The existing convention in `docs/evidence` is `.txt`, visible in `journal-20260810-monday-reopen.txt` and the `agvec-*.txt` vector captures, and the executor broke it by carrying the MT5 extension through unchanged. `state_1200252169.dat.live-breach-2026-08-18` committed normally and was never at risk, because `.dat` matches no ignore rule, which is why the loss was partial and therefore easy to miss.
Action: FIXED ON THE BRANCH by owner instruction of 2026-08-19, which changed the earlier disposition: all thirteen renamed from `.log` to `.txt`, matching the convention, and every LEDGER.md path citing them rewritten, verified by a grep for `docs/evidence/*.log` returning ZERO. THE SECOND EXPOSURE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIRST AND IS WHY THIS ENTRY STAYS OPEN: the files sat under `.claude/worktrees/phase2-stage7/`, and `.gitignore` also excludes `.claude/` entirely with the comment that everything under it is worktrees which must never enter history. A worktree is removed when its session ends. So the evidence was not merely uncommitted, it was one routine cleanup away from being destroyed, and the ledger cited its paths as the basis of findings that would then have rested on nothing. WHAT KEEPS THIS OPEN is that the mechanism is untouched: a future session copying a `.log`, a `.pyc`, an `.ex5` or anything else matching the ignore list into `docs/evidence` will hit exactly the same silent no-op. Two candidate guards are named and NEITHER is ruled, since the owner reserved it: a negation rule such as `!docs/evidence/**` so that directory is never ignorable, or a discipline that every evidence bank is followed by `git ls-files` on the path rather than by trusting the commit. THE STANDING LESSON, stated plainly: a successful `git add -A` is not evidence that a file was added, and this ledger's evidentiary standard requires the tracked list to be quoted rather than the command's exit code.
Updated 2026-08-23, SECOND LIVE EXPOSURE OF THE SAME UNCHANGED MECHANISM, and it was caught before anything was lost. The owner collected four EA journals for the weekend harvest into `C:\Users\www83\Downloads\AI\AccountGuardian\tmp`, at the REPOSITORY root and not at the Terminal data folder root where they were reported to be. Every file in that folder is named `.log`, because that is what MT5 names them, so all four were matched by the bare `*.log` rule under the Junk heading and were invisible to git: `git status` on the primary checkout read clean with 5.9 megabytes of primary evidence sitting untracked beside it, and a `git clean` would have taken all four. This is the same silent no-op the entry above describes, arriving through a different door: not an executor copying into `docs/evidence`, but the OWNER staging evidence at the repository root. The files are now banked as `.txt` at commit `c843be5` and the tracked list was quoted with `git ls-files` rather than trusting the commit. THE MECHANISM IS STILL UNCHANGED AND STILL UNRULED, and the two candidate guards named above are unchanged; what this exposure adds is that a negation such as `!docs/evidence/**` would NOT have covered this case, since the files were never under `docs/evidence`, which is worth knowing before either guard is chosen.
Status: OPEN, the thirteen files are recovered and tracked, four more were caught untracked on 2026-08-23, and the mechanism that loses them is unchanged and unruled

Issue:  UNRULED 2026-08-18. PHASE 3 DESIGN INPUT, MAXIMUM POSITION SIZE DERIVED FROM THE DAILY LIMIT. Owner input of this date, recorded during Stage 7 between Part 2 and Part 3, and it is an INPUT rather than a ruling: it is written here in the owner's terms and nothing in it is settled. The hazard: a position large enough to lose the entire daily limit in one move defeats the guardian, because the guardian locks only after the loss has landed. The proposal: the sweep in Phase 3 gains a size gate, and a position whose potential loss at a reasonable stop distance exceeds a fraction of the current enforced limit is closed on detection, through the same trade API family as sweep and flatten. Formula and fraction are to be ruled at Phase 3 kickoff. NOTHING IN PHASE 2 CHANGES and no code was touched when this was recorded.
Action: Four consequences the executor derived while recording this, offered as material for the kickoff and explicitly NOT as rulings or as a recommendation either way. ONE, this would be the first trade API call in the project. S1 and S2 have held at literally zero calls since Phase 0, most recently re run this session, and the only hit either row has ever returned is the header comment in `Sweep.mqh` asserting the rule. A size gate that closes positions changes the meaning of those rows from "no call exists" to "calls exist and are confined to `Sweep.mqh`", which is a different and weaker invariant, and the kickoff should decide the rows' new wording deliberately rather than let them quietly degrade. TWO, it sits in tension with ruling TWO, which keeps positions open when a lock is declared. Every enforcement the guardian owns today refuses, halts or locks and none of it touches a position; this one closes one. Reconciling the two is a kickoff question, not a detail: the natural reading is that ruling TWO governs the post breach window and the size gate governs the pre breach window, but that reading is not written anywhere and should not be assumed. THREE, "a fraction of the current enforced limit" has three candidate referents after question SEVEN and they diverge. The cascade is snapshot, then floor, then live, and the ratcheted floor can sit well below the live limit, so the same rule reads differently depending on which tier it binds to. FOUR, "potential loss at a reasonable stop distance" needs per symbol data the project does not yet hold: tick value, contract size and the broker's stops level. That is the Q7 symbol specification table, still unread since Phase 1 and still carrying the A8 corroboration debt, so this proposal inherits that debt rather than adding a new one.
UPDATED 2026-08-25 by the merge of `evidence/manual-trades-20260811`, on the fourth consequence's factual clause only; the consequence itself stands unchanged. The Q7 symbol specification table is NOT unread. It was read at the terminal on 2026-08-11 and Q7 closed WHOLE that day, discharging the A8 corroboration debt, evidence `docs/evidence/phase1-manual-trades-2026-08-11.md` with `docs/evidence/journal-20260811-manual-trades.txt` and `docs/evidence/terminal-journal-20260811-manual-trades.txt`, landed on main by commit `d4e2869`. So this proposal inherits NO corroboration debt. What it still needs is unchanged: the per symbol tick value, contract size and broker stops level carried into whatever formula the Phase 3 kickoff rules.
Status: UNRULED, Phase 3 input only, blocks nothing in Phase 2 and awaits the Phase 3 kickoff

Issue:  Next best action, and it is the owner's to run rather than the executor's. THE README TOKEN USAGE BADGE WRITER IS STILL LIVE AND IT LIVES OUTSIDE THIS REPOSITORY. The block itself is gone from README.md at commit `b8678f1`, eleven lines removed including the `<!--TOKENS-->` and `<!--/TOKENS-->` delimiters, per the owner's ruling of 2026-08-17 which is FINAL in DECISIONS of that date. That removal does NOT hold on its own and the reason is measured in the writer's own source rather than argued. Location, named exactly so the owner can act without hunting: `C:\Users\www83\.claude\token-tracker\`, which is inside the user profile and outside both this repository and the MetaTrader Terminal folder. Three parts. The script `Update-TokenReport.ps1`, 13284 bytes, last written 2026-08-02 16:25:26. The registration `projects.json`, which maps project id `lockmt5` to path `C:\Users\www83\Downloads\AI\AccountGuardian`, names this repository's README.md as its target, and carries `autoCommit` true. And the Windows Task Scheduler task `ClaudeTokenReport`, daily at 08:00 with StartWhenAvailable, read this session as State Ready, LastRunTime 2026-08-17 08:00:01, LastTaskResult 0, NextRunTime 2026-08-18 08:00:00. Its own log `run.log` records a run on every one of 2026-08-14, 08-15, 08-16 and 08-17, each ending `git=committed`, and the commit string it writes is `chore: token usage badge [skip ci]`, emitted at `Update-TokenReport.ps1:241`. THE MECHANISM THAT MATTERS, and it is the whole reason this entry exists rather than a note: `Update-Readme` at `:186` to `:211` does not merely replace an existing block. If the marker pair is present it replaces in place at `:199`. If the file is non empty and the markers are ABSENT it PREPENDS the entire badge to the top of the file at `:203`. So deleting the block is precisely the input that makes the job re author it whole, which is exactly the sequence that produced the committed conflict markers the 2026-08-17 merge caught: the owner deleted the header by hand, the job prepended a fresh block above what remained, and the two states were committed together.
Action: OWNER DISABLES IT. The executor did not, and will not, because the instruction of 2026-08-17 says to name the location and stop when the writer lives outside this repository. Two routes, neither recommended over the other because they differ in scope rather than in effect on this repository. Narrow, leaving the tracker running for any other project: delete the `lockmt5` object from the `projects` array in `C:\Users\www83\.claude\token-tracker\projects.json`, which stops both the README write and the auto commit for this repository while leaving the job itself alone. Whole, since the badge is ruled removed for good: `Disable-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'ClaudeTokenReport'`, or `Unregister-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'ClaudeTokenReport' -Confirm:$false` to remove it outright. Until one of those runs, expect the block back at the top of README.md at the next 08:00 firing, committed locally under the badge string above. The repository side was searched and is clean, recorded so nobody searches it twice: no `.github` directory exists at all, `.git/hooks` carries only the 212 byte `commit-msg` attribution stripper alongside the stock samples, a grep for TOKENS, token usage badge and token-usage across the whole tree returns hits only inside README.md itself and in prose inside LEDGER.md and docs/PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md, and the owner's PowerShell profile at `C:\Users\www83\OneDrive\Documents\WindowsPowerShell\Microsoft.PowerShell_profile.ps1` carries `Initialize-GitWorkspace` and `Start-ClaudeWorkspace` but writes no badge and names no TOKENS marker anywhere. Nothing under the MetaTrader Terminal folder was read, written or approached, per Rule A.
Status: OPEN, owner action, block removed at `b8678f1` and it returns at the next scheduled run until the job is disabled

Issue:  A PRIMARY ARTIFACT WAS DESTROYED BY THE EXECUTOR ON 2026-08-17 and the gap is permanent. The Sunday EA journal 20260816.log, 1466866 bytes, was deleted by a PowerShell call in which a helper function named `Rd` was shadowed by the built in AllScope alias `rd` for `Remove-Item`, aliases resolving ahead of functions. The call read as returning an empty file rather than as having deleted one, so it was not caught until the following call reported the path as missing. Recovery failed for want of elevation: `Win32_ShadowCopy` returns an initialization failure and `Get-ComputerRestorePoint` returns access denied, and `Remove-Item` does not use the Recycle Bin. What is permanently lost is Sunday 2026-08-16 from local 11:14:03 to end of day, which is exactly the window holding the sleep entry at 12:33:15, the wake at 15:51:05 and the single DEGRADED line at that wake. Committed snapshots preserve Sunday only to 11:13:33, at commits a61373e and 6c6bced. The terminal side journal for the same day was NOT touched and is preserved at docs/evidence/terminal-20260816-fullday-sleep-window.txt; Saturday's and Monday's files are unaffected.
Action: No recovery action remains and none is proposed. Two consequences are recorded so no later session overstates the record. First, the DEGRADED line at the 2026-08-16 wake may be cited as corroborated by timing and by code but NEVER as a quoted line, since no artifact holds it; the corroboration is the terminal reauthorization at 15:51:11.765 postdating the EA emission at 15:51:05, plus the Q10 gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:121. Second, P1-B's Sunday scan is incomplete for this reason in addition to the genuine 3 hour 18 minute sleep hole, which is why the 2026-08-17 ACTIONS entry recommends a re harvest next weekend rather than closing the row on reconstruction. A standing rule is added for executors and it is the reusable part of this entry: never name a helper with a short token, since `rd`, `del`, `ls`, `cp`, `mv` and `sc` are built in aliases that silently win over any same named function, and prefer inline code over helpers entirely when the code path touches evidence files. This sits with the CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06 and the stale directory entry trap of 2026-08-16 as the third measurement trap this project has hit.
RECOVERED BY OWNER 2026-08-17, and the restriction this entry imposed is LIFTED. Method, recorded because it is the reason the record survived and because it names the separation that must hold: the owner restored the file through Windows Previous Versions, an elevated facility the executor does not have and must not have. The file came back IN PLACE at its original path with its original size of 1466866 bytes and its original last write time of 2026-08-17 00:00:05. Under the read only rule it was NOT modified and NOT moved; it was copied out to docs/evidence/journal-20260816-fullday-recovered.txt, 733432 bytes after the UTF-16LE to UTF-8 transcode, md5 93BF21B92553E8820046DD95CA1E62BB, 2485 lines. Authenticity was verified rather than assumed: the previously committed 11:13:33 snapshot byte compares as a STRICT PREFIX of the recovered file, 397966 bytes comparing equal, so the recovered remainder is continuous with what was already banked and is not a substitute or a partial. Day totals on the recovered file are 2485 LIFE lines, 7 DEGRADED, 0 RESYNC, 0 WARN, 0 ALERT and 0 non LIFE lines. Six of the seven DEGRADED lines were already known from the surviving prefix, so the seventh is the wake line, exactly as the owner reported and exactly one.

The three lines this entry declared unquotable are now QUOTED, and any session may cite them as artifact backed. Last clean line before the sleep: `12:33:15.265 ... LIFE|state=ACTIVE|seconds_in_state=172857|waiting_on=-|anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|realized=-52.84|floating=0.00|base=1985.97|limit=99.30|server=2026.08.14 23:57:59|local=2026.08.16 12:33:15`. The wake line: `15:51:05.468 ... LIFE|state=ACTIVE|seconds_in_state=184727|waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED|anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|realized=-52.84|floating=0.00|base=1985.97|limit=99.30|server=2026.08.14 23:57:59|local=2026.08.16 15:51:05`. The line after it, already clean: `15:51:35.478 ... waiting_on=-`. The sleep signature is therefore confirmed on artifact: a single LIFE gap of 11870 seconds with no lines inside it, `seconds_in_state` advancing 172857 to 184727 by exactly that amount so the counter never reset, the anchor and every number identical either side, and exactly one DEGRADED line at the wake which cleared by the next emission. The network reauthorized at 15:51:11.765 and the next LIFE line at 15:51:35.478 was already clean, so the roughly three second RESYNC window fell entirely between two thirty second emissions. This is the sampling blindness diagnosis making a successful prediction on data that did not exist when the diagnosis was written.

Issue:  P1-K IS UNREACHABLE BY ITS NAMED PROCEDURE, established 2026-08-14 by a deliberate disconnect that produced none of the row's required artifact, and it is a permanent scope limit rather than a failed attempt. The row reads "HistorySelect false (P1-10) | disconnect mid-session | loud WARN distinct from zero-deals, no ACTIVE transition from that tick". The disconnect ran 12:27:40.793 to 12:32:15.828 and the whole day's journal contains ZERO WARN lines, counted rather than sampled. The mechanism is in the source and it is not a subtlety: AccountGuardian.mq5:121 tests !TERMINAL_CONNECTED and returns at :126 before any history call whatsoever, and Pnl.mqh:178 carries the same guard a second time. So pulling the network sets g_ag_degraded and exits the pass early, and HistorySelect is never invoked, which means the false return the row exists to observe cannot arise from a disconnect on this build. The Q10 DEGRADED gate PRE-EMPTS the P1-K path completely. This is the same class as the Q8 cadence cap and amendment A5's PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION: not a gap in the evidence but a property of the code that makes the named route incapable of producing the artifact.
Action: Owner rules, and there are three coherent options rather than one obvious fix, so none is recommended as authority. Reclassify P1-K as PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION on the source read above, on the ground that the branch it guards is now proven unreachable in the disconnect case and therefore cannot misbehave there. Or re-scope the row to a trigger that reaches HistorySelect while the terminal is still connected, which the executor has not identified and does not claim exists. Or strike the row and fold its intent into P1-O, since what P1-K was really protecting against, silent bad numbers during a history failure, is what the DEGRADED marker now covers on the disconnect path. Nothing changed in the code. Evidence: docs/evidence journal artifacts for 2026-08-14, whole day WARN count zero, plus source read at AccountGuardian.mq5:118-127 and Pnl.mqh:178.
RULED 2026-08-17: the owner adopts the first of the three options, PASS BY CONSTRUCTION, on the source read recorded above. FINAL in DECISIONS of that date.
Status: CLOSED, PASS BY CONSTRUCTION, ruled 2026-08-17

Issue:  P1-O REMAINS OPEN AND THE RESYNC GATE HAS NEVER EXECUTED LIVE, plus a second and independent defect found in the same run. Two live disconnects now exist, the 2026-08-13 wake from sleep and the 2026-08-14 deliberate cut, and neither produced a single RESYNC line. Of P1-O's four clauses, two are met and two are not. Met: the DEGRADED prefix appears on BOTH the waiting_on field and the numbers field group, observed at 12:28:03 and 12:28:33; and zero ALERTs fired during the gap, confirmed against a whole day count of zero. Not met: "DEGRADED throughout the gap", since only 2 DEGRADED lines exist against a gap of roughly nine LIFE lines; and the RESYNC clause, with zero RESYNC lines in the day. The immediate cause of the miss was contamination, three chart period changes at 12:28:36, 12:28:39 and 12:28:42 which forced the EA out of ACTIVE into a fresh SYNCING entry, so the reconnect came up through the ordinary stability counter instead of the reconnect coherence path at AccountGuardian.mq5:140. The row therefore needs one clean re-run with nothing touched.
Action: Re-run the disconnect with the chart, the timeframe and the properties dialog all left alone, holding the cut long enough for six or more LIFE lines, and watch for the RESYNC prefix on reconnect. Recorded separately and NOT caused by the contamination, because it would have been true of an untouched run in the same circumstances and it is the more useful finding of the two: WHILE DISCONNECTED BUT IN SYNCING, THE EA CARRIES NO DEGRADED MARKER AT ALL. The seven SYNCING lines from 12:29:03 to 12:32:03 read waiting_on=polls=0/3 with no degraded indication of any kind, and the terminal did not reconnect until 12:32:15, so every one of those lines was emitted offline. A reader of those lines cannot tell the terminal was down, which is the fail-visible property the A3 proof-of-life ruling exists to protect and which the SPEC states at section 6: a stuck guardian and a healthy one must never look identical from outside. Whether that wants a fix is an owner ruling and no mechanism is proposed here; it is noted that the same ruling interacts with the stale quote entry above, since both concern what the LIFE line tells a reader about the trustworthiness of its own numbers. Evidence: the 2026-08-14 journal artifact, lines 12:27:03 through 12:32:33, with the terminal log bracketing the true gap at 12:27:40.793 and 12:32:15.828.
Updated 2026-08-16 by the weekend freeze harvest, which supplies the clean re-run this entry asked for and REVERSES this entry's central claim. The machine sat untouched all weekend, so contamination is impossible, and the terminal lost its connection four times on 2026-08-16 without anyone staging it. Two of those losses were long enough for the 30 second LIFE cadence to sample, 93.8 seconds from 01:40:07.063 and 80.9 seconds from 05:30:17.549, and BOTH produced exactly three DEGRADED lines with the marker on the `waiting_on` field and the numbers field group together, with exact boundaries: no clean line inside either gap, no DEGRADED line outside one. Zero ALERTs, against a whole day non LIFE count of zero. So the clause this entry recorded as not met, "DEGRADED throughout the gap", is now MET, twice, and the "zero ALERTs" clause is met again. What this entry got wrong is the RESYNC conclusion. It read zero RESYNC lines as the gate never executing. The count is now zero across FOUR live disconnects, and the better explanation is that the gate is unobservable rather than absent: the timer is 1 second and `HistoryStablePolls` is 3, so a RESYNC episode lasts about 3 seconds, while the RESYNC prefix is only ever printed on a LIFE line and LIFE lines are 30 seconds apart. That is a one in ten sampling chance per event, and four events at that rate most likely yield zero. Nothing announces RESYNC entry or exit, since the weekend contains zero non LIFE lines of any kind. The same blindness is visible from the other direction in the two SHORT losses of 0.6 and 0.7 seconds at 01:52:24.723 and 05:31:48.284, which produced no DEGRADED line at all because they opened and closed between two emissions. The condition existed and the artifact does not record it. P1-O as written therefore asks a 30 second sampler to catch a 3 second signal, so the row can only close by luck, and the executor claims neither that the gate works nor that it is broken, because these artifacts cannot decide it. The SYNCING DEGRADED omission recorded in the Action above is UNAFFECTED and still needs its ruling; it did not recur this weekend only because the EA held ACTIVE throughout and never entered SYNCING. Evidence: docs/evidence/journal-20260816-weekend-freeze-sunday-partial.txt and docs/evidence/terminal-20260816-weekend-disconnects.txt.
RULED 2026-08-17: P1-O CLOSES on its met clauses, and the RESYNC clause together with the SYNCING while disconnected DEGRADED omission are reclassified as ONE Phase 2 observability item rather than as defects. FINAL in DECISIONS of that date. Corroborated after the ruling by the recovered Sunday journal, which supplies a FIFTH live disconnect: the wake at 15:51:05.468 carried the DEGRADED marker, the network reauthorized at 15:51:11.765, and the next LIFE line at 15:51:35.478 was already clean, so the roughly three second RESYNC window again fell entirely between two thirty second emissions. That is the sampling blindness diagnosis predicting correctly on data that did not exist when it was written, which strengthens the ruling's ground that this is an observation channel limitation and not a gate defect.
Status: CLOSED on met clauses 2026-08-17; RESYNC observability and the SYNCING DEGRADED omission carried to Phase 2 as one item

Issue:  QUOTE STALENESS IS NOT A DEGRADED CONDITION, measured live 2026-08-14 and it is the other half of the hazard Q10 was written for. Q10 covers the disconnect: the gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:121 is `if(!(bool)TerminalInfoInteger(TERMINAL_CONNECTED))` and that single condition is the ONLY thing that sets g_ag_degraded. Nothing anywhere tests how old the quote actually is. The consequence was observed for sixty two continuous minutes on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14: the broker's daily metals break stopped ticks at server 2026.08.13 23:57:59, TimeCurrent froze there, and every LIFE line from the 00:29:31 boot through 00:59:32 printed `state=ACTIVE|waiting_on=-` with `floating=-49.45` unchanged and a server stamp forty minutes and then sixty minutes behind the local stamp on the same line. The terminal was connected throughout, so the DEGRADED branch never ran, and the banner read perfectly healthy over numbers that were an hour old. Both stamps are on every line, so the staleness is fully visible in the artifact and simply not acted on. Distinguish from the 2026-08-13 wake, which is the same file and looks superficially similar but is the OPPOSITE case and did work correctly: there the terminal was genuinely disconnected, DEGRADED fired on the first pass, and the line carried the `DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions` marker exactly as Q10 requires. So the disconnect half is proven working and the connected-but-frozen half is proven missing, on the same day, in the same log.
Action: Owner ruling needed, and the reason it cannot be deferred to Phase 2 as a cosmetic matter is that it governs breach decisions. Q10's stated principle is no breach decisions from a frozen quote, and this window is a frozen quote that the code does not recognise as one, so a Phase 2 enforcement build would be free to evaluate, and to lock, on an hour old price every single trading night. The window is not rare or accidental: it is the broker's scheduled daily break, so it recurs every trading day for roughly one hour, and it straddles the 01:00 anchor boundary where the day's numbers reset. Nothing is changed in the code by this entry. A concrete detection mechanism IS proposed below at owner request of 2026-08-14, as a proposal for ruling and not as authority, and no session may cite it as ruled until the owner rules it.

Proposed mechanism, four parts. Part one, measure quote age locally and never by clock arithmetic. Keep a global g_ag_last_tick_local, set to TimeLocal() on any pass where the value of TimeCurrent() differs from the previous pass, and define quote_age as TimeLocal() minus g_ag_last_tick_local. This deliberately avoids computing a server to local offset, because the broker DST peg is an open measurement obligation in this ledger until the October harvest and any offset arithmetic would silently inherit that unknown. A purely local delta is immune to it. Part two, do not treat a closed market as a stale quote, which is the distinction that decides whether this marker means anything. A frozen quote while the symbol's quote session is closed is expected and correct, and marking it DEGRADED would make DEGRADED the normal state across every weekend and every nightly break, which devalues the marker to noise. Gate the DEGRADED verdict on the session being open, read from SymbolInfoSessionQuote for the guarded symbol, and when the session is closed emit a distinct non DEGRADED note in waiting_on, market closed, so the artifact still records the condition without claiming a fault. Part three, the threshold is a compile time constant and NOT a ninth input, proposed at 120 seconds. It stays a constant for the same reason Q1 fixed the anchor offset as one, and because A9 established that every input carries a full validation vector cost. 120 seconds cannot false fire on XAUUSD in an open session, where ticks arrive many times a second, and it catches the observed 62 minute break within two LIFE lines. Part four, and this is the part most likely to be got wrong, the stale branch must set g_ag_degraded but must NOT set g_ag_resyncing. The Q10 reconnect coherence ruling of 2026-08-09 reuses the history stability counter because a disconnect genuinely invalidates history, but a stale quote never broke HistorySelect, so forcing a three poll RESYNC on tick resume would delay the first evaluation of the new day by three seconds at exactly the 01:00 anchor boundary, which is the one moment in the day where prompt evaluation matters most. Two things the mechanism must not disturb, both measured this session and both easy to break: the day rollover must still fire on the first pass where TimeCurrent crosses the anchor, which is what was observed at 01:00:00.763 when ticks resumed, so any early return in the stale branch must not sit ahead of anchor maintenance; and the Q8 high water latch must be untouched, since a frozen clock never moves the anchor backward and no staleness handling should give it a way to. Evidence: docs/evidence/journal-20260814-observed-rollover.txt, the whole run of ACTIVE lines from 00:30:02 to 00:59:32, and docs/evidence/journal-20260814-rollover-window-extract.txt for the 00:55 to 01:06 window showing the freeze ending exactly at the rollover. Source read this session at AccountGuardian.mq5:118-127 and Pnl.mqh:178.
SCOPED 2026-08-17 by owner ruling, FINAL in DECISIONS of that date: this stays OPEN as a PHASE 2 INPUT and is NOT a Phase 1 blocker, because Phase 1 only observes while Phase 2 locks, so the hazard bites only once enforcement exists. The four part detection mechanism proposed above remains a PROPOSAL and is explicitly NOT ruled by that entry; no session may cite it as ruled.
RULED 2026-08-18 at the Phase 2 kickoff, FINAL in DECISIONS of that date, and the ruling goes AGAINST the mechanism proposed above rather than adopting it. Enforcement is NOT gated on quote freshness: while the terminal is CONNECTED a breach decision may be taken from a quote the guardian cannot prove is fresh, and the guardian acts on the last known price exactly as if it were current. The connection state is the whole boundary, so Q10's DEGRADED behaviour on a genuine DISCONNECT is unchanged and still blocks every decision, while the connected but frozen window this entry measured over 62 minutes on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14 is now ruled a normal enforcing condition. The four part mechanism survives only as an OBSERVABILITY proposal, parts one and two, the local tick age measure and the market closed distinction, and even those are unratified as an implementation: they may log a note, and they may never suppress, delay or gate a decision. Parts three and four, the threshold used as a gate and the RESYNC exclusion, are MOOT under the ruling. Design consequence carried into Phase 2 rather than left implicit: a Q6 breach snapshot captured from a computation whose floating leg priced off a frozen quote is valid by design, so no freshness field is added to the state file.
Status: RULED 2026-08-18, enforcement not gated on quote freshness while connected; the four part mechanism survives as an observability proposal only, still unratified as an implementation

Issue:  A CARRY POSITION DRAGS ITS WHOLE UNREALIZED LOSS INTO EVERY NEW DAY, measured at the 2026-08-14 rollover and raised as a design question rather than a defect, because it is what the Q2 identity as ruled actually produces. At the crossing, realized correctly reset to 0.00 and base correctly stayed at 1985.97 since there was no realized amount to fold in, but floating did not reset and was never going to: it read -49.45 on the last line of the old day and -45.65 on the first line of the new one, the difference being live price movement across the boundary and nothing else. The open carry, ticket 411788798, has been held since 2026-08-11. CORRECTED 2026-08-14 by measurement at the terminal: this entry originally said its floating "already contains two nights of swap", repeating the owner's instruction of 2026-08-13, and that is measurably FALSE. Swap on this instrument and account is exactly zero, shown twice, on the floating side where 4399.90 minus 4347.65 is 52.25 against a profit of -52.25, and on the realized side where 4399.90 minus the 4347.06 close fill is 52.84 against realized of -52.84, both exact to the cent with no residual after three nights held. Equity carries swap, so the equity minus balance check is an independent second witness. The design question this entry raises is UNAFFECTED and if anything cleaner: the carry dragged its whole unrealized loss across the boundary as pure price movement, with no financing component to argue about. So the new day opened at -45.65 against a limit of -99.30, forty six percent of the day's entire allowance consumed at second zero, on a loss that day did not produce.
Action: Owner rules whether this is intended. It is consistent with every current FINAL entry and nothing here reopens any of them, which is precisely why it needs raising rather than fixing: the Phase 1 build only observes, so today the consequence is a number on a banner, but a Phase 2 enforcement build would lock the account on a multi-day drawdown attributed entirely to the day that happens to be running when it crosses. The two obvious framings are that the daily limit governs the account's total exposure regardless of when the loss was earned, which is the current behaviour and defensible for a prop firm style daily drawdown rule, or that the day's PnL should measure only what changed during the day, which would need a floating baseline snapshotted at each anchor. Naming both so the ruling has something to choose between; neither is ruled and no code is touched. Evidence: docs/evidence/journal-20260814-rollover-window-extract.txt, the adjacent lines at 00:59:32 and 01:00:02.
SCOPED 2026-08-17 by owner ruling, FINAL in DECISIONS of that date: this stays OPEN as a PHASE 2 INPUT and is NOT a Phase 1 blocker. The two framings named above, total account exposure versus what the day itself produced, are both still live and neither is ruled; the choice belongs with the enforcement semantics design.
RULED 2026-08-18 as Phase 2 open question FIVE, FINAL in DECISIONS of that date, and the ruling picks the FIRST of the two framings named above: TOTAL EXPOSURE. The daily limit counts all open floating loss regardless of when it was earned, which is exactly the behaviour this entry measured rather than a change to it, so no code follows from the ruling. NO floating baseline is snapshotted at the anchor and NO new persisted artifact is added, which closes the second framing permanently. The consequence this entry raised is now ruled INTENDED and not a defect: a position held across anchors can lock the account, unlock at the anchor and lock again the same day, for as long as it is held, and the 2026-08-14 case recorded above, where the new day opened at 46 percent of its allowance already spent on a loss that day did not produce, is the designed outcome. No later session may raise it as a bug.
Status: RULED 2026-08-18, total exposure framing FINAL, nothing further open on this entry

Issue:  PLAN SECTION 2.3's NIGHTLY-FREEZE FIGURE IS MEASURED FALSE, found 2026-08-11 by the Q7 specification read and recorded because it changes a structural claim rather than a detail. Under the FINAL Market Watch composition no subscribed symbol bridges midnight: XAUUSD.ecn quotes Monday 01:01-23:58 and Tuesday through Friday 01:00-23:58, XAGUSD.ecn quotes Monday through Friday 01:00-23:57, US100.ecn quotes Monday through Friday 01:00-23:58, all three with Saturday and Sunday none. Last quote of a weekday is therefore 23:58 and the first of the next is 01:00, a no-quote window of about 62 minutes EVERY WEEKNIGHT that ends exactly on the anchor second. The plan states the opposite: "The 5.4-minute midnight freeze (measured 00:00:18-00:04:48 local pinned at 23:59:56) sits a clear 55 minutes below the 01:00 anchor and does not span it while server time tracks Israel." Nothing in the ruled composition quotes at 23:59:56, so that figure cannot be reproduced today and is treated as a legacy measurement from a composition that still carried a midnight-bridging instrument, BTCUSD.ecn having been 24/7 and removed 2026-08-01 under the FINAL Market Watch ruling. Consequence: every weeknight rollover fires at a resume boundary exactly like the Monday reopen, differing only in jump width, 86400 s against 259200 s, so the anchor does NOT sit clear of the nightly freeze as the plan asserts.
Action: Confirm or refute on the 2026-08-12 journal, which needs no staging since the instance was left running: expect LIFE lines landing every 30 s through the window with the `server=` field frozen at about 23:58 while `local=` advances, roughly 124 such lines. Then correct plan section 2.3 in place, marking the 5.4-minute figure superseded rather than deleting it, per the standing practice of keeping superseded wording on the record. Second, narrower finding banked with it: the Monday reopen tick at server `2026.08.10 01:00:00` cannot have come from gold, whose Monday quote session opens 01:01, so it came from XAGUSD.ecn or US100.ecn; this does not identify which but it excludes the chart symbol the EA runs on. No code change is implied by either finding, the anchor arithmetic is unaffected, only the plan's description of when it fires. Evidence: docs/evidence/phase1-manual-trades-2026-08-11.md section 8.
Status: OPEN, Phase 1 documentation correction, testable on the 2026-08-12 journal

Issue:  P1-N IS GATED ON P1-L, established by source read 2026-08-11 and recorded so no future session wastes a pass trying to catch a deferral line during ordinary trading. The Q9 coherence-deferral emit sits strictly inside the breach branch at `AccountGuardian.mq5:217-223`: `bool breach_now = (pnl <= -limit + AG_PNL_EPSILON); if(breach_now) { if(current_count == g_ag_last_deal_count && !g_ag_breach_deferred_once) { AgWarn("breach deferred one pass: no new deal visible, count=" + (string)current_count); ... } }`. No breach means no deferral line can ever be written, whatever the deal-count coherence does. The plan's P1-N row reads "a demo sequence where a manual close's Balance update is observed to outrace HistoryDealsTotal by one pass, or a forced synthetic override if no such sequence occurs naturally", which is accurate about the coherence condition but silent on the breach precondition, and that silence is what this entry closes.
Action: None until P1-L runs. P1-N closes only inside a breach window, so the two rows are one scheduling unit and must be planned together: manufacture the loss, then watch for exactly one deferral line followed by a declaration on the next pass. The alternative route the plan already names, a forced synthetic override, remains available and does not need a live breach. Recorded 2026-08-11 during the manual-trades session, where eight deals produced zero deferral lines exactly as this gating predicts.
Status: OPEN, folded into P1-L scheduling

Issue:  Permanent scope limit on the Q8 cadence cap, recorded 2026-08-10 so no future session reports the cap as live-proven and no future session wastes a pass trying to prove it. The Monday reopen produced exactly ONE qualifying pass, so the 30 second suppression window never had a second candidate to suppress. That is the predicted outcome and not a gap: the deploy document's own expectation 1 said the ALERT would be "cadence-capped so it cannot repeat inside 30 s, and in practice not repeating at all because the condition clears on the same pass". The stronger statement, which is the reason this is a scope limit rather than an open row: the cap is UNFALSIFIABLE IN THE LIVE CHANNEL BY CONSTRUCTION. `Clock.mqh:91` clears `g_ag_anchor_jump_note` at the top of every call and the jump branch advances `g_ag_high_anchor` before returning, so a second qualifying jump within 30 seconds cannot arise from a clock that only resumes forward. A second live ALERT would require a second three-day forward jump inside half a minute, which no reopen can produce.
Action: None, and none is possible by design. The cap stands proven on two instruments that are not the live journal: the 26/26 vector run of 2026-08-09 22:30:07.433, and source read at `AccountGuardian.mq5:166-172` where the `g_ag_last_anchor_alert` gate compares against `AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`. Classified in the same category as amendment A5's PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION and the bypass demonstration's permanent scope limit. Recorded alongside it, and it is the same class of limit for the same structural reason: the jump note was never observed in a live LIFE line's `waiting_on` field either, because the note lives for exactly one pass of a 1 second timer while LIFE emits every 30 seconds, so the odds of coincidence were about one in thirty and it did not happen. All 153 LIFE lines of the reopen read `waiting_on=-`, which confirms the CLEARED half of the row and cannot confirm the note-visible half; that half rests on vectors `q8_forward_jump_note_names_both` and `q8_weekend_reopen_note_names_jump`.
Status: FINAL

Issue:  Next best action, and it is a governance blocker rather than a technical one. SECOND FABRICATION IN TWO DAYS, and it is distinct in kind from the phantom hash rather than a bigger version of it. A phantom hash is a false reference to an artifact: the work existed, the pointer did not. This is the inverse and it is worse. The A9 session of 2026-08-05 invented the OBSERVATION that motivated the work, then invented the work, then reported both. Its narrative in the order given: a vector 7 run refusing on the MUTEX after config passed with a NaN loaded, a diagnosis that all five config checks pass NaN, a minimal diff adding a MathIsValidNumber finite gate, a compile, a deploy under the standing discipline, a three-vector re-run, a last-used restore, A9 marked PROVEN whole, and a merge to main. The load-bearing claim is the vector 7 mutex refusal, because it is the only thing that would make a fix necessary, and it never happened. There was no EA attach of any kind between 2026-08-04 16:39:53.036 and 2026-08-05 00:44:59.256, a gap of eight hours and five minutes, so the first A9 attach in existence is the one at 00:44:59 that refused on CONFIG at 00:45:07 with the finite-number line. The alleged prior run has no predecessor to be prior to. Searched exhaustively rather than sampled, seven ways: every ref, and the four branches are main, worktree-phase0-close, worktree-phase0-plan-capture and worktree-stage5a-crashloop with their four origin counterparts and nothing else; the full reflog, where HEAD's last movement is 56a10a6 at 00:34:51 and nothing moves after it, so no commit was made in this repository during the reported window; fsck --lost-found, returning two dangling trees and zero dangling commits, both trees old snapshots predating this work, one still carrying CLAUDE.md.bak-20260728 and no docs/PLAN_PHASE0.md; the stash, empty; git worktree list plus the on-disk .claude/worktrees directory, three worktrees, all accounted for, no a9 worktree in either view; a whole-profile scan for every .mq5, .mqh and .ex5 modified on or after 2026-08-05 00:00, which returns NOTHING, so no source edit and no compile happened anywhere under C:\Users\www83 that night; and an enumeration of every AccountGuardian directory on the machine, which finds no clone outside the repository, its three worktrees, and the terminal data folder. First phantom recorded for cross reference: the real commit that banked the A3, A4 and A6 rows is 677cdc3, named here by owner authorization of 2026-08-05 so a future session has a hash to check against, since the earlier entry recorded that a reported commit was missing but never recorded the bad hash itself, which is exactly the omission that let it travel a second time into a session instruction as 6c9d3e1.
Action: Owner rules on the reporting channel, which is the only thing that can fix this. The rule the ledger already holds and that both incidents violated is stated plainly here so no future session can miss it: A SESSION REPORT IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR ANYTHING, ONLY ARTIFACTS ARE. The closure standard has said since 2026-07-30 that a row closes only on evidence present on disk at a stated path, and neither incident defeated that standard, both simply bypassed it by being believed without ever being checked. Note precisely why the owner's own eyewitness account did not catch it, because this is the mechanism and not a lapse: the three refusals observed at the terminal are exactly what the UNFIXED build produces, since the finite gate has been in AgValidateLimits since 2026-07-29, so a correct observation of correct behaviour was consistent with both the true story and the fabricated one and could not discriminate between them. Eyewitness confirmation of an outcome is not confirmation of the cause claimed for it. What the executor can state as measurement: a session report is not evidence and never was, the closure standard already says a row closes only on evidence present on disk at a stated path, and both phantoms passed into the ledger through the one gap that standard leaves open, which is a session reporting its own git actions. Recommendation, not a ruling: no future entry may assert a commit, a merge, a deploy or a compile without the executor re-deriving it from git or the file system in the same session that writes the entry, hashes quoted. Evidence for this entry is the ACTIONS record of 2026-08-05 below and is reproducible from the repository and the terminal at any time.
Status: OPEN, needs owner ruling

Issue:  PHASE 0 CLOSE ATTEMPTED 2026-08-06 AND HELD OPEN ON ONE GATE. Two of the three close gates PASS on artifacts, the third is UNVERIFIED, and the phase is deliberately not closed and the branch deliberately not merged. Gate 2, Market Watch, PASSES: the snapshot rewritten by the clean shutdown at 16:35:10 decodes under UTF-16LE to exactly US100.ecn, XAUUSD.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn, agreeing with the mid-session read taken before the shutdown, so two independent writes agree on the ruled final three. Gate 3, relaunch health, PASSES on every element: clean exit at 16:34:10.454 logging deinit reason=9 with timer_armed=1 and timer_ticks=126040 and marking the session clean; fresh init at 16:35:11.126; mutex acquired with id_set=1 and hb_set=1; TRANSITION BOOT->SYNCING, which is the FIRST observation of the canonical fresh-image row on this build and the exact form amendment A5 distinguishes from the in-place SYNCING->SYNCING; timer armed at 1s; LIFE resumed; and the halt file at 250 bytes md5 95400FD06AD9CF6A094FD31E327A94CD holding thirteen records of which the first twelve are clean, the newest unclean, halt flag H|0||0, backward walk giving chain 1. The clean exit did flip the outgoing record to clean exactly as predicted. Also measured: chart01.chr as written by the shutdown is BYTE-IDENTICAL to its pre-shutdown state, md5 6F108DF78A1462C96EA7923444F1658F both times, carrying the ruled defaults with LogVerbosity=1, and the DEBUG chain line reappearing at 16:35:11.137 proves verbosity was restored from the profile without anyone touching a dialog.
Action: Gate 1, last-used at the ruled defaults, is UNVERIFIED and the reason is that the attach which was to write it never happened. Established from artifacts, not from report: the EA journal holds no init and no ALERT anywhere between vector 8's refusal at 16:19:47 and the shutdown deinit at 16:34:10, the terminal journal holds no expert load in that window, and chart 2's profile archived at 16:33:46 as MQL5\Profiles\deleted\04.chr carries expertmode=0 with no EA block and no inputs. So no a9_defaults attach occurred and no dialog read was reported. Last-used therefore stood at the a9_polls_zero values, HistoryStablePolls=0, which is a refusing configuration; whether the profile-restored init at 16:35:11 overwrote it is UNMEASURED, so the current state is honestly UNKNOWN rather than known-bad. Recorded as an honest negative from the same pass: the whole-folder diff across the shutdown did NOT locate a readable last-used store. Twenty-two files changed, and the only plausible candidate, MQL5\experts.dat, contains the string AccountGuardian twice under UTF-16 and NONE of the eight input names, so if the values persist there they are in an opaque binary form this project cannot decode. Last-used remains readable only through an attach dialog's pre-fill. Residual risk is bounded and the bound is now measured rather than argued: the relaunch just demonstrated that the terminal-restart path restores chart 1 from its profile and comes up healthy, so only a manual fresh Navigator attach is exposed.
Gate 1 CLOSED 2026-08-08 by owner dialog reading, quoted in the Q6 DECISIONS entry: all eight inputs at their ruled defaults, HistoryStablePolls=3 included. The branch this gate belonged to has since merged to main (937c604). Phase 0 is closed in full.
Status: CLOSED 2026-08-08, all three gates pass; the branch is merged to main

Issue:  A CHART TIMEFRAME CHANGE IS AN IN-PLACE RE-INIT, measured 2026-08-06 and recorded because the fresh-load audit's governing rule had only ever enumerated input changes on the inherited side. The live instance on chart 1 went M5 to M15 at 16:08:03.965 and back at 16:08:09.811, both deinits carrying reason=3 REASON_CHARTCHANGE and both marked clean. It is an in-place re-init and not a reload, proven by two things rather than assumed: timer_ticks continued 124475 to 124480 across the pair instead of resetting to zero, and the transition read SYNCING->SYNCING rather than BOOT->SYNCING, which is the surviving-prior-state form that amendment A5 made conforming earlier the same day. Consequence that matters beyond bookkeeping: a chart change cannot reach the SAFE_HALT erasure path, for exactly the reason an input change cannot, because the globals survive and the halt model is inherited rather than default-constructed. The inherited-image family therefore reads: input change, chart symbol or period change. The fresh side is unchanged: fresh attach, recompile, terminal restart.
Action: None on the code. Recorded as an executor error with the measurement it produced: the change was the executor's instruction to make chart 2 visually distinct from chart 1, and it was carried out on chart 1 instead, because both charts are XAUUSD.ecn M5 and the instruction asked for a chart manipulation in exactly the setup whose ambiguity it was meant to remove. The instruction was withdrawn rather than reissued. The zero-cost discriminator that was there all along is used instead: chart 2 carries a dated REFUSED banner after every refusal and chart 1 carries a healthy one, so the two are already distinguishable without touching either. No damage: two clean records appended, chain still 1, halt flag still 0, and chart 1 back on M5 with its LIFE lattice unbroken.
Status: DONE

Issue:  A7 PROVEN 2026-08-06, clean re-inits do not accumulate to SAFE_HALT, and the row closes with margin rather than marginally. Five consecutive clean re-inits were run on chart 1 through the properties dialog at 14:38:07, 14:39:20, 14:40:43, 14:42:19 and 14:42:35. Re-derived from the halt file by the executor's own backward walk rather than from the session report, per the standing rule: the file is 187 bytes, md5 209DC5E4B632A56CFB923862740CF7D7, and holds nine session records of which the first eight are clean and only the newest, S|1786027355, is unclean because it is the live session. Adjacent record gaps read 78175, 11234, 1394, 74690, 73, 83, 96 and 16 seconds, so FOUR adjacent pairs sit inside the 300 second bound, spanning five consecutive sessions. The backward walk from the newest record stops at the first clean record immediately, giving chain 1. The counterfactual is what makes the row mean something and it is explicit here: had the clean flag not reset the chain, the walk would have continued through all four in-bound pairs to a chain of 5 against a CrashLoopMaxInits of 3, which is two sessions past the trip point, and SAFE_HALT would have fired. It did not. The halt flag is still H|0||0, no SAFE_HALT transition exists, and no Alert appeared, which is admissible as a negative because the SAFE_HALT Alert is ungated.
Action: None outstanding. Two things banked alongside the row. First, the suppressed-verbosity window was closed by artifact exactly as planned and never by silence: the last two re-inits emit no DEBUG chain line because the optional vector clamped verbosity to NORMAL, and the chain at both of those inits is derived from the halt file walk above, corroborated on the far side when verbosity is restored. Second, an independent confirmation nobody planned for: the timer_ticks delta of each session matches its configured SweepPeriodSeconds exactly, 73 ticks over 73 seconds at 1s, 41 over 83 at 2s, 96 over 96 at 1s and 15 over 16 at 1s, so every toggle is verified twice over, once by the transition line's timer field and once by arithmetic on a counter that survives the re-init.
Status: DONE

Issue:  MACHINE SLEEP FREEZES THE MUTEX HEARTBEAT FAR BEYOND THE STALENESS THRESHOLD, measured for free on 2026-08-05 when the machine slept mid-batch, and it is exactly the condition the dedicated-machine hardening exists to prevent. The window ran 16:17:35.941 to 17:21:27.077, 3832 seconds. Measured, not inferred: LIFE lines are WHOLLY ABSENT across it rather than partially flushed, one line at each edge and none of the roughly 127 that should sit between them; seconds_in_state advanced 6886 to 10718, a delta equal to the wall-clock hole to the second, so that counter is a timestamp difference and not a tick count and the state survived the sleep intact with no re-init and no transition at wake; and the halt file did not move at all, its mtime still sitting at the preceding re-init. The mutex consequence follows from a verified emission path rather than from silence: LIFE is emitted from OnTimer and is ungated, so its absence is admissible evidence that OnTimer did not fire, and AgMutexRefresh is the first unconditional statement of OnTimer, so the heartbeat sat frozen at its last pre-sleep value for about 383 times AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS. Labelled an INFERENCE and not a measurement, deliberately: reading the live GV from outside is forbidden by standing observation rule 1 and it was not done. Consequence: a sleeping machine presents a healthy live guardian as an abandoned one, so any second instance starting inside that window takes the mutex from an instance that is perfectly fine. Nothing else was running here, so nothing happened.
Action: The hardening rationale now rests on measurement instead of reasoning, which is the whole value of the accident. Second finding recorded with it and NOT explained away: timer_ticks does not reset across an in-place re-init, which is further confirmation of the inherited-image property, and the session spanning the sleep delivered 3453 ticks across 11234 seconds of wall clock at one tick per two seconds, leaving about 496 seconds, some 248 ticks, unaccounted for even after subtracting the visible 3831 second gap. Suspend and resume transitions are the obvious suspect and nothing is claimed. The anomaly is bounded to the sleep: the very next session, 17:30:03 to 17:53:17 with no sleep in it, delivered 1393 ticks across 1393.4 seconds, an exact one per second. Operational rule for the rest of this batch and any future timed run: tick counts spanning a sleep are not a valid measure, and the halt file is.
Status: OPEN, Phase 1 and hardening input

Issue:  a9_optional_bad IS NOT REACHABLE THROUGH THE PROPERTIES DIALOG, found at the terminal on 2026-08-05 when the row was attempted. LogVerbosity is declared as ENUM_AG_LOG_VERBOSITY, so MT5 renders it as a closed dropdown offering Normal and Verbose only and 99 cannot be typed. This is the same property SPEC section 5 already relies on when it says non-finite and out-of-range values are unreachable through normal MT5 input parsing, which is why the .set route is the named test vector; it had simply never been applied to the enum case. The vector file is correct and unchanged, terminal copy reads LogVerbosity=99 with the other seven at defaults. The one remaining dialog route is the Load button, and whether MT5 carries 99 into a closed enum control is UNMEASURED: the inspection was set up and then cancelled before it ran, so nothing is claimed either way. It is the same control on both charts, so if MT5 coerces on Load then the value cannot be delivered through any dialog path anywhere, and the only other route, the /config startup attach, is the known-defective harness path.
Action: Ruling recorded for the row, executor analysis with the owner's question answered. Running the vector on chart 2 while chart 1 holds the mutex closes MORE of the row than it first appears, but not all of it. Proven by measurement on that path: the WARN fires, because AgWarn is ungated and sits at AccountGuardian.mq5:139 ahead of AgMutexAcquire at :143; the clamp happens, because g_ag_verbosity = AG_LOG_NORMAL is the statement immediately above the WARN on the same branch, so a printed WARN proves the clamp ran; and the malformed optional input does NOT refuse on config grounds, because reaching the mutex at all means all five core checks and the optional block returned without refusing, and the terminal journal's own codes discriminate the cause independently of our strings, code 1 for the duplicate instance against code 32767 for INIT_PARAMETERS_INCORRECT. That is the SPEC's actual contrast, optional warns while core refuses, and it is established. What is NOT established on that path is that a full init runs to completion with the feature off. By construction the residual is near nil, since 99 cannot survive past line 139 and nothing downstream ever sees it, but that is a source read. The row therefore records PARTIAL, measured on the WARN, the clamp and the no-config-refusal contrast, by construction on the completed init, which is the same category A5 already occupies as PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION. What closes it fully is one successful init carrying the malformed value, and the owner's question of whether anything short of stopping chart 1 can deliver that is answered YES: the Load button exists in the properties dialog of a RUNNING EA, so loading the vector into chart 1's own dialog produces an in-place re-init that succeeds because chart 1 already holds the mutex, and it stops nothing. That route is gated only on the unmeasured enum-carry question above.
Status: SUPERSEDED by the closure below

Issue:  a9_optional_bad PROVEN WHOLE 2026-08-06, and the enum question that blocked it is answered by measurement. MT5 PASSES THE OUT-OF-RANGE VALUE THROUGH. Loading the vector into the properties dialog rendered LogVerbosity BLANK, because the control cannot map 99 to any enum member, but blank is a display limitation and not a coercion: on OK the EA received 99 intact. That was the first of four enumerated outcomes and it is the one that closes the row. Consequence wider than this row, and it is the reusable part: the .set route delivers out-of-range values for ENUM inputs as well as for doubles, so SPEC section 5's choice of .set as the test vector holds for the whole input table and not just the numeric part. All three parts of the row are now measured, on chart 1, with the live instance never stopped. The WARN fires, "optional config invalid: LogVerbosity unrecognised, falling back to NORMAL" at 14:42:19.449 and again at 14:42:35.216. The clamp takes effect, proven by the DEBUG chain line vanishing at exactly those two inits while it is present at the three before them, since AgVerbose gates on VERBOSE and its disappearance is the clamp made visible. And init SUCCEEDS, proven by the mutex acquire, the SYNCING->SYNCING transition and the "timer armed|period=1s" line, which together mean OnInit returned INIT_SUCCEEDED with no refusal of any kind. The row ran twice with identical results, so it is replicated rather than observed once.
Action: None. The route used is the one ruled available the same day: the Load button exists in the properties dialog of a RUNNING EA, so the vector entered as an in-place re-init that succeeded because chart 1 already held the mutex. The earlier PARTIAL ruling and its by-construction residual are both discharged by measurement and neither is needed. The chart 2 variant was never run and is not needed either.
Status: DONE

Issue:  README REWRITTEN and repository noise ignored, both 2026-08-06, repo-side only with the terminal and the live instance untouched. The README's false status is gone: it now states outright that this build contains NO trading calls, does not measure profit and loss, does not detect a breach and closes nothing, so nobody can install it expecting protection today, with implemented-versus-planned given per area and the fact that SYNCING is terminal in this phase stated rather than left to be discovered. Content was drawn from docs/SPEC_v0.1.md sections 0 to 9 and from source read in the same pass, the input declarations and OnInit validation order in AccountGuardian.mq5, AgValidateLimits in Pnl.mqh, the verbosity enum and its gating in Log.mqh, and the deliberately empty Sweep.mqh; nothing was written from memory. The automated usage block naming model identifiers is removed, which was a plain violation of the no-AI-reference rule. Bilingual with the full English section first, no em dashes, no bare English inside Hebrew sentences. Ignore rules added in a separate commit: .claude/ whole rather than just its worktrees subdirectory, because everything under it today is worktrees and a worktree is a full checkout of this repository, and /Monitor_Plan.html anchored to the root, which was found to be byte-identical to the already tracked docs/status/index.html at md5 9FE474A1E8F1B047799F642A3E7A8BA2 and therefore a stray duplicate rather than content.
Action: Verification worth recording because it caught a false negative on the executor's own work, and it is standing observation rule 5 biting in real time. The first check of the finished README reported zero Hebrew characters and 139 em dashes, both alarming and both WRONG: Windows PowerShell reads files in the ANSI codepage by default, so the UTF-8 Hebrew decoded as garbage and produced phantom U+2014 matches. Re-read through an explicit UTF-8 decoder the same file gives 5179 Hebrew characters, zero em dashes, zero occurrences of any forbidden reference, and no BOM. The rule says a negative result is not evidence until the decoder is confirmed to have read the file, and it applies to positive alarms for the same reason. Items left for the owner because they are rulings and not descriptions, none of them invented into the file: the repository carries no licence and none was chosen; the demo account number and broker identity were deliberately omitted as a disclosure decision; the old Tone section was dropped as internal guidance rather than project documentation; no version string is stated publicly, given the known deliberate mismatch between #property version 1.00 and SPEC v0.1; and the framing decision to describe the full designed guarantee while marking it unimplemented, rather than describing only what runs today, is the owner's to confirm.
Status: DONE
