Issue:  Standing measurement obligation, opened 2026-08-08 by the Phase 1 investigation: the broker server DST peg is documented but NOT measured. JustMarkets publishes server time as GMT+2 in winter and GMT+3 in summer, which means the server observes a DST calendar, and the calendar it follows determines the windows where server time and Israel civil time disagree by an hour. The plan enumerates the divergence windows for each candidate peg (US, EU, fixed GMT+3) rather than picking one, because picking one would be an assumption dressed as a fact. Own evidence is consistent with a US peg and proves nothing: the measured Monday reopen at server 01:00:20 equals Sunday 18:00 New York under GMT+3 with US DST in force, which is the metals-session convention, but a single reopen stamp cannot distinguish the pegs. Both server and Israel DST transitions land inside the weekend freeze on this Market Watch, so TimeCurrent never visibly steps from a server DST change; the Monday reopen simply stamps at the new offset, which is exactly why this needs a deliberate harvest rather than a passive observation.
Action: Harvest the dual-clock LIFE lines across the window 2026-10-25 to 2026-11-01, the first calendar stretch where Israel has left DST and the candidate server pegs disagree. Both clocks are already on every LIFE line since the 72ed960 fix, so the offset is readable per line and the transition instant is bounded by the lattice spacing. Record the measured offset before and after and name which peg it settles. Until measured, no session cites a server DST calendar as fact.
Corrected 2026-08-10 by the reopen measurement, figure only, conclusion unchanged. The "measured Monday reopen at server 01:00:20" this entry cites is a SAMPLING ARTIFACT and must not be re-cited as the resume instant: it was read off a LIFE line, and LIFE lines land on a 30 second grid. Measured today at the actual event lines, the resumed server clock came back at exactly `2026.08.10 01:00:00`, 21 seconds earlier, with the first Monday LIFE line stamping server 01:00:21 which is where the old figure came from. The peg reasoning is UNAFFECTED, because 01:00:00 server under GMT+3 is still Sunday 18:00 New York, so the entry's "consistent with a US peg and proves nothing" verdict stands exactly as written. Two things the reopen adds for the October harvest. The dual-clock fields are confirmed present and usable on every LIFE line, `server=` and `local=` both, which is the instrument this harvest depends on. And the local clock currently trails the server clock by about one second, measured across 33 post-resume lines, so the harvest must read the offset from the two fields on the same line and never infer it from the terminal's own line timestamp.
Status: OPEN, Phase 1 input, window opens 2026-10-25

Issue:  Ratchet mechanism awaits owner ratification, the narrow remainder of the limit-inflation entry reconciled 2026-08-04. The decision to build the ratchet is RULED and now sits in DECISIONS; what is still unruled is the corrected mechanism only, carrying an explicit UNRULED 2026-07-30 marker in docs/DESIGN_PHASE1_2.md and citable by no session as authority until ruled. The correction, in one line: ratchet the derived limit_currency as a single running-minimum scalar floor_currency rather than the two raw inputs independently, because under Q8's min-of-enabled a leg that is not binding can be disabled with zero effect on the enforced limit, and the first-pass input-level ratchet would have blocked that as a false positive (owner's worked case: base 2000, percent 5 gives 100, currency 50 binds, disabling the percent leg moves nothing). Downstream consequences all re-derived from the correction in the document: floor_<login>.dat drops to three fields, floor_day_anchor, floor_currency and checksum; the breach snapshot needs no interaction because the ratchet governs pre-breach ACTIVE while Q6's snapshot governs post-breach LOCKED and the phases are disjoint; the boot-time limit_for_comparison cascade gains a tier 2, min(live_limit_currency, floor_currency) when a valid same-day floor exists, between Q6's snapshot and the live-input fallback; and the residual narrows to file plus GV destruction of both the state and the floor artifacts together with input inflation.
Action: Owner ratifies or corrects the mechanism. Implementation is Phase 2 and gated on Phase 0 green regardless, so this is not on the critical path today. Design only, nothing implemented.

Issue:  RESOLVED, and it supersedes the hard stop below. The SAFE_HALT bypass is DEMONSTRATED against 694d570. At 22:26:37 a fresh Navigator attach inherited the both-zero inputs MT5 retains as last-used, refused on the config check, and its OnDeinit wrote a default-constructed model over the halt file: six session records reduced to zero, then reseeded by the next healthy attach at 22:26:54. Nobody staged it, and no evidence was lost, because the pre-state was already backed up, the journal recorded it, and the post-state proves the wipe. The discriminator is measured rather than argued: the erasing refusal logged timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0, an image whose globals had never been touched, while the harmless 22:35:29 input-edit refusal logged timer_armed=1 and timer_ticks=514 in an inherited running image, same inputs and same alert text, opposite consequence. Evidence: docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md addendum, backups halt_1200252169.dat.after-config-refusal-2026-07-30 (pre, md5 3209ABA4) and halt_1200252169.dat.post-erasure-reseeded-2026-07-30 (post, md5 3A98CFF4). Erasure half only, per the standing scope limit.
Action: The re-sequencing ruling the entry below asks for is no longer needed for the config path. 72ed960 deployed and compiled at 22:39:30, 0 errors 0 warnings, but the running instance still carries the 694d570 image because MT5 does not hot-reload an externally recompiled .ex5, so the fix proof needs a removal and a fresh attach. The SPEC threat-model wording was ruled by the owner on 2026-07-30 and applied to section 7 and to the section 9 threat-model addition: erasure requires a refusal inside a fresh program image, a refusal in an inherited image is harmless, the timer_armed and timer_ticks discriminator is stated in the wording so the boundary stays testable, the fresh-image routes are enumerated, and the original trigger sentence is kept because it was always the fresh-load case.
Status: DONE

Issue:  Fresh-load audit, ordered by the owner and delivered in docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md. Governing rule, now measured: an input-change re-init reuses the loaded image and its globals survive, while a fresh attach, a recompile, and a terminal restart all reset globals, and MT5 seeds a fresh attach from last-used inputs that persist across restarts via the chart profile. Exposed rows: A1 (a malformed second chart refuses at the config check before ever reaching the mutex, testing nothing), A2, A3, A4, A6 and A7 (all restore inputs from the chart profile, and since AgHaltAppendSession sits downstream of every refusal return, a malformed profile makes crash counting unable to accumulate at all), A8 (only through its starting attach), and A9 in the opposite direction: a malformed row driven by editing a RUNNING instance inherits a populated model, so the fix's skip branch never executes and the row proves the refusal without proving the fix.
Action: Adopted for the rest of the matrix: every attach instruction names the exact input values to confirm before OK, the executor takes the halt baseline BEFORE the attach rather than after, and each A9 row runs as a fresh load beginning from a removal.
Status: FINAL

Issue:  SUPERSEDED by the entry above, kept for the record. HARD STOP, contact 1 halted after step 2. The bypass demonstration did not reproduce, and the reason narrows the bypass itself. On 694d570 the owner set both daily limits to zero in the properties dialog at 22:08:42; the refusal fired verbatim as predicted, but the halt file was NOT erased: all six session records survived and record 6 flipped clean=0 to clean=1, the legitimate outcome of the outgoing session's clean deinit. Measured cause: timer_ticks=40714 is identical in the reason=5 deinit and the reason=8 deinit, so the program image was never unloaded between them. An input change runs OnDeinit then OnInit inside the same loaded program, globals intact, so the refused init inherited the model loaded at 10:50:08 and the unconditional AgHaltSave wrote a POPULATED model rather than a default-constructed one. Erasure requires genuinely fresh globals, which means a fresh program load that then refuses. Evidence: docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md, backup halt_1200252169.dat.after-config-refusal-2026-07-30 md5 3209ABA485948D39872B0181C06BE2D6. The SPEC threat-model trigger sentence (attach a second instance, let the mutex refuse it) is the fresh-load case and stands; what is measured false is the ledger's framing of a properties-dialog edit as a demonstration vector.
Action: Owner ruling needed on two things before the batch resumes. First, the re-sequencing: demonstrate the erasure on 694d570 via a genuine fresh-load refusal, either a second-chart mutex refusal (the SPEC's own trigger, and A1's setup) or an EA removal followed by a fresh Navigator attach carrying a malformed core input. Second, the wording precision in the SPEC threat model and in the bypass ISSUES entry, which is a SPEC change and therefore owner-ruled. The live halt file was deliberately NOT restored from backup: nothing was destroyed, and record 6's new clean flag is information a restore would overwrite.
Status: BLOCKED on owner

Issue:  Measured design input for Phases 1 and 2, recorded now and NOT acted on, per owner instruction. TimeCurrent freezes for the whole weekend, so everything derived from it freezes with it: the day anchor floors TimeCurrent, and expiry compares TimeCurrent to locked_until. A lock taken Friday therefore expires when quotes resume, not at its computed anchor. Q7 accepted that direction as erring locked, but accepted it as a rare dead-market edge case; this measurement shows it is a routine weekly condition lasting about two days, and the weekly measurement window inherits the same property.
Action: Specify day anchor and expiry semantics in Phase 1 and 2 against a clock that is normally frozen for 48 hours every week. The owner's threat-model reading is CONFIRMED for expiry and CORRECTED for the PnL window. Confirmed: Market Watch contents are owner-mutable and influence how long TimeCurrent stays frozen, removing symbols can only delay an unlock which errs locked, and no arrangement can push TimeCurrent ahead of real server time because it derives from server-supplied quote timestamps rather than the local clock, which is exactly why Q7 chose it over TimeTradeServer. Correction, flagged as analysis to verify in Phase 1 rather than as measurement: the PnL window is NOT safe in both directions. SPEC 4.2 selects history over (anchor, now); if now is a frozen TimeCurrent and a deal executes after the freeze point, that deal falls outside the upper bound and is not counted, which is fail-open under-counting rather than erring locked. A stale anchor lengthens the window and errs strict, but a stale upper bound truncates it and errs permissive. Phase 1 obligation: the history select upper bound must not be a frozen clock. Scope note per owner ruling 2026-08-01, applied 2026-08-02: classification downgraded from blocker class to a Phase 1 correctness obligation, not closed. The concrete crypto example that illustrated it was struck on 2026-08-02, pre-approved by the owner, as no longer operative: under the Market Watch ruling the selection is XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn only, all 24/5, so no instrument quotes while TimeCurrent is frozen at the weekend and no deal can execute inside the frozen window here. The obligation survives because the general defect needs no 24/7 instrument: an upper bound derived from a possibly stale clock truncates the window and under-counts realized loss on any quiet stretch, and the measured 5.4 minute midnight freeze is the same mechanism at small scale. Added by measurement 2026-08-01: TimeCurrent is not monotonic either. After BTCUSD.ecn left Market Watch, the 23:26:38 reconnect rewound it from 2026.08.01 23:02:41 to 2026.07.31 23:57:59, about 25 hours backward, so Phase 1 and 2 semantics must tolerate a backward-stepping clock; backward errs locked for expiry but truncates the PnL upper bound further, the same fail-open direction.
Status: OPEN, Phase 1 and 2 input

Issue:  SAFE_HALT BYPASS, found by source read on 2026-07-29 before any acceptance row ran. OnDeinit runs after a refused OnInit (reason 8) and saved the halt model unconditionally at AccountGuardian.mq5:234. On that path AgHaltLoad never executed, so the model was default-constructed empty, and writing it erased all session records and cleared the persisted halt flag on disk. GV AG_HALT_<login> still read 1, so the next init saw GV-set against file-clear and logged RESUMED_FROM_SAFE_HALT: a malfunctioning guardian back in service with no human act, violating the manual-restart-only ruling. Trigger needs no privilege: attach a second instance and let the mutex refuse it. Every config-refusal return at AccountGuardian.mq5:92-113 reaches the same path, so the A9 input matrix would have destroyed evidence too. Blocks A1, A2, A3, A4, A6, A9. Classified as a bypass, not data loss.
Action: Fixes written, not yet compiled or deployed, pending step 1 of the owner's sequence which must demonstrate the bug against the unfixed build first. Fix 1: g_ag_halt_loaded in Persist.mqh, set at every exit of AgHaltLoad, checked in OnDeinit, with an INFO line on the skip so it is never silent. Gating on g_owns_mutex was rejected as conflating mutex ownership with model validity, and it would have masked the config-refusal case. Fix 2 (A8, same build): TimeCurrent and TimeLocal on the LIFE line. Recorded in the SPEC threat model with the general obligation and the best-effort caveat on manual-resume detection. Halt file backed up first, two copies, md5 0f40c49bc67a3767b22b9491910e1ade. Closed 2026-08-04: both fixes shipped and were proven on 72ed960 on 2026-07-30 and re-proven on the Stage 5a build at 15:56:00, and the blocked-rows list this entry names is fully discharged, A1, A2, A3, A4 and A6 all closed by the kill session of 2026-08-04 and A9 gated only on the Load-browser check.
Status: DONE

Both checks passed on a full scan with no sampling, 3115 LIFE lines across three log files: check 1, the server clock frozen at 2026.07.31 23:57:59 for 3067 consecutive lines with no forward advancement anywhere before the natural Sunday reopen; check 2, LIFE continuity against an expected lattice of exactly 3115 at the 30 second cadence, max gap 30.00 s, zero missing lines, unbroken across two midnight log rotations. The ACTIONS entry of 2026-08-03 recorded that closure and this ISSUES entry was never updated to match, which is how the row came to be reported as open by a session reading ISSUES alone. Second, and this removes the need to rely on that inspection: the row is INDEPENDENTLY RE-CONFIRMED on the deployed build by measurement taken today, 120 LIFE lines on 2026-08-05 from local 00:00:25 to 00:59:55 carrying a server clock frozen at 2026.08.04 23:57:59 throughout, an exact 30 second lattice with no missing line, which is four times the 30-line threshold this entry asked for. The two unfulfilled sub-items of the original action are recorded as not done and not needed: the XAUUSD.ecn symbol-specification read was labelled corroboration rather than a gate, and the timer_ticks-at-deinit second measure is superseded by a 3115-line lattice count, which is the stronger instrument.

One expectation in the earlier entry is CORRECTED by the same measurement: the file is not rewritten only at shutdown, its mtime of 2026-08-05 22:31:33 falls in the middle of an uninterrupted terminal session that began 2026-08-04, so MT5 refreshes it periodically during operation. The ledger's instruction to "re-read the snapshot after the next terminal shutdown rewrites it" was therefore based on a wrong model of when the file moves, and the cross-check could have been discharged at any point in the last two days. Recorded because the same wrong model was about to shape a shutdown plan today.

Issue:  CORRECTION to the entry below, dated 2026-07-29. The root cause recorded there ("the terminal-side algo-trading gate") is STRUCK as unproven. Status: OPEN

Issue:  CLOSED AS PHANTOM. No defect was ever demonstrated. The reported blocker rested on two independent observation failures that together produced a convincing picture of a dead timer: the old build's liveness line routed through AgVerbose and was suppressed at NORMAL verbosity, so silence was guaranteed regardless of execution; and the F3 dialog does not refresh while it is open, so a variable that was updating the whole time displayed as frozen. Sampled correctly (open, read, fully close, wait, reopen, read) the mutex heartbeat advances by the elapsed seconds. The behaviour of build 3627886 is UNKNOWN and unprovable in retrospect, and no cause is backfilled here.
Action: The change set was still worth landing on its own merits, independent of the phantom: the EventSetTimer return was unchecked at all three call sites, and SYNCING was a state the EA could occupy indefinitely while emitting nothing. Amendment A3 is the reason the question became answerable at all. Corroboration from MQL5\Logs\20260729.log: 15 LIFE lines, spacing a consistent 30 s to within 10 ms, seconds_in_state advancing 1, 31, 61, 91, and deinit reporting timer_armed=1 with timer_ticks=212 across a 213-second session, which is one tick per second sustained.
Status: DONE

Issue:  Standing observation rules, learned the hard way, binding on every future session.
Action: (1) External reads of bases\gvariables.dat are never evidence while the terminal runs. (2) The F3 dialog is a snapshot; a GV reading is admissible only as open, read, fully close, wait, reopen, read. (3) Absence of log output is never evidence of absence of execution unless the emitting line is known to be unsuppressed at the active verbosity; verify the emission path before concluding anything from silence. (4) Added by owner order 2026-07-30: journal lines emitted at OS shutdown can be lost unflushed even when the code that printed them ran to completion, so the halt file, not the journal, is the authoritative witness for shutdown behavior; measured on the 2026-07-29/30 overnight event, where OnDeinit provably ran (clean flag written, mutex zeroed) and its deinit lines do not exist in the log. (5) Added by owner order 2026-07-31: a decoder that fails silently returns an empty result indistinguishable from a genuine absence of matches; measured 2026-07-30 when iconv returned empty on a UTF-16 CRLF chart profile that PowerShell read correctly. Any negative result from a text search must confirm the decoder actually read the file before the absence counts as evidence. (6) Added by measurement 2026-08-04: the metaeditor64.exe process exit code is NOT a compile status signal and is inverted in practice on this installation. Measured with a control: the real build logged "Result: 0 errors, 0 warnings" and exited 1, while a deliberately broken copy of the same source logged "Result: 3 errors, 0 warnings" and exited 0, producing no ex5. Any gate keyed on the exit code would therefore pass every broken build and fail every clean one. The authoritative signals are the log's Result line and the presence and timestamp of the ex5, and both must be read. (7) Added by measurement 2026-08-04: the ex5 SIZE AND HASH ARE NOT BUILD IDENTITY. Five consecutive compiles of byte-identical source, verified unchanged by md5 across all five, produced five different binaries: 40992, 39982, 39450, 40470 and 40224 bytes, every one a different md5, a spread of about 1500 bytes. Two ex5 files therefore cannot be compared to decide whether they were built from the same source, in either direction: equal size would not prove sameness and unequal size does not prove difference. This strikes the corroborating use of the "ex5 39642 bytes" style figure recorded in earlier entries; those entries' conclusions stand on their other evidence, the figures are simply not identity. Build identity rests on three things instead: the md5 of every source file against the committed tree, the ex5 modification timestamp, and the runtime behaviour of the loaded image in the journal, which is the only measure that speaks to what is actually running rather than what is on disk.
Status: FINAL

Issue:  SUPERSEDED, kept for the record. Reported as a blocker on the owner's F3 reading: the timer never fired. AG_HB_1200252169 exists in the live store with the value written at acquire (Persist.mqh:251) and frozen since, and AgMutexRefresh has never executed. Mechanism, and it is inverted from what was logged before: a frozen mutex heartbeat always reads stale, and staleness authorizes takeover under F8, so every second instance takes the mutex unconditionally. Single-instance protection does not exist in the 3627886 build. Acceptance rows A1 and A2 are both broken, not merely untestable. Static findings that pin the cause to timer arming rather than to an early return: EventSetTimer had three call sites (AccountGuardian.mq5:139, 152, 161), each immediately before a return INIT_SUCCEEDED, and the journal's boot transition proves the line-161 path was the one taken with g_timer_seconds=1; between OnTimer entry and the refresh write there was exactly one guard, g_owns_mutex, set true at line 113 on that same path; inside AgMutexRefresh the only guard compares the instance GV to g_ag_instance_id and logs an ALERT when it fires, and no such line exists. So no early return can account for the freeze, and the return value of EventSetTimer was never checked.
Action: See the phantom-closure entry above. The changes shipped and are verified; the premise did not survive.
Status: DONE

Issue:  SUPERSEDED by the entry above, kept for the record. Mutex heartbeat GV AG_HB_1200252169 did not appear in an external read of bases\gvariables.dat while the EA was attached and initialized, although AG_ID_1200252169 did. The claim "this is an absence, not a read artifact" was overstated and is withdrawn: that file is a snapshot owned by the running process, and a constant length across samples is equally consistent with a file that is simply not being refreshed. Static source read settles two candidates and clears both: every write uses GlobalVariableSet, the persistent form, and GlobalVariableTemp appears nowhere in the build, so the temporary-GV explanation is out; GlobalVariablesFlush is called on all five write paths including the per-tick refresh at Persist.mqh:265, so the missing-flush explanation is out. The leftover-from-an-earlier-session explanation for AG_ID is also out: the stale prior-project globals are unsuffixed, AG_ID_1200252169 carries our account suffix, and the only session of ours that ever executed is the 14:09:40 manual attach, which wrote it at Persist.mqh:250, one line before the mutex heartbeat write.
Action: Waiting on the owner's F3 reading of the live store, which is authoritative. No fix proposed until then. If F3 shows the mutex heartbeat live and incrementing, the defect was the external read method, and this entry closes with the standing rule that external gvariables.dat reads are never evidence while the terminal runs.
Status: OPEN

Issue:  External reads of bases\gvariables.dat while the terminal is running are unreliable as evidence, in both directions: the file is a snapshot the process owns, absence there does not prove absence in the live store, and a stale length does not prove a stable variable set. One wrong conclusion has already been drawn from it.
Action: Never cite that file as evidence again. The live store is read through the terminal F3 dialog or from inside MQL5 via GlobalVariableCheck and GlobalVariableGet.

2026-08-27. VERSION 1 OF THE REALIZED PEAK TRAILING FLOOR IS IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE ON BRANCH `worktree-v1-realized-peak`, COMPILES CLEAN, AND IS ACCEPTANCE PENDING ON EVERY ONE OF ITS FOUR ROWS. Four commits off main at `de8d9c3`, in the order the units were built and each compiled before it was committed: `80cc46a` the fold extension in `Pnl.mqh`, `19e158e` the peak model and its file and `AgPeakUpdate` in `Persist.mqh`, `665ffb4` the boot load and the breach comparison in `AccountGuardian.mq5`, `428614c` the lock level journal line. THE DIFF IS 522 INSERTIONS AND 2 DELETIONS ACROSS THREE FILES, ALL UNDER `MQL5/`, and the two deletions are named here because a diff this large with two removed lines invites the question: they are the old `bool breach_now = (pnl <= -enforced_limit + AG_PNL_EPSILON);` and the old `double AgRealizedFold(...)` signature line, the latter re appearing unchanged twenty lines lower as the forwarder. NOTHING ELSE WAS REMOVED ANYWHERE.
WHAT THE MECHANISM IS, IN THE TERMS THE RULING SET USES. `AgRealizedRunFold` is the existing single sorted walk in `Pnl.mqh` with a running maximum added beside the running minimum it already carried, plus the ticket and time of the deal that last raised it. `AgRealizedFold` keeps its frozen signature and its two outputs exactly and is now a forwarder onto it, so the defect 3 shape 1 FINAL of 2026-08-20 is satisfied in letter and no second walk exists anywhere. The peak block in `Persist.mqh` mirrors the ratchet floor block above it function for function, `AgPeakPath`, `AgPeakSerialize`, `AgPeakSave`, `AgPeakResetModel`, `AgPeakQuarantine`, `AgPeakLoad`, `AgPeakEffectiveLevel` and `AgPeakUpdate`, with ONE deliberate difference and it is the whole of D3.1: a corrupt or missing floor reseeds from the live limit, a corrupt or missing peak RECONSTRUCTS FROM TODAY'S DEAL HISTORY, and nothing in the block ever seeds a peak from an input. In the breach tail `peak_level` is the peak minus the POST RATCHET `enforced_limit` per D7, `ratchet_level` is `-enforced_limit`, and the comparison is `pnl <= MathMax(ratchet_level, peak_level) + AG_PNL_EPSILON`, which is D2 with what `pnl` means left untouched, which is D1.2.
ONE DESIGN QUESTION THE SPEC DID NOT SETTLE WAS SETTLED BY THE CODE AND IS RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT FOR A REVIEWER TO DISCOVER. `AgEvaluateActive` calls `AgRealized` and not `AgRealizedFold` above the breach tail, and that region is required to stay byte identical, so the running maximum CANNOT be threaded down from it. `AgPeakUpdate` therefore calls `AgRealizedRunFold` itself, every pass. THAT IS A SECOND CALL AND NOT A SECOND WALK: the fold exists once, with the F12 whitelist and the Q3 formula written down once, which is the property both the shape 1 FINAL and the fold's own comment protect. The cost is one more pass over one day's deals per timer tick and it is stated here rather than discovered later. The gain is that reconstruction is the ORDINARY path rather than a restart only path, so D3.1 has no separate code to be wrong on its own, and a dip and recovery between two samples cannot hide from a running maximum that sees every deal in order.
THREE READINGS WERE TAKEN THAT THE SPEC LEFT OPEN, ALL IN THE STRICT DIRECTION, ALL RECORDED AS READINGS AND NOT AS RULINGS. ONE, the peak's anchor MOVES FORWARD ONLY. On the D3.1 backward step case the anchor is held at the later day rather than rewound, so a clock rewind followed by a correction cannot present itself as a rollover and reset the peak in one act; that is the ratchet's NO RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP clause applied to the peak for its own reason. TWO, on a backward step in steady state the peak is HELD and is NOT raised from that pass's reconstruction, because a rewound anchor widens the walk's window into the previous day and the running maximum it returns is a two day figure that no single day ever reached. Holding loosens nothing, so D2 is untouched: D2 governs the max BETWEEN the two mechanisms and neither term moves down there. THREE, `AgPeakUpdate` runs AFTER the `HistoryDealsTotal` read rather than before it, so the Q9 deferral's count is provably the one Phase 1 took; the fold re selects the same window with the same anchor, so the count could not differ either way.
THE FOUR JOURNAL LINES THE ACCEPTANCE ROWS DEPEND ON ALL EXIST. `realized peak reconciled|anchor|reconstructed|persisted|taken|source`, where `source` names the D3.1 case outright as `no_file`, `equal_anchor_max`, `stale_anchor_reconstructed` or `backward_step_max`, so the two legs of the restart row are separable on the artifact. `realized peak raised|anchor|old|new|deal_ticket|deal_time`. `realized peak reset at rollover|old_anchor|new_anchor|old_peak|new_peak`, fired from a branch that re stamps the anchor and logs and does nothing else, THE RESET ITSELF BEING THE WALK'S WINDOW MOVING exactly as the FINAL's Reason requires and NOT an action taken. And `lock level|enforced_limit|peak|ratchet_level|peak_level|chosen|pnl|realized|floating`, emitted UNCONDITIONALLY before every breach declaration and at the LIFE cadence on every ACTIVE pass that does not declare, the deferred breach pass included. ONE PROPERTY OF `chosen` IS WRITTEN DOWN NOW SO IT IS NOT READ AS A DEFECT LATER: the peak term can never sit BELOW the ratchet term, a running maximum starting at zero and both terms subtracting the same `enforced_limit`, so the field reads `tie` while the day has no realized gain and `peak` once it has one, and `ratchet` is UNREACHABLE while that invariant holds. Seeing `ratchet` in a live journal is a finding, not a normal reading.
THE COMPILE, AND THE BUILD IDENTITY IN THE FORM STANDING RULE 7 REQUIRES RATHER THAN AS AN `.ex5` HASH. Every one of the four units compiled `0 errors, 0 warnings` before it was committed, and both vector scripts were compiled as well because `Persist.mqh` is included standalone by them, `AgPhase1ClockVectors` and `AgPhase2StateVectors`, both `0 errors, 0 warnings`. 2026-08-24, THE VERSION 1 RULING SET D1 THROUGH D8 WAS TAKEN AND RECORDED. Owner gave D1 through D5 in chat on this date and then D6, D7 and D8 as amendments in the same session, all recorded verbatim in the FINAL entry of this date in DECISIONS. WHAT WAS RULED, one line each: only trade closures including partial closures raise the day's realized peak and floating profit never does; the floor is checked against full account equity including floating, so an open position diving below it locks immediately rather than waiting to be closed; the peak rises only intraday and resets only at the day boundary; the stricter of the ratchet floor and the realized peak floor always wins, in every scenario including restart and day rollover, with no exceptions; a restart reconstructs the peak from today's deal history with the persisted value a cross check only and the stricter of the two taken on mismatch; the reset uses the day boundary already FINAL for the daily base with no new boundary defined anywhere; storage is a separate `peak_<login>.dat` so that `floor_<login>.dat` stays byte identical to what is deployed; the peak term uses the post ratchet `enforced_limit` rather than the raw live limit; and the peak never enters the lock snapshot, being PRE BREACH ONLY exactly as the ratchet is. THE VERIFICATION THAT PRECEDED THE RULING, performed read only in plan mode earlier this same date and recorded here because the ruling rests on it: twenty three FINAL entries touching the daily PnL formula, the daily base definition, the day boundary, the Phase 2 ratchet and the lock trigger conditions were quoted verbatim and checked one by one against the rulings, all twenty three found present exactly once and every quoted clause byte identical, ZERO CONFLICTS FOUND. One clause is superseded and one only, question FIVE's "NO new persisted artifact is added"; two entries are constrained but NOT superseded, question SEVEN and the 2026-08-20 defect 3 shape 1 ruling, both of which version 1 satisfies in letter. A GOVERNANCE FACT SURFACED BY THE SAME VERIFICATION AND RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT IMPLICIT: `P3-1` and `P49`/`P53`, which ruling D4 makes the gate on deployment, DO NOT EXIST IN THIS FILE. They live only on the unmerged `phase3-defect-fixes` branch, together with three FINAL owner rulings of 2026-08-19 and 2026-08-20 that bear directly on this work, so this ledger cannot close the D4 gate from its own contents and a session reading only this file would not know the gate exists. line carries `reason=9` and the words `session marked clean` in the emission itself, which is the

AccountGuardian (XAUUSD.ecn,M15) removed`, `22:25:40.783 Terminal exit with code 0`,

exited cannot carry globals across, so the image is fresh by construction as well as by transition
form.

THE TIMER TICK RESET IS DEMONSTRATED ON MEASURED BOUNDARIES RATHER THAN CLAIMED, and the arithmetic is
given because the counter is the discriminator the ledger has leaned on since 2026-07-30. The counter
is PER SESSION, and both closed sessions of this date prove it: init 20:46:14.542 to deinit 22:09:02.450
is 4967.9 seconds against a reported `timer_ticks=4967`, and init 22:09:22.201 to deinit 22:25:39.589 is
977.4 seconds against a reported `timer_ticks=977`. Both match to the second at the one second timer, so
each session's counter began at ZERO at its own init and nothing carried over. The outgoing session's
977 is therefore its own lifetime and not a running total, which is exactly the reset property an
inherited image would violate by continuing the previous count, as the 2026-08-06 chart change did when
it ran 124475 to 124480 across a pair of deinits.

THE RUNNING IMAGE IS `1C88C4A1`, AND THE LIMIT OF THAT CLAIM IS STATED RATHER THAN LEFT IMPLICIT,
because standing rule 7 says the ex5 hash is not build identity and that runtime behaviour in the
journal is the only measure of what is actually loaded. What is measured: the file on disk hashes
`1C88C4A1`, its last write is the copy at 22:21:53, the process exited and restarted, and the expert was
loaded at 22:25:55.867 which is AFTER that write, so the image loaded is the file that was on disk. What
is NOT available: a runtime discriminator. The defect 1 fix alters lock expiry and the defect 3 fix
alters what a boot derived lock persists, and this boot had NO LOCK, `locked_until=1970.01.01 00:00:00`,
so the two builds are behaviourally identical on a flat no lock boot and the journal cannot tell them
apart. THAT IS NOT A GAP IN THIS DEPLOY, IT IS THE REASON THE LIVE ACCEPTANCE ROWS EXIST, and it is why
neither defect is marked DONE here.

A MEASUREMENT TRAP WAS HIT AND CAUGHT THIS SESSION, recorded because it is the fourth of its class in
this project and it very nearly produced a false negative on the whole harvest. The first search for
AGVEC lines returned ZERO on all three candidate journals. `iconv` DOES NOT EXIST on this machine, and
the shell reported that on stderr while the pipeline's `grep -c` printed a clean zero on stdout, so the
absence was manufactured entirely by a missing decoder. Standing observation rule 5 is what caught it:
the decoder was made to prove it had read the file, the same command returned zero LINES as well as
zero matches, and the files are 1.5 megabytes. Re read through PowerShell with an explicit
`-Encoding Unicode` the same files give 1704, 2887 and 2538 lines and 85 AGVEC hits. This sits with the
CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06, the stale directory entry trap of 2026-08-16 and the alias shadowing of
2026-08-17. THE REUSABLE PART: a text tool that is absent behaves exactly like a text tool that found
nothing, so rule 5's requirement to confirm the decoder read the file covers the case where the decoder
was never there at all.

TASK 2, THE WEEKEND WINDOW, ITS BOUNDARIES AND WHAT THE RESTART TRUNCATED.

THE WINDOW OPENS AT THE FRIDAY CLOSE AND IS STILL OPEN AT THE END OF THE ARTIFACTS. The server clock
stops at `2026.08.21 23:57:59` and every LIFE line from that instant to the last line banked carries
that same server stamp, across Saturday and Sunday alike. The LOCAL instant of the last tick is derived
from the guardian's own measure rather than from the clock: Saturday's first LIFE line at local
00:00:08.996 reads `quote_age=131s`, giving a last tick at local 2026-08-21 23:57:57, and Saturday's
last pre restart line at 23:29:35.548 reads `quote_age=84698s`, giving the same 2026-08-21 23:57:57 to
the second. Two independent readings 23 hours apart agree exactly. THE ANCHOR IS PINNED at
`2026.08.21 01:00:00` for the whole weekend, since a frozen `TimeCurrent` never crosses, and the last
genuine rollover in the harvest is Friday's,
`AG|2026.08.21 01:00:05|INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.20 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.21 01:00:00|jump=86400s`
at local 01:00:03.347. THE WINDOW DOES NOT CLOSE INSIDE THESE ARTIFACTS. The market had not reopened
when the last line was written and the Monday reopen is not in the harvest, which bounds what any row
can close on today.

THERE ARE TWO RESTARTS IN THE WINDOW, NOT ONE, and the earlier one was not reported. SATURDAY:
`AG|2026.08.21 23:57:59|INFO|deinit|reason=9|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=336575` at
local 23:29:57.392, then init at 23:30:15.552, `BOOT->SYNCING` at 23:30:15.574 and
`SYNCING->ACTIVE|history stable|polls=3/3` at 23:30:18.580. SUNDAY, the reported one, is the restart
bracket quoted above, deinit 20:45:30.432 and ACTIVE again at 20:46:17.555. Both are clean deinits at
reason=9 and both come up through the canonical `BOOT->SYNCING` fresh image form.

WHAT THE RESTARTS TRUNCATED, stated exactly, because it is a measurement loss and not a data loss. NO
JOURNAL WAS LOST: the EA journal is per day and append only and every line either side of both restarts
is present. What was cut is the guardian's OWN continuous measure of the frozen quote. `quote_age` is a
LOCAL tick age accumulator and it reseeds at every init, so the single closed market condition was
sampled as THREE separate runs instead of one. Run one ended at `quote_age=84698s` at local 2026-08-22
23:29:35, 23 hours 31 minutes 38 seconds, cut by the Saturday restart. Run two ended at
`quote_age=76498s` at local 2026-08-23 20:45:14, 21 hours 14 minutes 58 seconds, cut by the Sunday
restart. Run three was still climbing when the artifacts end. `seconds_in_state` was truncated with it,
76496 on the last line before the Sunday restart and 28 on the first ACTIVE line after it, and the
ACTIVE episode that began Saturday 23:30:18 ended. NOTHING THE ROWS BELOW DEPEND ON WAS TRUNCATED,
because each run reports the same branch and no verdict rests on any one run's length.

ROW ONE, THE MARKET CLOSED DISTINCTION. IT DOES NOT CLOSE, AND WHAT THE WEEKEND SUPPLIES IS THE
STRONGEST NEGATIVE THE ROW HAS EVER HAD. `AgObservabilityNote` returns the quote age string when
`AgQuoteSessionOpen()` is true and the fixed string `market closed` when it is false, so exactly one of
two strings can appear. THE STRING `market closed` OCCURS ZERO TIMES IN ALL FOUR JOURNALS, counted over
the whole files and not sampled. The other branch occurs 120 times Thursday, 23 times Friday, 2874
times Saturday and 2393 times Sunday. SATURDAY IS THE DECISIVE FILE: a complete market closed day, 2878
LIFE lines, and 2874 of them carry the OPEN session branch with the age climbing monotonically to
`AG|2026.08.21 23:57:59|LIFE|state=ACTIVE|seconds_in_state=340175|waiting_on=quote_age=84698s|anchor=2026.08.21 01:00:00|realized=0.00|floating=0.00|base=2133.00|limit=106.65|pnl_vs_limit=0.00 vs -106.65|server=2026.08.21 23:57:59|local=2026.08.22 23:29:35`.
The four Saturday LIFE lines without the note are fully accounted for and none of them is a
`market closed` line: one SYNCING line at 23:30:16 reading
`waiting_on=history stability poll not yet run this session`, and three post boot ACTIVE lines at
23:30:46, 23:31:16 and 23:31:46 reading `waiting_on=-` because the age had not yet reached the ruled
120 second threshold. SO WHAT THE BROKER SESSION STATE REPORTED ACROSS THE CLOSED MARKET IS: OPEN,
CONTINUOUSLY, THROUGH THE ENTIRE WEEKEND. The 2026-08-19 observation that narrowed this row on one
night's nightly break now holds across a full weekend close, on three independent accumulations
totalling more than 44 hours of frozen feed, and the narrowing extends: on this broker and this Market
Watch the `market closed` branch is not reached during a weekend either. THAT IS AN OBSERVATION AND NOT
A RULING. Whether the row is reclassified as unreachable by construction, in the class amendment A5 and
P1-K already occupy, is the owner's to declare and no reclassification is applied here.

A SECOND PROPERTY OF THE NOTE WAS MEASURED FOR FREE AND IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT BEARS ON THE SAME ROW.
THE NOTE IS SUPPRESSED WHOLE ON A DEGRADED PASS. The twelve Sunday LIFE lines lacking the note are nine
DEGRADED lines, one SYNCING line and two under threshold ACTIVE lines, and every one of the nine reads
`waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED` with NEITHER the quote age string NOR
`market closed` anywhere on the line. So the note reports only on an ACTIVE, connected, non degraded
pass whose age has passed 120 seconds. A reader holding a DEGRADED line therefore cannot tell whether
the market was open or shut, which is a fourth suppression case beyond the three the row was written
against, and it is stated as a measurement with no fix proposed.

ROW TWO, P2-H, PER ITS STANDING INSTRUCTION. IT DOES NOT CLOSE, AND IT DID NOT FAIL EITHER, BECAUSE ITS
PRECONDITION NEVER AROSE. The row's claim is that a limit raised while locked produces a loud WARN
while the Q6 snapshot holds, so it needs a LOCKED evaluation pass to observe. THE ACCOUNT WAS NEVER
LOCKED AT ANY POINT IN THE WINDOW. Every state value appearing in the four journals is ACTIVE or
SYNCING and nothing else: Thursday and Friday ACTIVE only, Saturday and Sunday ACTIVE and SYNCING.
Stated precisely so no later session miscounts, a plain search for LOCKED returns exactly one hit in
each of Saturday and Sunday and BOTH are the substring inside `locked_until` in the boot line
`INFO|lock state file loaded|reason=-|locked_until=1970.01.01 00:00:00|weighed by the boot derivation at the SYNCING exit`,
which is the epoch value a file with no lock carries. There were no positions and no exposure: every
LIFE line in the window reads `realized=0.00|floating=0.00|base=2133.00|limit=106.65` and the terminal
journal confirms `0 positions, 0 orders` at each authorization. The string `input changed` occurs ZERO
times in all four files. So the weekend market close the 2026-08-19 ruling named as the window this row
waits for HAS NOW PASSED WITHOUT SUPPLYING IT, and it did not supply it for a reason that has nothing
to do with the market being shut: the row needs a lock, and a lock needs a losing position, and there
was none. NO SUBSTITUTE WAS ATTEMPTED and none may be, per the standing instruction of 2026-08-18. The
ruling's other clause is untouched and is restated rather than acted on: the row may prove unreachable
through the terminal UI altogether, since a reload always reseeds the witness first, and that outcome
is the owner's to declare rather than the executor's to infer. Nothing here declares it.

ROW THREE, WHAT THE BROKER SESSION STATE REPORTED, is answered inside row one above and is not
repeated.

THE IMPLEMENTATION, commit `4d6b5e2`, ONE FILE, 86 insertions and 9 deletions. `AgBootDerivation`
gains four out parameters, `have_snapshot_out`, `breach_time_out`, `limit_out` and `base_out`, all
initialised at the head of the function so the two NOT EVALUABLE returns leave them defined rather
than indeterminate, and all four assigned immediately after the three tier cascade. They are published
UNCONDITIONALLY rather than only when a witness fires, and that is deliberate: the caller decides on
`have_snapshot` and not on which witness won, so keeping the publication at one site keeps the
decision at one site. `AgEnterLockFromBoot` takes the same four and its DAILY_BREACH site is now a
three way branch, CORRUPT_STATE to `AgStateSetCorrupt` untouched, `have_snapshot` true preserving the
loaded triple byte for byte, and `have_snapshot` false writing the derivation's own. THE NON BLANKET
RULE IS THE WHOLE OF THE FIX and the build instruction named it explicitly: writing the derivation's
values unconditionally would have been a no op for the limit, since the cascade sets
`limit_cmp = g_ag_state_limit_snap` at tier 1, and would still have overwritten the base and the breach
time with freshly computed values, which is the substitution Q6 forbids.

WHY `limit_out` IS THE ENFORCED LIMIT AND NOT THE RAW LIVE ONE, recorded because getting it wrong
would be silent. `limit_cmp` is what the disjuncts compare against and is therefore what Q6 means by
the snapshot, and it is already the ratchet floor when the floor is the stricter value. Snapshotting
the raw live limit instead would have loosened every boot derived locked window by exactly the amount
the floor was holding back, which is the same trap the Stage 5 entry of 2026-08-18 recorded for the
ACTIVE path and which is now closed on this path by construction.

the reset the new destination needs and comment.

A DEFECT IN THE APPROVED PLAN'S OWN PHRASING WAS FOUND WHILE IMPLEMENTING IT, AND IT IS THE MOST
IMPORTANT THING IN THIS ENTRY. The plan's shape A table said the expiry block "gains whatever reset
the new destination needs", which reads as boilerplate and is not: WITHOUT THE RESET THE FIX DOES
NOTHING AT ALL. `g_ag_stable_polls` and `g_ag_last_history_total` are declared at `Pnl.mqh:250-251`
and are advanced or cleared ONLY inside `AgHistoryStable`, which is called from exactly two sites, the
SYNCING branch and the Q10 RESYNC gate, and neither runs while LOCKED. So at an expiry they still hold
whatever the last SYNCING occupancy left, which on a session that booted normally is
`g_ag_stable_polls = 3` with the deal total latched. Entering SYNCING in that state, on an account
whose deal count has not moved, takes `AgHistoryStable`'s count unchanged branch at `Pnl.mqh:273-274`,
increments to 4, returns `4 >= 3` true on the FIRST poll, and the EA leaves SYNCING on the very tick it
entered. The three second discipline the whole fix exists to buy would have been bought and not spent,
and the artifact would have read `polls=4/3`, which is neither the `polls=3/3` prediction P27 names nor
the `polls=<n>/3` for n below 3 it excludes. The expiry block therefore sets `g_ag_stable_polls = 0`
and `g_ag_last_history_total = -1` at `:487-488`, which are byte for byte the two assignments
`Pnl.mqh:257-258` already performs on a disconnect, so the semantics are borrowed from the file that
owns those globals rather than invented here.

TWO SMALLER CHOICES INSIDE THE SAME BLOCK, both recorded rather than left in the code to be found. The
transition now carries a detail field, `history stability required before the first post-expiry breach
decision|polls=0/<HistoryStablePolls>`, which is what P26's `<detail>` placeholder anticipated. And
`g_ag_dynamic_waiting_on` is set to `polls=0/<HistoryStablePolls>` instead of the empty string it was
set to before, because `AgWaitingOn`'s SYNCING default at `State.mqh:96-98` reads "history stability
poll not yet run this session", which is FALSE after an expiry since the boot occupancy already ran
one. A LIFE line that happens to land inside the three second window now says something true.

TASK 2, THE DEFECT 3 FIX PLAN, commit `1c655ca`, `docs/FIXPLAN_PHASE3_DEFECT3_2026-08-20.md`, and IT
RECOMMENDS NOTHING. Two candidate shapes are laid out side by side and they agree on everything except
one field's meaning, so the choice is isolated to that one disagreement: shape 1 writes the derivation
instant into `breach_time`, shape 2 recovers the true breach instant from the replay when the replay
disjunct fired and falls back to the derivation instant when only the live disjunct did. Shape 2 is the
only one that reaches outside the EA, into `Pnl.mqh`'s shared fold, and the plan names two sub variants
of that reach without choosing between them either. TWO FURTHER VARIANTS ARE NAMED AND EXPLICITLY NOT
PROPOSED: writing the snapshot from inside `AgBootDerivation`, which would put a persistence side
effect in a function that also returns NOT EVALUABLE at `:250` and `:257` on passes where nothing
should be written; and redefining `have_snapshot` so it no longer tests `limit_snap > 0.0`, which fixes
nothing because with zeros on disk there is no value for tier 1 to use and the change would turn a
missing protection into a wrong one.

THE PLAN SEPARATES WHAT THE DEFECT ACTUALLY COSTS, by enumerating every consumer rather than
characterising them. `g_ag_state_limit_snap` is a BEHAVIOUR GATE, since `have_snapshot` at `:273-275`
tests it and tier 1 at `:279` is its only consumer, so zeros make tier 1 unreachable for the whole class
of boot derived locks. `g_ag_state_breach_time` IS READ BY NOTHING in the running logic, its only
consumers being the serializer, the loader and the vectors, so losing it is a pure loss of record.
`g_ag_state_base_snap` is read outside persistence in exactly one place, the Q6 input change WARN at
`:451-452`, where a boot derived lock today prints `limit=0.00 base=0.00` and tells the operator the
opposite of the truth about what governs their locked window.

THE SHAPE A INTERLOCK IS STATED EXPLICITLY, as the instruction required, and it is now a fact about
this branch rather than a prediction. After `0a4c86b` the expiry block clears `g_ag_locked_until` at
`:463` and calls `AgStateResetModel()` at `:464` before transitioning, and `OnTimer` rewrites the GV
mirror from memory every tick at `:918`, so at the SYNCING exit the FILE witness at `:211` cannot fire,
the GV witness at `:224` cannot fire, and the DERIVED witness is the only one left, with `have_snapshot`
false for the same reason. EVERY EXPIRY TIME RE LOCK IS THEREFORE A DERIVED WITNESS LOCK WITH NO
SNAPSHOT, which is exactly the case defect 3 gets wrong, so on this branch as it stands every expiry
time re lock would persist zeros. That is the window the owner's coupling closes. Once defect 3 is
fixed the same path persists reason 1, the derivation's own `limit_cmp` as the limit, its own `base`,
and a non zero breach time, and the NEXT boot then reads `tier=snapshot` where today it is structurally
incapable of reading anything but `tier=floor` or `tier=live`.

PREDICTIONS RUN P47 TO P60 and continue the defect 1 plan's numbering unbroken. P49 is the row the fix

reachable again, one word in one journal field. P51 and P52 are the two negatives that stop the fix

PERFORMED BY THE OWNER in the main checkout, from the command and the message file the executor dictated, because a worktree cannot check out a branch that is already checked out elsewhere and this session is isolated to its worktree by design. The executor verified it read only afterwards and did not perform it.

VERIFICATION, all three commands run against the merged tree rather than against this narrative, and their output quoted rather than summarised. `git log --oneline -1 main` returns `1ea7c2d merge: Phase 2 Stage 7, deploy and live acceptance`. `git diff --stat main worktree-phase2-stage7` returns EMPTY, so main's tree is byte identical to the branch and nothing was dropped in the merge. `git log 49ea967..main --format='%an %ae' | sort -u` returns exactly one line, `www8351 www8351@gmail.com`. An attribution scan over the same range for co-authored, generated with, anthropic, ai assistant and claude returns ZERO on all five.

TWO MECHANICS CHECKS THE EXECUTOR PROMISED AT PREPARATION TIME AND THEN RAN. The commit has TWO parents, `49ea9679d19c58bfadb9fc7e73b1b1c30bb018c4` and `6219dfe07d4e19437f7b7838b8938ec393bc98df`, so `--no-ff` held and no fast forward occurred despite the merge base equalling main's head, which is exactly the case where git would otherwise have fast forwarded. AND THE COMMIT CARRIES THE COMPOSED MESSAGE rather than git's bare default MERGE_MSG. That is the third consecutive merge to survive the window that lost the messages of `937c604` on 2026-08-08 and `d52f063` on 2026-08-11, and the reason is the mitigation adopted on 2026-08-17 and repeated here: merge with `--no-ff -F <message file>` in ONE command, never `--no-ff --no-commit` followed by a separate commit, so no prepared index sits uncommitted for a periodic committer to take. Still a mitigation and still not a diagnosis; the cause of the two original losses remains unestablished.

THE ANTICIPATED LEDGER.md HAND UNION WAS NOT NEEDED AND THE EXPECTATION IS NOW DISCHARGED BY MEASUREMENT. The owner's standing instruction was that this merge would need the union per the `ce6964d` precedent. Re derived at merge time as that precedent itself demands: the merge base was `49ea967`, equal to main's head, with `HEAD..main` at zero commits, so main had not moved since the branch was cut and there was no second side for a union to reconcile. No conflict was possible on any file. This is the second time the anticipation has proved unnecessary, after 2026-08-17, and the lesson both times is the same one: re derive the base at merge time rather than trusting either the instruction or a prior entry.

WHAT THE MERGE CARRIED, and the count is only right because a defect was caught first. The branch's own `git diff --stat main HEAD` reported FOUR files at first inspection, against a ledger narrative claiming a dozen banked journals, and that discrepancy is what exposed the `.gitignore` swallow now recorded as its own OPEN issue. After the thirteen journals were renamed from `.log` to `.txt` the merge carries seventeen files: LEDGER.md, the ratchet epsilon hotfix in `Persist.mqh`, the eight new vectors in `AgPhase2StateVectors.mq5`, the live breach state file, and thirteen evidence journals. Git classifies the journals as binary because MT5 writes them UTF-16LE, so they archive faithfully and will not text diff. HAD THE MERGE RUN BEFORE THAT CATCH, main would now cite evidence paths that do not exist in it, and the files themselves would have gone with the worktree.

2026-08-19, A REPORTED MACHINE SLEEP WAS INVESTIGATED AND NO SUCH EVENT EXISTS IN ANY ARTIFACT. Owner reported that the PC entered sleep by accident and was woken, describing it as a third clock shape after a disconnect and a kill, with an approximate window of 13:00 to 15:00, and asked for the surrounding lines and the state file comparison. THE INVESTIGATION RETURNED A NEGATIVE AND IT IS RECORDED AS ONE, because the 2026-08-05 rule binds an entry to what its own session measured and a reported event that leaves no trace must be written down as absent rather than assumed and narrated. THE WINDOW WAS FIRST READ AS 2026-08-19 AND THE OWNER CORRECTED IT TO 2026-08-18, so the first pass dismissed it on the wrong ground, that 13:00 to 15:00 had not yet happened when the check ran at 10:22 on the 19th. That ground is withdrawn and the correct window was then scanned directly at a tighter threshold. RESULT ON THE CORRECTED WINDOW: across 13:00 to 15:00 on 2026-08-18 there is exactly ONE discontinuity in the guardian's LIFE sequence over 35 seconds, and it is 69 seconds at `13:45:33 -> 13:46:42`, which is the owner's own P2-B terminal restart via Task Manager, already banked in this entry. The line count corroborates it: 239 LIFE lines across the 13:00 and 14:00 hours against 240 expected at a 30 second cadence, the single missing tick being the one that 69 second gap swallowed. A two hour suspension would have left a two hour hole; the largest hole is 69 seconds. THREE INDEPENDENT FACTS PLACE THE MACHINE AWAKE THROUGHOUT: the P2-C adapter disconnect at 14:58:54 to 15:05:12 produced CONTINUOUS LIFE lines carrying `DEGRADED: disconnected`, which is the disconnect signature and not a suspension; the executor read the live log at 13:41, 13:47 and 15:04 and each read returned fresh lines; and the broker liquidation at 13:38:07, the relaunch at 13:46:41 and the reconnect at 15:04 all fall inside the window and are all evidenced.

THE ARITHMETIC, which is stronger than an absence of notices. On 2026-08-19 the LIFE lines run at their 30 second cadence from `00:00:00` to `10:22:00` with ZERO gaps exceeding 60 seconds. The expected line count across that span is 37320 seconds divided by 30 plus one, which is 1245, and the ACTUAL count is 1245: every tick is present and none is missing. The counter agrees independently: `seconds_in_state=33720` on the `10:22:01` line, and 10:22:01 less the 01:00:01 entry into ACTIVE is exactly 33720 seconds, so it tracks wall clock with no jump. The only non LIFE lines in the entire day are the three expiry lines at 01:00. No `deinit`, no `init`, no `TRANSITION` beyond the expiry, no `DEGRADED`, no `quote_age` after the nightly break closed. THE FILES AGREE: `state_1200252169.dat` is unchanged at md5 `793A00F2931A3563EE890EDA20E30BFE` since 01:00:00, and `halt_1200252169.dat` has not been written since 2026-08-18 16:01:24, which is decisive on its own because `AgHaltAppendSession` writes that file at EVERY `OnInit`, so there has been no EA initialisation of any kind for eighteen hours. On 2026-08-18 the same gap scan finds exactly three, all already banked and all explained: 25788 seconds at 03:28:35, the unattended Windows Update reboot; 69 seconds at 13:45:33, the terminal restart; and 103 seconds at 15:59:42, the two forced kills. NOTHING between 13:00 and 15:00 on either day.

CONFIRMED FROM OUTSIDE MT5 AT THE OWNER'S REQUEST, WHICH IS THE STRONGER FORM OF THIS NEGATIVE. `Get-WinEvent` over the Windows System log for event ids 42 and 107, sleep and resume, across 2026-08-18 00:00:00 to 2026-08-19 23:59:59 returned "No events were found that match the specified selection criteria". So the operating system recorded no suspension either, from a source entirely independent of the guardian's own logging. Owner accepted the negative: no sleep occurred.

WHAT A SLEEP WOULD LOOK LIKE, recorded so a future session can recognise the third shape rather than re deriving it. A suspended machine cannot run the MT5 timer, so a sleep leaves TWO simultaneous signatures that neither of the other two shapes produces together: a gap in the LIFE sequence, like a kill, AND a `seconds_in_state` that JUMPS across that gap rather than resuming, because the counter is derived from a stored entry timestamp rather than accumulated tick by tick. A disconnect freezes the server clock while local advances and the lines keep coming. A kill ends the lines and starts a new session with a fresh `init` and a halt file entry. A sleep would keep the same session, skip the lines, and step the counter. Neither signature is present anywhere in the two days examined. The only machine level event on record remains the 03:28 to 10:38 Windows Update reboot of 2026-08-18, which is a reboot and not a suspension, and it is named here as the nearest thing rather than as an identification of what the owner recalls.

THE NIGHTLY FREEZE STRADDLED THE EXPIRY ANCHOR, WHICH IS THE PRECONDITION OF AN OPEN ISSUES ROW ARRIVING LIVE ON THE FIRST NIGHT IT COULD. The server clock stopped at `2026.08.18 23:57:59` and stayed there while local time advanced normally, with the Stage 6 note climbing from `quote_age=121s` at 00:00:00 to `quote_age=3691s` at 00:59:30, then jumped in one step to `2026.08.19 01:00:01`. That is a 61 minute 32 second freeze across the 01:00 anchor, the same shape measured on the night of 2026-08-13 into 14. THE LOCK EXPIRED ONE SECOND AFTER THE CLOCK CAUGHT UP, so in wall clock terms it expired on time and the P2-I delay was zero, because the freeze happened to end exactly at the anchor rather than after it. THE HAZARD DID NOT BITE AND ITS PRECONDITION IS NOW OBSERVED: the guardian went LOCKED to ACTIVE directly, with no SYNCING pass and no resync gate, on a clock that had just jumped an hour, and the first ACTIVE evaluation ran ungated. THE `quote_age` BRANCH OF THE STAGE 6 NOTE FIRED FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND THE `market closed` BRANCH STILL HAS NOT. `AgObservabilityNote` returns `AgQuoteSessionOpen() ? ("quote_age=" + age + "s") : "market closed"`, so the two are mutually exclusive and the artifact now carries the OPEN-session branch across a full hour of frozen quotes. Worth recording because it bears on the one item still keeping the Stage 4 entry open: throughout the entire nightly break the broker's session table reported the quote session OPEN, which is why the note read `quote_age` and never `market closed`. If that holds generally then the `market closed` branch cannot be reached during a nightly break at all and needs a weekend or a holiday, which narrows how that last item can ever be closed. Stated as one night's observation and not as a rule.

THE RECONCILIATION BELOW IS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN, CORRECTED 2026-08-19 BY THE OWNER'S DIRECT READING OF THE HISTORY TAB. THE DEPOSIT LANDED AT 2026-08-18 16:51:35, as the owner stated and as planned; the executor's claim that the 2133 was already present at 16:01:27 is FALSE. RECORDED AS OWNER READ FROM THE UI, because no artifact corroborates it and the executor states that plainly: a grep of the whole 2026-08-18 terminal journal for balance and deposit deal lines returns ZERO, and the journal after 16:30 carries only network lines, so MT5 never wrote a balance record anywhere the executor can read.
THE ERROR, NAMED PRECISELY RATHER THAN GLOSSED, and there were two faults stacked. FAULT ONE, the load bearing step. The executor read `tier=floor` as meaning the live limit MEANINGFULLY exceeded the floor. It licenses no such thing: the label comes from `limit_cmp < live_limit`, an EXACT double comparison, and the ratchet epsilon defect fixed the day before proves that exact comparisons in this codebase are routinely decided by a one ULP round trip artifact. `tier=floor` is satisfied by a margin of 1e-14 and cannot carry a quantitative conclusion. FAULT TWO, the arithmetic, and this is the one that should have been caught. Base is INVARIANT under trading deals, since a loss reduces `ACCOUNT_BALANCE` and the all deals sum by the same amount, so with `base=2133.13` and `realized=-1893.50` the balance at 16:01:27 was `2133.13 - 1893.50 = 239.63`, the liquidation residue. That is the SAME 239.63 the executor's own bound produced, and it was treated as a floor beneath a supposed pre existing 2133 instead of being recognised as the residue itself. The executor then compounded it by assuming the owner's 2133 would be ADDED to that figure, expecting about 2372.63, seeing 2133.00 at the 01:00 rollover, and concluding the deposit was absent. THE CORRECT READING: the balance operation at 16:51:35 took the account FROM 239.63 TO 2133.00, and today's `base=2133.00` is the post deposit balance rather than evidence of anything pre existing. Every artifact is consistent with the owner's timestamp.
ALSO NOTED FROM THE SAME JOURNAL PULL AND NOT PREVIOUSLY RECORDED: an unplanned connection loss on 2026-08-18 at 18:02:53, `connection to JustMarkets-Demo3 lost`, with reauthorization at 18:09:04 and resynchronisation reporting `0 positions, 0 orders`. About six minutes, while the account was LOCKED, nobody's doing, and it passed without incident.

THE WITHDRAWN RECONCILIATION IS PRESERVED BELOW AS WRITTEN, since a ledger that edits away its wrong turns cannot be audited.
A RECONCILIATION THE EXECUTOR CANNOT CLOSE AND IS HANDING TO THE OWNER. `base=2133.00` with `limit=106.65`, and the floor file storing `106.65000000` exactly, pins `DailyLossPercent` at 5 in the running EA beyond inference, since 2133.00 at five percent is 106.65 exactly. With percent 5 established, the 16:01:27 witness line, `realized=-1893.50` with `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor`, REQUIRES `base > 2133.13` at that moment and therefore `ACCOUNT_BALANCE > 239.63`. Had the owner's 2133 deposit landed at about 16:52 onto a zero balance, the balance at 16:01:27 would have been 0.00, base would have been 1893.50, the live limit 94.68, and that line would have printed `limit_cmp=94.68|tier=live`. IT DID NOT. So the 2133 now in the account was ALREADY PRESENT at 16:01:27, and no further 2133 landed after it, or today's base would read about 4266 rather than 2133.00. The executor cannot see balance operations at all: the terminal journal records no balance deal on any date this session. The owner is asked to check the timestamp of the balance operation in the account history, and the discrepancy is recorded unresolved rather than explained away.

A MEASUREMENT TRAP FOUND IN THE DICTATION ITSELF AND CORRECTED BEFORE A SINGLE PATH WAS NAMED. The instruction says to dictate md5 from `git show` at `3ad7ef4`. That value is WRONG for this purpose on five of the seven files, because `.gitattributes` sets `* text=auto` and `core.autocrlf` is `true`, so `git show` emits the LF normalized blob while the file the owner actually copies carries CRLF. `AccountGuardian.mq5` is the clearest case: blob `da184295730b31a994866d55ca7a46de`, file on disk `c1e12b30ae8fddd76ccaebcf2eef7069`. Dictating the blob hash would have made every landed file fail its own verification. What is dictated is therefore the md5 of the source file on disk, which is what `Get-FileHash` reports at both ends of the copy, with the blob hash recorded here only for provenance: `Clock.mqh` blob `6c74f366bf04f4376659465d7e53b9db`, `Persist.mqh` blob `6985e83a4189aad6264cf120c6bd197b`, `Pnl.mqh` blob `f26dd45fd7355374b836de71a9d56cc1`.

THREE SESSIONS ARE IN TODAY'S LOG AND THE MIDDLE ONE WAS NOT ORDERED BY ANYONE, which matters because it is where the first Phase 2 ACTIVE pass actually happened. `init|build=Phase1` at 00:09:50 is the pre deploy session. `init|build=Phase2` at 01:59:57 is an EA reload the terminal performed by itself the moment MetaEditor wrote the new `.ex5`, seven seconds before any human acted, and its `deinit|reason=9` at 02:01:19 is the owner's restart taking it down. `init|build=Phase2` at 02:01:36 is the ordered restart. THE RATCHET SEED ROW IS CLOSED on the first Phase 2 ACTIVE pass, both halves quoted from the middle session: `AG|2026.08.18 01:59:57|DEBUG|no ratchet floor file, first session on this account` at init, then `AG|2026.08.18 02:00:02|INFO|ratchet seeded|anchor=2026.08.18 01:00:00|floor=106.66` on the first pass after `SYNCING->ACTIVE` at 02:00:01. AN UNPLANNED CRASH SURVIVAL LANDED BETWEEN PART 1 AND THE HOTFIX, AND IT IS BANKED AS EVIDENCE BECAUSE NOBODY DESIGNED IT. The machine was restarted at about 03:29 by an unattended Windows Update. This was reported by the owner and then VERIFIED FROM THE JOURNAL rather than taken on report, which matters because a clean shutdown and a killed process look identical in a summary and completely different in the log. The 02:01:36 session's last line is `AG|2026.08.18 03:28:37|WARN|ratchet HOLDING ...` and there is NO `deinit` line after it, against three clean `deinit|reason=` lines elsewhere in the same file, so the session was killed rather than closed. Recovery, quoted: `AG|2026.08.18 10:38:22|INFO|init|build=Phase2|account=1200252169|server=JustMarkets-Demo3`, then `DEBUG|no lock state file, first session on this account`, `INFO|ratchet floor loaded|anchor=2026.08.18 01:00:00|floor=106.66`, `DEBUG|crash-loop check|chain=1/3 consecutive unclean, gap bound 300s`, `TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s` at 10:38:22 and `TRANSITION|SYNCING->ACTIVE|history stable|polls=3/3` at 10:38:25. A grep for SAFE_HALT across the whole day's log returns ZERO. The crash-loop chain reading 1 of 3 rather than 2 is correct and not a miss: the gap bound is 300 seconds and the outage was about seven hours, so the chain did not extend, which is the negative-gap discipline behaving as ruled. The floor survived the reboot and reloaded from file, which is the persistence half of the ratchet proven by an event rather than by a vector. Owner has since set the `NoAutoRebootWithLoggedOnUsers` policy and Active hours 02:00 to 20:00, so this is not expected to recur, and the observation is kept because an unplanned kill is evidence no scheduled test produces.

THE SAME LOG QUANTIFIES THE DEFECT AND THE NUMBER IS WORSE THAN THE FINDING FIRST SUGGESTED. `ratchet HOLDING` appears 221 times in `20260818.log`. One is the legitimate vector case from the 00:09 run, `live=150.00 floor=80.00`. 2026-08-18, PHASE 2 STAGE 7 HOTFIX, THE RATCHET EPSILON, committed on `worktree-phase2-stage7` under the owner ruling of this date recorded in DECISIONS. Two files, 92 insertions and 2 deletions. The two deletions are the whole of the behavioural change: `if(live_limit < g_ag_floor_currency)` becomes `if(live_limit < g_ag_floor_currency - AG_PNL_EPSILON)` and `else if(live_limit > g_ag_floor_currency)` becomes `else if(live_limit > g_ag_floor_currency + AG_PNL_EPSILON)`. Everything else added is comment and vectors. `Persist.mqh` gains `#include <AccountGuardian/Pnl.mqh>` because `AG_PNL_EPSILON` is defined there, and that include was checked for cycles before it was written rather than after: `Pnl.mqh` includes only `Clock.mqh`, declares its own globals and names `DailyLossPercent` and `DailyLossCurrency` only inside message strings, so it drags in no EA-only symbol and the vectors script, which includes `Persist.mqh` alone, still stands on its own.

THE COST OF THE FIX IS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED, since a one cent band is not free. A genuine limit change smaller than one cent now moves neither the floor nor the journal, and in the lowering direction that errs LOOSE by up to a cent, which is the first place in Phase 2 where a tolerance does. It is accepted on the same grounds the breach disjuncts already use `AG_PNL_EPSILON` for money comparisons, and it is named here so a later session weighs it instead of rediscovering it.

VECTORS, section K, eight new checks taking the set from 76 to 84. `k1` reloads a floor that was seeded from the live arithmetic exactly as the guardian computes it, `AgLimitCurrency(2133.13, 5.0, 0.0)`, rather than from a round number, because a round number does not exercise the bug. `k2`, `k2b` and `k2c` are the row the ruling asked for: one pass at the same live limit immediately after the reload, with no branch permitted to fire. The two assertions are chosen so they cannot false pass. A `g_ag_last_ratchet_warn` still at zero proves the HOLD branch was not taken, because that branch always warns when the stamp is zero; a bit identical `g_ag_floor_currency` proves the LOWER branch was not taken, because that branch is the only writer of the global. `k3` and `k4` then do the same thing WITHOUT depending on how any particular value rounds, by placing the floor half a cent above and half a cent below the live limit, which makes the row deterministic on any platform. `k4`'s direction is the mirror hazard the finding named and it is now covered: under the old exact comparison a floor a hair ABOVE the live limit selected the LOWER branch, and that branch calls `AgFloorSave`, so the guardian would have rewritten the file on every pass forever. `k5` and `k6` guard the fix from being a blanket mute, at two cents, the smallest move that clears a one cent band: a two cent raise must still warn and a two cent decrease must still lower the floor.

STATIC ROWS AND SCOPE, re run against the modified tree rather than carried from Part 1. Zero trade API calls in the build, unchanged. Scope: `git diff --stat` names two files and no others, so `AccountGuardian.mq5` is untouched and the pre breach tail of `AgEvaluateActive` is byte identical to `3ad7ef4` by construction rather than by inspection. NOT YET DONE AND NOT CLAIMED: the compile is the owner's and has not run at the time this paragraph was written, and the 84 vectors have not executed. Both are recorded as pending below rather than as passing.

PART 2, THE OBSERVABILITY ROW, CLOSED ON QUOTED JOURNAL. THE SERVER CLOCK FROZE FOR THE WHOLE WINDOW AND THAT IS A SECOND INDEPENDENT WITNESS TO THE SAME EVENT. ONE ORDERING DETAIL IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT LOOKS LIKE A GAP AND IS NOT ONE. The LIFE line at local 12:09:03 already carries the frozen `server=2026.08.18 12:08:42`, yet it reads `waiting_on=-` with NO DEGRADED marker, and the RESYNC gate arms four seconds later. So for roughly one pass the guardian was working from a quote that had already stopped advancing while still reporting itself healthy. That is the ruled behaviour rather than a miss, and it is the first live sighting of it: the stale quote FINAL of 2026-08-18 put the whole boundary on the connection state and explicitly refused to gate on quote freshness, so a pass that sees `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` true is a normal pass no matter how old the quote is. Banked here so a later session reading this window does not mistake the four second lag for a defect in the marker.

WHAT THE ROW DOES NOT PROVE, stated so the evidence is not read as more than it is. Also confirmed clean across the same window and the 48 minutes around it: zero `TRANSITION` lines after the 11:29 boot, so the EA held ACTIVE and RESYNC behaved as a gate inside ACTIVE rather than as a state of its own; zero SAFE_HALT; zero ALERT; and zero EA emitted `ratchet HOLDING` lines, which extends the hotfix's proof through a disconnect and a reconnect.

OPERATING CONSTRAINTS FOR PART 3, given by the owner before the live breach and recorded because they explain what this session did NOT close as much as what it did. The executor stops and waits for an explicit go before each of four owner actions: placing manual losing trades on the demo, restarting the terminal, disconnecting the adapter, and changing any EA input. The executor also stops AFTER the lock event and waits for the owner to confirm that positions are still open, rather than inferring the position state from the journal, which is the correct discipline for ruling TWO since the journal reports the guardian's decision and not the broker's book. Four things are NOT authorized: closing or modifying any position, writing anything under the MT5 Terminal folder, merging to main, and pushing. Three of those four were already standing, RULE A of 2026-08-17 for the folder and CLAUDE.md for merge and push, and the position one is moot from the executor's side since no trade API is reachable from a dictation. A FIFTH ITEM IS NEW AND IS THE ONE A LATER READER WILL NEED: the 01:00 row is NOT to be marked DONE today, whatever the expiry window does tonight. So if that row is found open in this ledger with the mechanism apparently working, that is an owner instruction being honoured and not an omission.

WHERE MAIN STANDS, CORRECTED BY THE OWNER AND THEN RE DERIVED RATHER THAN ACCEPTED. Owner confirmed that `49ea967` on main is the ledger record of the Phase 2 merge and accepted it, so main's head is `49ea967` and NOT `3ad7ef4`. `3ad7ef4` is the merge commit itself and it is main's second most recent commit, which is why the Stage 7 instruction naming main "at `3ad7ef4`" is right about the deployed tree and one commit short of main's head. THE ANTICIPATED LEDGER.md HAND UNION IS RECORDED AS ANTICIPATED, AND THE MEASUREMENT AS OF NOW SAYS IT WOULD NOT BE NEEDED. On today's refs the merge would therefore resolve LEDGER.md with no hand work at all. THE EXPECTATION IS NOT DISCHARGED BY THAT and is deliberately left standing, because it becomes correct the moment main moves, which is exactly what happens if the owner commits to main independently before the Stage 7 branch merges, and because the merge is not being performed now. Re derive the merge base at merge time rather than trusting either this paragraph or the instruction; that is the whole lesson of the `ce6964d` precedent and of the entry that found it unnecessary.

NOT DONE, AND NOT DONE DELIBERATELY: no merge and no push.
