PART 3, THE LIVE BREACH. A LOCK WAS DECLARED ON THE REAL ACCOUNT AT 13:02:42 AND THE ROW AS SPECIFIED DID NOT CLOSE. Both halves of that sentence are the entry. The instruction's row asked for exactly one `TRANSITION ACTIVE to LOCKED` line with full arithmetic. There is no such line: `ACTIVE->LOCKED` appears ZERO times in `20260818.log` and `SYNCING->LOCKED` appears once. The lock is real, correct and fully evidenced, and it arrived through the boot derivation rather than through the ACTIVE breach tail, so the row is recorded as what happened rather than as what was asked for.

THE SEQUENCE, quoted, because the interleaving is the whole story. At 13:02:33 the M1 session was at `realized=574.00|floating=-368.00|pnl_vs_limit=206.00 vs -106.66`, still in profit. At 13:02:37 the ACTIVE breach tail detected the breach and refused to act on it yet: `AG|2026.08.18 13:02:37|WARN|breach deferred one pass: no new deal visible, count=21`. That is the one deferral mechanism of the 2026-08-18 DECISIONS entry behaving exactly as ruled. At 13:02:39, two seconds later, the owner changed the chart period again and the session was torn down mid deferral: `INFO|deinit|reason=3|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=5586`. THE SECOND PASS NEVER RAN, so the declaration the deferral was waiting to make never happened on that path. Three seconds after that the replacement session reached the SYNCING exit and caught the same breach independently: `AG|2026.08.18 13:02:42|INFO|boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=0|realized=574.00|floating=-920.50|running_min=-7.00|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, then `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED|boot derivation: DAILY_BREACH|locked_until=2026.08.19 01:00:00`.

THE ARITHMETIC IS CHECKED RATHER THAN QUOTED. `realized=574.00` plus `floating=-920.50` is `-346.50`, which clears `-106.66` with room. `live=1` and `replay=0` is the correct pair and not an inconsistency: the replay folds CLOSED deals and its `running_min=-7.00` never approached the limit, while the entire loss sat in open positions, so the live disjunct is the only one that could fire. `tier=floor` because no state file existed at that moment, so the cascade skipped tier 1 and took the ratcheted floor, which equals the live limit today. `locked_until=1787101200` is 2026-08-19 01:00:00, exactly one day anchor forward, which is `AgNextDayAnchor` and nothing else.

COUNTS, ALL EXACT AND ALL FROM THE FILE: one `SYNCING->LOCKED`, zero `ACTIVE->LOCKED`, one `|ALERT|` event, one `Alert:` popup which is that same event surfacing to the terminal rather than a second declaration, one `boot witness` line, one `breach deferred` line. LIFE lines from 13:03:03 read `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until`.

THE OLDEST OPEN ITEM IN THIS LEDGER CLOSED BY ACCIDENT AND IT IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THIS ENTRY. The three boot witnesses have been unmeasurable since Stage 4 and have kept an ISSUES entry open through five stages, on the argument that each needs a real state file, a real GV or real broker deals that no synthetic fixture can produce. THE DERIVED HISTORY WITNESS, witness 3, HAS NOW FIRED ON A REAL BREACH with its arithmetic logged. It was not produced by the test plan: it was produced by the owner changing the chart period at the exact moment the ACTIVE path had deferred, which is a two second window nobody could have scheduled. The defense in depth argument that justified building three witnesses is no longer an argument, it is an observation: the primary path was interrupted mid decision and the account was still locked five seconds later.

A DEFECT IN WHAT THE LOCK PERSISTED, found by reading the file rather than the log. `state_1200252169.dat`, 79 bytes, mtime 13:02:42, quoted whole: `AGSTATE|1|1200252169`, `L|1|1787101200|0`, `N|0.00000000|0.00000000`, `C|2087926071`. THE Q6 SNAPSHOT IS ZEROS AND THE BREACH TIMESTAMP IS ZERO. Cause, from source at `AccountGuardian.mq5:393`: `AgEnterLockFromBoot` calls `AgStateSetBreach(until, g_ag_state_breach_time, g_ag_state_limit_snap, g_ag_state_base_snap)`, passing the EXISTING model values, which is right when the FILE witness fires and an earlier snapshot must be preserved, and wrong when the DERIVED witness fires with no file at all, because the model is then default constructed and the derivation's own freshly computed limit and base are discarded rather than recorded. SEVERITY, both halves stated. Not an enforcement hole today: the file witness re-fires on reason plus an unexpired `locked_until` and never consults the snapshot, so the lock survives restarts, and tier 2 supplies the same 106.66 on this account. It IS a permanent loss of record: the breach's timestamp is gone, and `have_snapshot`, which tests `g_ag_state_limit_snap > 0.0`, will be false on every future boot for this lock, so the tier 1 protection Q6 exists to give is absent for every boot derived lock rather than for this one only. NOT FIXED, and deliberately: the build under acceptance is the one that just locked a live account, and the executor does not change it mid row.

RULING TWO IS NOT VERIFIED AND IS DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN AT THIS POINT. The ALERT text asserts that open positions stay open, and the journal cannot corroborate that, because it reports the guardian's decision and not the broker's book. Per the owner's operating constraints the executor stops here and waits for the owner to confirm the position state by hand. The 01:00 expiry row is likewise NOT marked done, per the same constraints, even though `locked_until` is now set and observable.

RULING TWO HOLDS, AND THE EXECUTOR IS NOT THE WITNESS FOR IT. Owner reported that thirteen XAUUSD sell positions of 0.5 lot each were open at 13:02, corrected from an earlier count of four, that the broker force closed all of them on margin, and that the guardian closed nothing. What the executor can corroborate from artifacts, read only: the terminal journal records thirteen closing buy deals of 0.5 XAUUSD.ecn, tickets `#279906117` through `#279906181`, ALL at price 4397.35 and all inside 65 milliseconds at 13:38:07, which is consistent with a forced liquidation; and the broker's own resynchronisation line after the later relaunch reads `terminal synchronized with Just Global Markets Ltd.: 0 positions, 0 orders`. WHAT THE ARTIFACTS DO NOT SAY IS ALSO RECORDED: a grep of the terminal journal for stop out, stopout and margin returns ZERO hits, so the CLOSE REASON is nowhere in any log. The broker's reason is the owner's account and is not corroborated. NO ORDER WAS ISSUED BY THE EA and that rests on three independent artifacts, the strongest first: the build contains zero trade API calls, re-verified this session by the tree wide grep that returns only the `Sweep.mqh` header comment; the EA log carries no order or trade line of any kind after the lock, only LIFE lines; and the thirteen deals carry no expert attribution in the terminal journal.

AN OBSERVABILITY GAP FOUND WHILE ANSWERING THE OWNER'S QUESTION, recorded because it cost real evidence today. LOCKED-state LIFE lines carry NO numbers field: they print state, seconds_in_state and waiting_on and nothing else. Through the entire liquidation of thirteen positions at 13:38:07 the guardian's log said `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry` and not one figure. The owner asked for balance and equity at that moment and the executor cannot supply either from any readable artifact, because no artifact carries them. That is not a defect in enforcement and it is a hole in the record exactly where the record matters most.

HOW A `DEAL_TYPE_BALANCE` DEAL IS TREATED, answered from source at the owner's request and quoted rather than summarised, because a deposit was contemplated. IN THE DAILY SUM IT IS EXCLUDED, `Pnl.mqh:109`, the F12 whitelist: `if(type != DEAL_TYPE_BUY && type != DEAL_TYPE_SELL) continue;`, so a balance deal contributes nothing to `cumulative` and nothing to `running_min`, and a deposit does not reduce the day's realized loss. IN THE DAY BASE IT IS NEUTRAL BY CONSTRUCTION, `AgDayBase` returning `AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_BALANCE) - all_deals` where `AgDealsSumAll` counts all deal types: a deposit raises Balance and raises the all deals sum by the same amount, so Base is unchanged, which is the Q2 identity the function's own comment states. CONSEQUENCE, stated as fact and with no recommendation either way, per the owner's instruction: base and limit are unmoved by a deposit, the day's realized loss stays fully counted, and the lock holds to its expiry regardless. One thing DOES change: at 13:02:42 the derived witness fired with `replay=0` because the loss was floating, and after the liquidation made it realized the same witness fires with `replay=1` from broker history alone, independently of the state file and the GV mirror.

P2-B CLOSES AND TWO OF THE THREE BOOT WITNESSES FIRED ON ONE BOOT. Owner killed and relaunched the terminal while locked. Quoted: `INFO|lock state file loaded|reason=DAILY_BREACH|locked_until=2026.08.19 01:00:00|weighed by the boot derivation at the SYNCING exit`, `TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s`, then at 13:46:44 `INFO|boot witness FILE fired|reason=DAILY_BREACH|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00` and `INFO|boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, then `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED` and LIFE lines at `state=LOCKED`. THE FILE WITNESS HAS NOW FIRED FOR THE FIRST TIME, joining the derived witness from 13:02:42, so two of the three paths the Stage 4 entry called unmeasurable are measured on artifacts. The `BOOT->SYNCING` label also settles a question this session raised: the `ACTIVE->SYNCING` lines seen earlier belong to the chart change path and not to a cold start, and a cold start does print `BOOT`.

THE `replay=1` FIRING WAS PREDICTED IN ADVANCE AND THEN MEASURED, which is why it is banked as a measurement and not as an expectation. Before P2-B ran, the executor stated that the derived witness would fire with `replay=1` this time, on the reasoning that the liquidation had converted a floating loss into a realized one and `running_min` folds realized deals only. The 13:02:42 firing had `live=1|replay=0|running_min=-7.00`; the 13:46:44 firing has `live=1|replay=1|running_min=-1893.50`. The owner later asked for this to be recorded as an expectation attached to the P2-B row; it is recorded as the stronger thing, a prediction made before the event and confirmed by the artifact after it. THE SIGNIFICANCE IS NOT THE FLAG BUT WHAT IT MAKES INDEPENDENT: with `replay=1` the lock is now derivable from broker deal history ALONE, so deleting both the state file and the GV mirror would not unlock the day.

THE KILL WAS NOT A KILL, AND THE EXECUTOR'S OWN PREDICTION IS WHAT CAUGHT IT. Before the row ran the executor predicted the crash-loop chain would advance past `1/3` and stated in advance that a reading of `1/3` would need explaining rather than shrugging at. It read `chain=1/3`. The halt file gives the reason: the killed session is recorded `S|1787058159|1`, MARKED CLEAN. Task Manager's End task sends a close message to a GUI application first, so MT5 shut down gracefully and `OnDeinit` ran. So P2-B proves the lock survives a RESTART and the crash path remains untested; a genuine test needs a forced termination. A SECOND RESULT FALLS OUT OF THE SAME EVIDENCE AND IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIRST: the journal has NO `deinit` line for that session, because the log buffer died with the process, while the halt file already carried the clean marker, having been flushed by the atomic write. The halt file's record survives a shutdown that the journal does not.

A FACTUAL CORRECTION AGAINST THE OWNER'S REPORT, derived from the log rather than asserted. The owner reported the demo balance as 0. The 13:46:44 witness line prints `tier=floor`, and that label is emitted only when `limit_cmp < live_limit`. Since `limit_cmp` is the ratchet floor at 106.6565, the live limit must EXCEED 106.6565, so `base > 2133.13`. With `base = ACCOUNT_BALANCE - all_deals` and the day's deals summing to `-1893.50` with no balance deals, ACCOUNT_BALANCE is above roughly 239.63. Had the balance truly been 0, base would be 1893.50, the live limit 94.68, and the line would have read `limit_cmp=94.68|tier=live`. It does not. So the liquidation left a residue. No exact figure is claimed here, only the bound the artifact forces, and the owner should read the terminal rather than take this number.

P2-C CLOSES, DISCONNECT AND RECONNECT WHILE LOCKED, and the executor's published prediction was WRONG ON ONE OF FIVE POINTS. The prediction was written and given to the owner BEFORE the row ran, which is the only reason the error is visible at all, and it is recorded here in full rather than quietly corrected. Correct: zero `RESYNC entered` and zero `RESYNC exited` lines; zero TRANSITION lines; zero ALERT lines; no state file write, `state_1200252169.dat` still at mtime 13:46:44 and md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19` throughout; the numbers field absent on every line, since `AgPnlNumbersString` returns empty whenever the state is not ACTIVE; and the server clock frozen while local advanced. WRONG: the executor predicted ZERO DEGRADED markers and there are ELEVEN, on LOCKED LIFE lines from 14:59:42 to 15:04:42.

WHY THE PREDICTION FAILED, stated as a reading error rather than as a surprise. The executor traced `g_ag_degraded` and `g_ag_resyncing`, found both set in exactly one place, the `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` guard at the head of `AgEvaluateActive`, correctly concluded that neither can be set while LOCKED, and stopped there. It missed `AgObservabilityNote()` at `AccountGuardian.mq5:500`, a Stage 6 addition that returns `"DEGRADED: disconnected"` from a SEPARATE global, `g_ag_obs_connected`, which the timer samples every tick independently of the state machine. The comment at lines 102 to 110 states that these globals are kept deliberately separate from `g_ag_degraded` "precisely so the two cannot be confused"; the executor read that comment and drew the opposite conclusion from it.

THE ERROR PROPAGATED INTO A WRONG FINDING BEFORE IT WAS CAUGHT, which is the part worth recording. On the strength of the wrong prediction the executor told the owner that a disconnect while locked would be "almost invisible" and that this WIDENED the LOCKED observability issue opened earlier this session. That was wrong and is withdrawn: Stage 6 covers connection state under a lock perfectly well. The observability issue stands for the NUMBERS and only the numbers, which is what it says, so no entry needs correcting. It is recorded because the wrong conclusion was one sentence away from being written into that issue.

WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY PROVES, and it is a design claim holding up rather than a null result. The two degraded channels are distinguishable in the artifact, exactly as the Stage 6 comment claims. ACTIVE emits `waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions`, from the Q10 gate at line 532, as seen at 12:09. LOCKED emits `waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until` plus a separate `DEGRADED: disconnected` field, from the Stage 6 note at line 502, as seen now. Different strings from different sites, so a reader can always tell which fired. The absence of RESYNC under a lock is likewise correct rather than missing: the gate exists to hold the first post-disconnect BREACH DECISION until history is stable, and a locked guardian takes no breach decisions. Window measured from the frozen clock: 14:58:54 to 15:05:12, six minutes eighteen seconds. THE ONE PASS LAG APPEARS FOR THE THIRD TIME: at 14:59:12 the server stamp had already frozen at 14:58:54 while no marker was present yet, the same shape as 12:09:03 and for the same ruled reason.

A GAP DERIVED FROM THIS ROW AND EXPLICITLY NOT OBSERVED, recorded as a reading rather than as a finding, and deliberately NOT opened as an ISSUES row without the owner. Because `g_ag_resyncing` is set only inside `AgEvaluateActive`, a disconnect that begins AND ends inside a locked window arms no resync gate at all. Lock expiry then runs `AgTransition(AG_STATE_ACTIVE, "lock expired", "")`, entering ACTIVE directly rather than through SYNCING, and the coherence gate is `if(g_ag_resyncing)`, so with the flag false the first post expiry pass performs no stability check. For this to matter the disconnect must STRADDLE the expiry boundary: reconnect after `locked_until`, the clock jumps past it, expiry fires within a tick or two, and ACTIVE is entered while history may still be synchronising. That is the nightly break and weekend freeze shape this ledger already documents at length. It did not happen tonight and could not have: the reconnect was 15:05 and expiry is 01:00, about ten hours apart.

P2-H DID NOT CLOSE, AND THE REASON WAS PUBLISHED BEFORE THE ROW RAN. Owner raised `DailyLossPercent` from 5 to 10 while locked. There are ZERO `input changed while LOCKED` lines in the journal. The row's claim, that a limit raised while locked produces a loud WARN while the snapshot holds, is therefore UNTESTED rather than passed or failed. The cause is the one the executor put in writing before the owner touched the input: MT5 reloads the EA on an input change, `INFO|deinit|reason=5`, and `AgEnterLockFromBoot` reseeds the Q6 input witness from the LIVE inputs at `AccountGuardian.mq5:386` to `:388`, so the witness captured percent 10 as the locked-in value and now agrees with the input. Nothing remains to warn about. The executor's other published prediction for this row, that the WARN would read `limit=0.00 base=0.00` and expose the empty snapshot in operator-facing text, is likewise untested since the WARN never fired. WHAT THE ROW DID PROVE, and it is not nothing: the lock held across the input change without a flicker, `locked_until` unchanged at 2026-08-19 01:00:00, the state file rewritten byte identical at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19`, no transition out of LOCKED, and LIFE lines resuming at `state=LOCKED` with `seconds_in_state` restarting from 15, which is the reload and not a state change. HOW TO ACTUALLY TEST IT, named and not performed: the WARN needs a LOCKED evaluation pass that observes an input differing from the captured witness, which an input change through the dialog can never produce because the reload always reseeds first. That is a row that may not be reachable through the terminal UI at all, and it is left for the owner rather than worked around. OWNER INSTRUCTED 2026-08-18: leave the row UNTESTED and do not manufacture a substitute test, since a substitute would prove something else while carrying this row's name.

THE INPUT CHANGE WAS ERASED A SECOND TIME, BY THE CRASH, AND NO ARTIFACT ANYWHERE NOW RECORDS THAT IT HAPPENED. Owner observed after the forced kills that `DailyLossPercent` reads 5 on the chart, having set it to 10 at 15:36 and never restored it, and asked what the EA actually booted with. ANSWERED FROM ARTIFACTS, with the negative results stated first. B1, the 16:00:46 boot, left ZERO journal lines, so nothing whatever can be said about its inputs. B2, the 16:01:24 boot, is no better served directly: the EA logs no input value at any point, verified by grep over `AccountGuardian.mq5`, where `DailyLossPercent` appears only as the input declaration at line 19 and inside arithmetic and comparisons. The current LIFE lines imply nothing either, since LOCKED lines carry no numbers, which is the second time in one session that the observability gap has blocked a direct answer. AND THE OBVIOUS CHECK FAILS: the `tier=floor` label cannot discriminate, because with base above 2133.13 both five percent, about 106.7, and ten percent, about 213, exceed the 106.6565 floor, so both print `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor` identically.
THE DECISIVE ARTIFACT IS THE CHART STATE FILE, read only: `MQL5\Profiles\Charts\Default\chart01.chr`, mtime 2026-08-18 13:46:03, containing under its `<expert>` block `DailyLossPercent=5.0` and `DailyLossCurrency=0.0`. That mtime PREDATES the 15:36 input change by nearly two hours. MT5 persists chart state, EA inputs included, on clean shutdown, and `taskkill /F` denied it that chance on both kills, so the file still holds the pre change value and the relaunch restored 5 from it. Corroborated independently by the Q6 witness itself: `AgEnterLockFromBoot` captures the live input at boot and `AgEvaluateLocked` warns on any divergence, and with ZERO `input changed while LOCKED` lines all day the live input has equalled the boot captured value continuously since 16:01:24, which with a chart reading 5 means it booted at 5. Restored from the PROFILE's chart state file, not from a template; `Default.tpl` is dated 2026-08-03 and is not implicated.
SO THE OPERATOR ACTION WAS ERASED TWICE OVER, by two independent mechanisms, and this is the finding rather than the input value. Once from the guardian's own witness, by the reseed on reload at 15:36. Once from the terminal's persisted chart state, by a process kill that prevented the save. There is now NO artifact anywhere, in the journal, in the state file, or in the chart, recording that `DailyLossPercent` was ever set to 10 on a locked account. It survives only in the owner's memory and in this ledger. Enforcement never moved and was never at risk; what was lost is the record.

THE CONSEQUENCE THE OWNER ASKED TO BE RECORDED ON THIS ROW, and it is larger than the row itself. THE RESEED ON RELOAD ERASES THE RECORD OF AN INPUT CHANGE MADE WHILE LOCKED. `AgEnterLockFromBoot` sets `g_ag_locked_in_percent` and `g_ag_locked_in_currency` from the LIVE inputs on every boot, so after any reload the witness matches whatever the inputs now say, and the guardian can no longer tell that they ever differed. An operator who raises a limit during a locked window leaves NO trace of having done so in the journal: no WARN at the time, because the reload preempts the first comparing pass, and none afterwards, because the witness has been re-seeded to agree. Today's run is the demonstration: `DailyLossPercent` went 5 to 10 at 15:36 on a locked account and the journal contains zero `input changed while LOCKED` lines. Enforcement did not move and that is not the point; the audit trail did not record an operator action on a locked account. OWNER INSTRUCTED 2026-08-18: the reseed question JOINS THE NEXT BUILD AS DESIGN ITEM 5, classed as a DESIGN ITEM AND NOT AS A DEFECT. That classification is the owner's and is recorded as given: the four items before it are defects with a wrong behaviour to correct, while this one is a deliberate behaviour whose correctness is open. WHETHER THE RESEED IS CORRECT BEHAVIOUR IS A QUESTION FOR THE NEXT BUILD AND IS NOT RULED HERE. The argument for it is on the record already, in the Stage 3 note the reseed was written to answer: without it the first LOCKED pass compares live inputs against a default-constructed 0.0 and warns about a change nobody made. The argument against it is this paragraph. Both are recorded and neither is decided.

ALL THREE BOOT WITNESSES HAVE NOW FIRED, AND THE STAGE 4 ENTRY THAT WAITED FIVE STAGES FOR THIS IS ANSWERED. On the 15:36:27 boot, in one block: `boot witness FILE fired|reason=DAILY_BREACH|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, `boot witness GV fired|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, and `boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`. The GV path had never fired before today. Counting the whole day: DERIVED fired at 13:02:42 with `replay=0` and again at 13:46:44 and 15:36:27 with `replay=1`, FILE fired at 13:46:44 and 15:36:27, GV fired ONCE, at 15:36:27 only. THE SINGLE GV FIRING IS NOT A COINCIDENCE AND IS THE SUBJECT OF ITS OWN ISSUES ROW opened this session: the mirror is overwritten with zero by `OnTimer` on every cold boot before the witness reads it, so it can only fire on a reload that preserved memory, which is what 15:36:26 was and what 13:46:41 was not.

THE CRASH PATH CLOSES ON TWO FORCED KILLS, and the executor's published table was right on every row it named and incomplete on one it did not. Owner ran `taskkill /F /IM terminal64.exe` twice, relaunching after each, with the two inits deliberately placed inside the 300 second window so the crash-loop chain could advance. THE HALT FILE IS THE PROOF THE KILLS WERE HARD and it is quoted whole at the tail: `S|1787060801|1`, the 13:46:41 session that Task Manager's End task shut down gracefully, then `S|1787067386|0`, `S|1787068846|0`, `S|1787068884|0`, three consecutive UNCLEAN entries. The contrast between the `|1` and the three `|0` in one file is the discriminator the P2-B entry said was needed and could not supply.

B2 VERIFIED AGAINST THE PREDICTION, LINE BY LINE: `DEBUG|crash-loop check|chain=2/3 consecutive unclean, gap bound 300s`, the chain advancing for the first time in the project's history; `TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING`, a true cold start; `boot witness FILE fired`; `boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor`; `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED`; one ALERT; and `state_1200252169.dat` rewritten byte identical at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19` for the fourth boot running. `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor` was predicted specifically and holds even though `DailyLossPercent` is now 10: the live limit is roughly 213 and the ratchet floor at 106.6565 is still the stricter value, so the floor governs.

THE GV WITNESS DID NOT FIRE ON THIS COLD BOOT EITHER, WHICH IS THE SECOND INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION AND TURNS THE DEFECT FROM AN OBSERVATION INTO A PATTERN. Count for the whole day: `boot witness GV fired` appears exactly ONCE, at 15:36:27, the parameter reload that preserved memory. It is absent from the 13:46:41 cold boot and absent from the 16:01:24 cold boot, both of which reached LOCKED on FILE and DERIVED alone. Two cold boots, two non firings, one warm reload, one firing: the self erase mechanism recorded in its own ISSUES row predicts exactly that split and nothing else explains it.

A RESULT NEITHER THE OWNER NOR THE EXECUTOR PUT ON THE LIST, and it is a clean pass on a path that had never run live. `AG|2026.08.18 16:01:24|WARN|stale mutex heartbeat (11s), taking over crashed-instance mutex`. The killed instance died holding the mutex and never released it, and the F8 stale heartbeat takeover did precisely what it exists for, measured at an 11 second staleness against the 10 second threshold. One occurrence in the whole day's log, and it took a genuine process kill to produce it; every earlier restart this session was graceful enough to release the mutex cleanly.

B1 LEFT NO JOURNAL LINES AT ALL, AND THAT CORRECTS SOMETHING THE EXECUTOR WROTE EARLIER THIS SESSION. There are ZERO EA lines between 16:00:00 and 16:01:20; the log runs from `15:59:42` straight to the B2 init. B1 lived 38 seconds and lost its entire boot block, `chain` line included. Before the flush the executor told the owner that a hard kill "loses only seconds of journal, not minutes" and that this SOFTENED the P2-B finding about the halt file outlasting the journal. THAT WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN: it generalised from the 15:36:26 session, which had lived 23 minutes and had therefore already flushed, to a case with no flush at all. The P2-B finding stands undiminished. B1 IS THEREFORE ATTESTED BY TWO ARTIFACTS AND ZERO LOG LINES: its halt file entry `S|1787068846|0`, and B2's own `chain=2/3`, which could only reach 2 by walking back onto an unclean B1 entry inside the 300 second window. The chain arithmetic is itself evidence of the session that left no trace. B1's own `chain=1/3` line is UNVERIFIABLE and is recorded as such rather than assumed from the arithmetic that says it must have read that way.

THE 2133 DEPOSIT IS INVISIBLE IN EVERY ARTIFACT THE EXECUTOR CAN READ, AND THAT IS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AT ONCE, ONE A GAP AND ONE THE DESIGN WORKING. Owner deposited 2133 on the demo at about 16:52 while the account was LOCKED, having asked in advance to be told what would be visible. THE LIFE LINES DID NOT MOVE, as predicted before the deposit: sixteen lines from 16:44:25 to 16:51:55 are identical in every field except `seconds_in_state` and the two clocks, `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until`, with no numbers block at all. The state file stayed at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19`, the halt file at `18A82E7E498B038D7A80295B62D4985F`, the floor file untouched since 02:00:00. NO CORROBORATION EXISTS: the terminal journal ends at 16:01:24 and records no balance deal, so the deposit rests on the owner's report alone and the executor states that rather than implying otherwise.
THE TWO CAUSES MUST NOT BE CONFLATED AND ARE SEPARATED HERE. That the LIFE lines carry no numbers under a lock is the OPEN observability gap, and this is the fourth time in one session it has blocked a direct reading. That the deposit will never appear in the guardian's numbers, not even after expiry returns it to ACTIVE, is the DESIGN WORKING AS RULED and is not a gap at all: the F12 whitelist excludes balance deals from realized, and the Q2 day base identity makes them neutral, verified on tonight's own figures, `base = B + 1893.50` before and `base = (B + 2133) - 239.50 = B + 1893.50` after. The only quantity a deposit moves is `ACCOUNT_BALANCE`, which the guardian logs in no state. So the guardian is BUILT blind to deposits WITHIN THE DAY THEY LAND, deliberately.
CORRECTED 2026-08-19 AT THE EXPIRY, AND THE ORIGINAL WORDING WAS WRONG. This paragraph first said that `base` and `limit` after 01:00 "should read as though nothing was deposited". THAT IS FALSE AND IS WITHDRAWN. The Q2 neutrality holds only inside the day the deposit lands, because `all_deals` counts it there and cancels it out of the identity. AFTER THE ROLLOVER THE NEW ANCHOR EXCLUDES YESTERDAY'S DEALS ENTIRELY, so `all_deals` returns to about zero and `base` collapses to `ACCOUNT_BALANCE` itself, deposit included. Measured at 01:00:31: `base=2133.00`, which IS the account balance and not a coincidence. So a deposit is invisible for the remainder of its own day and fully visible from the next anchor onward. The executor wrote the wrong half of that before the event and is recording the correction here rather than editing the claim away.

2026-08-18, PHASE 2 STAGES 2 THROUGH 6 MERGED TO MAIN, merge commit `3ad7ef4`, parents `06d92ee` and `35628f6`, eleven branch commits carried, nine files changed, 2117 insertions and 28 deletions. 2026-08-18, THE FULL VECTOR SET RUNS CLEAN, `AGVEC|SUMMARY|76/76`, verified from the terminal's own journal per the FINAL of 2026-08-05. THE EVIDENCE, QUOTED. ALL TWENTY NEW RATCHET VECTORS PASS, counted as twenty and named in full because each carries part of question SEVEN: `j1_seed_takes_the_live_limit`, `j1b_seed_stores_the_floor`, `j1c_seed_stores_the_day_anchor`, `j2_lowers_on_a_decrease`, `j2b_enforces_the_lowered_value`, `j3_holds_the_floor_against_a_raised_limit`, `j3b_enforces_the_floor_not_the_raised_limit`, `j4_reseeds_at_rollover`, `j4b_reseed_moves_the_day_anchor`, `j4c_reseed_enforces_the_new_live_limit`, `j5_no_reseed_on_a_backward_step`, `j5b_backward_step_does_not_move_the_anchor`, `j5c_backward_step_still_enforces_the_held_floor`, `j6_floor_file_reloads`, `j6b_floor_survives_a_restart`, `j7_stale_floor_is_declined`, `j7b_same_day_floor_is_applied`, `j8_corrupt_floor_is_quarantined`, `j8b_corrupt_floor_resets_to_nothing`, `j8c_corrupt_floor_file_was_not_deleted`. THE RATCHET'S OWN LINES ARE THE RUNTIME WITNESS and they read exactly as ratified. `ratchet seeded|anchor=2026.02.10 01:00:00|floor=100.00`. `ratchet HOLDING against a raised limit (question SEVEN): live=150.00 floor=80.00; the floor is enforced until the next day anchor`, which is the inflation defence firing on a real pass. `ratchet reseeded at rollover|old_anchor=2026.02.10 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.02.11 01:00:00|old_floor=80.00|new_floor=150.00`. And `floor file failed checksum or does not parse, quarantined as AccountGuardian\floor_990000011.dat.bad, the ratchet reseeds from the live limit on the next completed pass (no lock follows: the floor is not lock state)`, which measures the deliberate difference from the state file's corruption policy. Recorded because its absence would otherwise read as a defect: the backward step case emitted NO WARN, because the cadence cap had just fired on the HOLD line milliseconds earlier, and `j5` and its two companions passed on the state assertions regardless. The cap working is the correct outcome, not a missing line.

A MEASUREMENT TRAP, THIRD VARIANT OF THE SAME FAMILY, AND THIS ONE NEARLY PRODUCED A FALSE ACCUSATION. The first read of `20260818.log` returned 11356 bytes, 21 lines and ZERO AGVEC lines, and the directory entry agreed at 11356 with a last write time of 00:09:41. The run is stamped 00:09:57, SIXTEEN SECONDS LATER. The read had raced the event. A second read minutes later returned 36682 bytes and the complete run. Had the executor stopped at the first read it would have reported that no 76/76 run exists, which is a direct contradiction of the owner's report and the most damaging kind of wrong answer this ledger can produce, since the evidence rule exists to catch fabrication and would here have manufactured an accusation instead. What prevented it was not care but procedure: the next check was of the deployed binary, which was already the 76 check build at md5 221AAAD649DEE151443485F493E463E8, and a binary in place with no output demanded an explanation rather than a conclusion. THE RULE THIS YIELDS, alongside the stale directory entry of 2026-08-16 and the growing stream length of this date: A NEGATIVE RESULT FROM A LIVE LOG IS NEVER EVIDENCE OF ABSENCE. It is only ever evidence about the moment of the read. Absence counts only after the writer is known to be finished, or after a second read at a later time agrees.

2026-08-18, PHASE 2 STAGES 5 AND 6 IMPLEMENTED, THE RATCHET AND THE OBSERVABILITY PASS, commit `0db69a0`, three files, 493 insertions and 7 deletions. STAGE 5, THE RATCHET, exactly the five ratified elements and no more. Single scalar `floor_currency` persisted as `floor_<login>.dat` through `AgAtomicWrite`, carrying the same never loaded guard and the same free name quarantine the state file family uses. It SEEDS on the first completed ACTIVE computation, LOWERS on a decrease as a running minimum, HOLDS on an increase with a cadence capped WARN naming both the live value and the floor, RESEEDS AT ROLLOVER, and does NOT RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP. The ratcheted quantity is the DERIVED `limit_currency` and never the raw inputs, which is the correction the 2026-07-30 addendum made and the ruling ratified: under Q8's min of enabled a leg that is not binding can be disabled with zero effect on the enforced limit, and an input level ratchet blocks that as a false positive on the owner's own worked case.

`min(limit, floor)` IS PRE BREACH ONLY and the code makes that structural rather than careful. It is applied in the live comparison, called from the breach tail and from nowhere else, and as TIER 2 of the boot cascade in the order the instruction named, snapshot then floor then live. Q6's snapshot governs the locked window unconditionally and the floor never touches it, the two being disjoint by construction since the ratchet governs pre breach ACTIVE while the snapshot governs post breach LOCKED. ONE CONSEQUENCE WORTH STATING because getting it wrong would be silent: the snapshot taken at breach is the ENFORCED limit, not the raw live one, so it is automatically the floor at that instant exactly as the 2026-07-30 addendum predicted, and snapshotting the live value instead would have loosened the locked window by precisely the amount the ratchet had been holding back.

TWO DESIGN CALLS INSIDE STAGE 5 THAT THE RULING DID NOT SPELL OUT, both recorded rather than left in the code to be discovered. FIRST, a backward step HOLDS the floor and KEEPS ENFORCING IT. The alternative, treating a floor whose anchor is later than the window as stale, would let one clock rewind drop the floor in a single act, which is the same one step disarm the negative gap ruling of 2026-08-04 refused in the crash loop chain; holding is also the strict direction. SECOND, a corrupt floor file is quarantined and reset but LOCKS NOTHING, unlike a corrupt state file which locks via CORRUPT_STATE. The floor is a pre breach tightening and not lock state, so losing it errs toward the live limit, and the residual that follows, that destroying the floor file resets it on the next seed, is the one the 2026-07-30 ruling already accepted on the record when it ordered the ratchet built.

`AgRatchetUpdate` was placed in `Persist.mqh` beside the floor family rather than in the EA. That is the owner's ruling C of this date applied to a second case rather than a new decision: a script cannot include an EA, so while the function sat there the six vectors question SEVEN calls for could not be written at all. The WARN cadence arrives as a parameter rather than by relocating the EA's `AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, which keeps Phase 1's structure untouched.

STAGE 6, OBSERVABILITY, ALL THREE ITEMS AND ALL OF IT LOGGING. RESYNC ENTRY AND EXIT INFO LINES, edge triggered on the flag the Q10 reconnect coherence gate already maintains, emitted from the timer rather than from inside the gate, which keeps the evaluation path untouched and is also the better instrument: the RESYNC window is about three seconds against a thirty second LIFE cadence, which is why five live disconnects produced zero observations and why the row could only ever close by luck, and an event line is not sampled. DEGRADED MARKER ON SYNCING AND LOCKED LIFE LINES while `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` is false, closing the measured 2026-08-14 gap where seven SYNCING lines emitted entirely offline were indistinguishable from healthy ones to any reader; ACTIVE already carries Q10's own marker and is deliberately not double marked. QUOTE AGE NOTE when the tick age exceeds the ratified 120 seconds, with `SymbolInfoSessionQuote` drawing the market closed distinction so the marker does not fire across every weekend and every nightly break until nobody reads it, and with a symbol that reports no quote session at all treated as OPEN and never as closed, since claiming market closed without evidence would explain away a genuinely frozen feed.

THE RULINGS APPLIED. STAGE 4, THE BOOT DERIVATION. `AgBootDerivation` runs at the SYNCING exit and ORs three witnesses with STRICTEST WINNING on duration. FILE witness fires on `locked_until > now` rather than on the reason field, so an expired lock's stale record reads as not-locked and needs no clear-on-unlock rewrite. GV witness carries a bare timestamp and therefore defaults to DAILY_BREACH, a GV having no CORRUPT_STATE concept to express. DERIVED witness replays broker history and is the AUTHORITY, precisely because a local machine attacker cannot forge or delete server side history while the file and the GV are both locally writable and are therefore accelerators rather than authority. CORRUPT_STATE takes the reason on a tie, because a DAILY_BREACH label would hide the one thing that finding says, that the record cannot be trusted. THE OR ONLY ADDS A LOCK: no witness unlocks, unlock remains exclusively the `TimeCurrent >= locked_until` comparison, and an unevaluable replay returns a third code that holds the EA in SYNCING rather than licensing an unlock, which is the F6 obligation made structural rather than advisory.

ONE SHARED REPLAY, which is what the instruction asked for and is worth stating precisely. `AgRealizedFold` folds the whitelisted deals once and returns BOTH the realized sum and the running minimum, so the F12 whitelist and the Q3 per deal formula exist in exactly one place and cannot drift between the live path and the replay. `AgRealized` is now that same fold with the minimum discarded. The refactor is behaviour preserving BY CONSTRUCTION and the argument is stated rather than assumed: sorting cannot change a sum, so every Phase 1 figure this function has produced is reproduced exactly. The fold sorts `(DEAL_TIME, DEAL_TICKET)` ascending per design item 1's tiebreak, and the reason it must is that a running minimum is order sensitive where a sum is not, MQL5 does not document `HistoryDealGetTicket`'s order, and an intra second dip is exactly the case where ordering decides whether the dip is visible at all. The insertion sort is deliberate and bounded: this runs at boot only, never per tick, which is the design's own scope note about not replaying the whole history every second.

THE GV MIRROR is now written from memory on every tick beside the mutex heartbeat, unconditionally, which is what makes it self healing: a GV cleared through the terminal's own Global Variables window while the EA is alive is restored within one tick. The state FILE is deliberately NOT rewritten per tick and stays event triggered, at breach, at expiry and at corruption handling, matching the halt file's pattern.

VECTORS EXTENDED BY 14 TO 56, covering exactly what ruling C unblocked and nothing more: Q1's base, ruling THREE's extra day including the measured 00:58 pre anchor case where the unfrozen path locks for 120 seconds and the frozen path locks for more than a day, ruling FOUR's floor and its no-op when the computed value already clears it, the witness path's clamp, and the rewound clock geometry where the latch floor sits ABOVE the clamp ceiling and must win. That last case is constructed by moving the latch rather than the clock, which reproduces the exact geometry deterministically without needing a machine whose clock has been stepped backward. Vector `i14` asserts the domain split head on by proving the computed path is never clamped, which is the one way the two domains could silently collapse into each other.

2026-08-18, PHASE 2 STAGE 3 IMPLEMENTED, LOCK SEMANTICS, commit `7621ce7`, one file, 267 insertions and 16 deletions. RULE A held, nothing under the Terminal data folder was written; the only terminal contact this stage was the read-only journal verification recorded in the entry above. Stage 4 is authorized and NOT started, per the instruction to stop after each stage.

WHAT CHANGED. The breach tail of `AgEvaluateActive` no longer shouts and stays ACTIVE; it declares a lock. `AgDeclareLock` snapshots limit and base into the state file per Q6, computes `locked_until`, transitions to LOCKED and raises an ALERT whose text says in terms that no order is sent and open positions stay open, so the operator is not left expecting a flatten that ruling TWO explicitly defers to Phase 3. The LOCKED case in `OnTimer`, an empty block since Phase 1, now calls `AgEvaluateLocked`.

THE BOUNDS, all three rulings of this date in one function so the composition order is stated once and cannot drift. Q1 is the base, next day anchor from the breach instant. RULING THREE takes the anchor AFTER the imminent one when the quote is frozen at the moment of breach, a full trading day, which is what stops the measured 62 minute pre anchor freeze of 2026-08-13 producing a lock that expires minutes later at the reopen. RULING FOUR floors the result at `AgNextDayAnchor` of Q8's high water latch, which never recedes, so a breach declared after a backward clock step cannot compute an already past expiry. THE 2026-07-30 CLAMP IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT from the computed path and present in the witness path, exactly as the precedence ruling of this date directs by giving each bound a domain; `AgLockedUntilFromWitness` applies clamp first as the upper bound and floor last as the lower bound, which is the mechanism by which the floor wins under a rewound clock. That witness helper is written now and is NOT REACHED in this stage, since no witness is read until Stage 4; it sits beside the computed one so the two domains cannot be re-derived differently later.

EXPIRY IS THE ONLY UNLOCK PATH and compares `TimeCurrent` per Q7. Nothing else leaves LOCKED: no input, no absence of evidence, no reconnect. A frozen or backward clock therefore delays an unlock, which errs locked and needs no special case, and the Friday breach that outlives its computed expiry by about 48 hours across the weekend freeze is that behaviour working rather than a defect.

THE FROZEN QUOTE MEASURE, and where it sits is a deliberate choice worth recording. It is a purely local tick age delta, never clock arithmetic, so it inherits nothing from the broker DST peg this ledger still has open until the October harvest. It lives in `OnTimer` and not inside `AgEvaluateActive` for two reasons: it must keep measuring in EVERY state including LOCKED, since ruling THREE reads it at a breach that may follow any state history, and putting it in the evaluation path would have broken the byte identical requirement for no gain. IT GATES NOTHING. The breach is declared from the frozen quote exactly as the stale quote ruling of this date requires, and the measure is read only afterwards to size the consequence, which is the boundary ruling THREE itself draws.

A DEFECT IN THIS STAGE, FOUND BY THE EXECUTOR BEFORE COMMIT AND FIXED, recorded because it would have been invisible until the first live breach. `g_ag_state_loaded` is set only by `AgStateLoad`, and Stage 3 as first written never called it, so `AgStateSave` would have refused on every breach under the never-loaded-never-written rule and the lock would have held in memory only, silently failing to persist. `AgStateLoad` is now called in `OnInit` and its result is DELIBERATELY NOT ACTED ON, which is the smallest correct fix: loading is what makes a later write legitimate, while acting on what the file says is Stage 4's boot derivation. A second defect was fixed the same way: the input change witness compared live inputs against values a fresh image has never set, so a Stage 4 boot restoring a lock from the file would have emitted a change WARN naming a change nobody made. It is now guarded by an explicit captured flag rather than by an accident of initialization.

WHAT WAS BUILT. `Persist.mqh` gains the `state_<login>.dat` family per design doc item 5, reusing `AgAtomicWrite` and `AgChecksum` unchanged: `AgStatePath`, `AgStateSerialize`, `AgStateSave`, `AgStateLoad`, `AgStateResetModel`, `AgStateSetBreach`, `AgStateSetCorrupt`, `AgStateQuarantine`, `AgStateQuarantinePath`, and the model globals including `g_ag_state_loaded`. Magic is `AGSTATE`, deliberately distinct from `AGHALT` so the two formats cannot cross-read. TWO DELIBERATE DEPARTURES, both recorded because a later session must not read either as an accident. ONE, quarantine picks a FREE name rather than the design document's plain `.bad`. The document says to reuse the halt file's `FileMove` pattern; that pattern uses `FILE_REWRITE`, so a second corruption would overwrite the first quarantine, which the FINAL of 2026-07-29 forbids in terms, lock artifacts are never deleted, only quarantined. The FINAL outranks the document's convenience wording, so `.bad`, `.bad.2`, `.bad.3` accumulate and a vector asserts it. The halt file's own single-name path is untouched, being out of Stage 2's scope and not a lock artifact. TWO, money fields are stored at 8 decimals, not the cent. Base 1985.97 at the ruled five percent is 99.2985, which the banner prints as 99.30, and Q6 makes the snapshot the enforced limit, so storing the printed value would change enforcement on every restart. The 0.01 epsilon of 2026-07-30 is a comparison rule and not a storage format; a vector asserts the stored limit is NOT 99.30.

THE MEASUREMENT OF THE SESSION, AND IT CORRECTS THIS LEDGER: A WORKTREE COMPILE IS POSSIBLE AFTER ALL, WITH NO DEPLOY AND NO TERMINAL WRITE. The 2026-08-09 implementation entry records that MetaEditor's include resolution rooted against the deployed terminal tree and that no compile log could exist for that branch, and the Stage 5 deploy entry later explained the error 106 as "a consequence of compiling a file outside any MQL5 root". That explanation is INCOMPLETE, measured today in both directions. Compiling `AgPhase2StateVectors.mq5` from inside the worktree's own complete MQL5 root still resolved every include to `C:\Users\www83\AppData\Roaming\MetaQuotes\Terminal\D0E8209F77C8CF37AD8BF550E51FF075\MQL5\Include\AccountGuardian\`, the terminal data folder, and produced `Result: 101 errors, 12 warnings`, every error an undeclared identifier naming a Stage 2 symbol, because it had compiled the new script against the DEPLOYED Phase 1 `Persist.mqh` of 2026-08-04. So the include root follows MetaEditor's configured data folder and not the compiled file's tree. Adding `/inc:` pointing at the worktree's own `MQL5` directory fixes it completely: the log then names the worktree's own include paths and both compiles pass. This removes a limitation Phase 1 carried from Stage 2 to Stage 5 and it means every future Phase 2 stage can bank a real compile result before any deploy exists.

2026-08-18, THE UNPUSHED LOCAL HISTORY IS REWRITTEN SO `CLAUDE.md` NEVER ENTERED IT, route one of the three named in the ISSUES entry this closes, authorized by the owner. HASH MAPPING, and it is the first thing recorded because every earlier entry of this date cites the old hashes and those objects no longer exist: `b4e5da2` became `9f494bc`, `476d42b` became `22f1a7a`, and `4e42fce` and everything below it are untouched. Any reference to `b4e5da2` or `476d42b` anywhere above in this ledger, including inside the FINAL reversal entry in DECISIONS, now names a commit that cannot be looked up; the mapping here is the only way to resolve them and the entries themselves are left as written, since ACTIONS is append only and DECISIONS is locked. METHOD, stated because it is not what the instruction literally named: `git rebase -i` is unavailable in this environment, so the equivalent was done with `git filter-branch --index-filter 'git rm --cached --ignore-unmatch CLAUDE.md'` over the range `4e42fce..HEAD`, which drops the file from every tree in the range rather than editing one commit by hand. Both commits survive as commits and neither went empty, since each still carries its `.gitignore` and `LEDGER.md` changes. VERIFIED against baselines captured before the rewrite, not by eye: `LEDGER.md` at the new HEAD is byte identical to the old HEAD, sha256 `75992de7d7fc7d12fc02b0cda028b9b75ff072bcc4bd301e1341a1a1b56ef1b2`, 952 lines; `.gitignore` is byte identical, sha256 `c9c0f902405f1295fa1de5da2c20d782d2eaf515d0113e1bcbf732c446c0cf1f`, 37 lines with `# ccode-managed` first; `git log --all -- CLAUDE.md` returns nothing; and the working copy of `CLAUDE.md` is untouched at sha256 `dfc7329cc9288d9f9636da04d2488f5f67f4266786f3efdfaff15d6eac9e1772`, 4548 bytes, with its original mtime. THE PURGE WAS A SECOND STEP AND WITHOUT IT THE REWRITE WOULD HAVE BEEN COSMETIC: `filter-branch` keeps the original history at `refs/original/refs/heads/main`, which `git log --all` still reads, so that ref was deleted, all reflogs were expired with `--expire-unreachable=now`, and `git gc --prune=now` was run. Confirmed by object lookup rather than by log output: `git cat-file -e` on both old commits now fails with `Not a valid object name`, so the blob carrying the protocol text is gone from this repository rather than merely unreferenced. COST, recorded so it is not discovered later as a surprise: expiring all reflogs removed the undo history for every branch in this repository, not just for this rewrite. No work was lost, since every other branch is still reachable through its own ref, but the safety net that would let a future session recover an accidental reset is empty and will refill only as new operations happen. Nothing was pushed, and no force push was performed or attempted.

2026-08-18, `CLAUDE.md` IS UNTRACKED AGAIN, REVERSING THE TRACKING CHANGE MADE EARLIER THE SAME DAY. The owner unlocked the FINAL tracking entry with the explicit `unlock decision` phrase and the entry name, which is the only route protocol rule 5 allows, and the reversal is now FINAL in DECISIONS above the entry it reverses, with that entry left intact. What was done: `!CLAUDE.md` removed from `.gitignore`, the `# ccode-managed` sentinel left as the first line, and `git rm --cached CLAUDE.md`. Verified rather than assumed, since `git rm` is the one step here that could have destroyed the file: the working copy is byte identical across the change, sha256 `dfc7329cc9288d9f9636da04d2488f5f67f4266786f3efdfaff15d6eac9e1772` before and after, 4548 bytes, mtime unchanged, and `git check-ignore` now matches it against `*.md` again with no negation. RECOVERABILITY, which is the whole reason the tracking ruling existed and is now recorded so no future session reopens the question on that ground: the owner's profile copies `CLAUDE.md` to `~/.claude/backups` on every launch, so the file survives an overwrite or a lost working copy without git holding it. FLAGGED, not acted on, and it is the one loose end this reversal does not close by itself: `CLAUDE.md` remains in this repository's history at commit `b4e5da2`, where it was first added, and `git rm --cached` removes it from the index and from future commits only. Nothing has been pushed, so the file is not public yet and the reversal is fully effective as long as that stays true, but a push of the current history would publish it despite this ruling. Two ways out exist, dropping or rewriting that commit, and both rewrite history, so neither was performed on the executor's own initiative. Owner's call.

Investigation banked with this entry, and the reuse surface below is RE DERIVED BY GREP AND READ THIS SESSION rather than quoted from the kickoff narrative, because the 2026-08-05 rule binds an entry to what its own session measured. `AgAtomicWrite(const string path, const string content)` is at Persist.mqh:67 and its single existing caller is Persist.mqh:107, `return AgAtomicWrite(AgHaltPath(), AgHaltSerialize());`, which is the halt file path the state file will reuse unchanged. `AgEvaluateActive()` opens at AccountGuardian.mq5:118 and its Q10 connection gate is the third line of it, AccountGuardian.mq5:121, `if(!(bool)TerminalInfoInteger(TERMINAL_CONNECTED))`, with the same guard repeated at Pnl.mqh:178. The dispatch tail reads exactly as the Phase 1 close described it: the SAFE_HALT early return sits at AccountGuardian.mq5:405 to 406 with the comment "closes nothing, sweeps nothing, no expiry", ACTIVE dispatches to `AgEvaluateActive()` at :422, and the LOCKED case at :423 to :426 is an empty block whose only content is the comment "Phase 2 adds expiry and lock-witness reconciliation here." So the branch Phase 2 fills is present, empty, and positioned after the halt return and beside the SYNCING and ACTIVE branches rather than inside them. `AgNextDayAnchor` is a one line function at Clock.mqh:45, `return AgDayAnchor(t) + 86400;`, which is the whole of the Q1 expiry arithmetic. `AG_STATE_LOCKED = 3` is declared at State.mqh:16 with its name string at :39 and its waiting_on case at :101, so the state exists and renders today with no code able to enter it.

Expiry arithmetic under the measured clock regimes, assembled from artifacts already on this ledger rather than measured again today, and stated here because Phase 2 is where each one starts to matter. A Friday breach sets locked_until to Saturday 01:00, but TimeCurrent freezes at Friday 23:57:59 for the whole weekend, measured most recently across the 2026-08-15 and 2026-08-16 files, so the comparison stays false until the Monday reopen steps the clock and the lock outlives its computed expiry by about 48 hours. That errs locked and is acceptance row P2-I rather than a defect. The nightly break straddle is the one that produces a NEW question: 62 continuous minutes of frozen TimeCurrent while connected were measured on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14, the freeze straddles the 01:00 anchor, and a breach evaluated inside it, now valid under today's ruling, computes locked_until as the anchor of the day that is about to end, so the lock expires minutes later at the reopen and the new day opens carrying the same losing position. Backward clock steps are the mirror hazard: a 25 hour rewind was measured 2026-08-01, and while locked a rewind only delays expiry, which errs locked, but a breach declared AFTER a rewind computes an already past locked_until, and the clamp ruling of 2026-07-30 bounds locked_until from above only. Expiry cannot fire at all while disconnected, since TimeCurrent does not advance, which errs locked by construction and needs no new rule.

2026-08-17, the Monday reopen block, and an executor error that destroyed a primary artifact. The destruction is put first because it is the most important fact in this entry and because a later session must not discover it by finding a missing file. THE EXECUTOR DELETED the source artifact 20260816.log, the whole Sunday EA journal, 1466866 bytes. Cause, stated exactly so the trap is reusable: a PowerShell helper function was named `Rd`, and `rd` is a built in AllScope ALIAS for `Remove-Item`. PowerShell resolves aliases BEFORE functions, so the helper never ran and the call `$l=Rd "$ea\20260816.log"` executed `Remove-Item` against the journal. It failed silently and returned nothing, which read as an empty file rather than as a deletion, and the deletion was only identified on the next call when the path reported as not existing. `Remove-Item` does not use the Recycle Bin. Recovery was attempted and failed: `Win32_ShadowCopy` returns an initialization failure and `Get-ComputerRestorePoint` returns access denied, both for want of elevation. The file is gone. Standing rule added, alongside the CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06 and the stale directory entry trap of 2026-08-16: NEVER name a helper with a short token, and never with one that collides with a built in alias, since `rd`, `del`, `ls`, `cp`, `mv`, `sc` and others are aliases that silently win over any function of the same name. Prefer inline code over helpers when touching evidence, and never let a read path and a destructive cmdlet share a name.

What was lost and what survived, stated precisely rather than reassuringly. Lost: the Sunday EA journal from local 11:14:03 to end of day, which is the segment containing the sleep entry, the wake, and the single DEGRADED line the owner reported. Survived, because they had already been snapshotted and committed: docs/evidence/journal-20260816-p1f-close-binding.txt, 1348 lines through 11:13:33, at commit 6c6bced, and docs/evidence/journal-20260816-weekend-freeze-sunday-partial.txt through 11:04:33 at a61373e. Also unaffected: Saturday's complete file and Monday's. Net effect on the record is that one specific line, the DEGRADED line at 15:51:05, can no longer be quoted from an artifact. Everything else the owner reported about the sleep is independently reconstructible and is reconstructed below.

Sleep survival number two, unplanned, VERIFIED without reference to the deleted file. Cause per the owner and not measurable from here: the machine was unplugged and Windows sleep on battery was still at its default, the Never setting from 2026-08-13 having applied to plugged in only. Two independent witnesses establish the sleep. First, the terminal journal brackets it exactly: `connection to JustMarkets-Demo3 lost` at 12:33:14.992 and `authorized on JustMarkets-Demo3` at 15:51:11.765, a gap of 11876.773 seconds, with the following line reading `previous successful authorization performed from 5.29.18.75 on 2026.08.16 05:31:49`, which proves no intervening authorization and therefore no terminal restart inside the gap. Second, and this is the load bearing one, the state counter is continuous ACROSS the sleep and can be checked from MONDAY's file alone. Every one of the 1164 LIFE lines emitted on 2026-08-17 implies the same ACTIVE start when `seconds_in_state` is subtracted from the local stamp: 2026-08-14 12:32:18, one distinct value with zero variance across the whole day. That origin reproduces both of the owner's Sunday figures to the second, 172857 seconds after it landing on 2026-08-16 12:33:15 and 184727 seconds after it landing on 2026-08-16 15:51:05. So the ACTIVE run has been unbroken since the 2026-08-14 reconnect, straight through a 3 hour 17 minute 50 second suspension, with no re init, no SAFE_HALT, no takeover and no state reset. The process itself corroborates it, PID 2032 still reporting a start time of 2026-08-14 11:55:58. The counter therefore advances on wall clock rather than on ticks or timer callbacks, which is the same mechanism the 2026-08-05 mutex heartbeat entry predicted and the 2026-08-13 sleep first showed.

One honest limit on the above, recorded rather than glossed. The owner reports a single DEGRADED line at the wake and that is almost certainly right, since the EA resumed and emitted at 15:51:05 while the network did not reauthorize until 15:51:11.765, so that line was emitted disconnected and the Q10 gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:121 must have marked it. But it CANNOT BE QUOTED, because the executor deleted the file that held it. The claim is corroborated by timing and by code, not by artifact, and no future session may cite it as a quoted line.

This sleep is a DIFFERENT CLASS from the 2026-08-13 one and the difference is worth keeping. That sleep woke into a live market and produced a Q8 anchor jump on the first pass. This one began and ended entirely inside the weekend freeze, with `TimeCurrent` pinned at 2026.08.14 23:57:59 and the anchor pinned at 2026.08.14 01:00:00, so nothing needed to be caught up at the wake and no Q8 firing occurred or should have. Sleep survival is therefore now observed in both classes, inside a freeze and across a live boundary.

The Monday reopen, and the corrected prediction lands EXACTLY. Quoted from docs/evidence/journal-20260817-monday-reopen.txt. Last frozen line at 00:59:35 reads `anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|realized=-52.84|floating=0.00|base=1985.97|limit=99.30` with `server=2026.08.14 23:57:59`. Then at 00:59:59.487, four lines at one stamp: `INFO|anchor jump accepted: previous_high=2026.08.14 01:00:00, accepted=2026.08.17 01:00:00, jump=259200s`, the matching ALERT carrying the Q8 amendment text, the terminal Alert popup, and `INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.17 01:00:00|jump=259200s`. Then at 01:00:05 the first new day line reads `anchor=2026.08.17 01:00:00|realized=0.00|floating=0.00|base=2133.13|limit=106.66` with the server clock unfrozen to 2026.08.17 01:00:07. The prediction corrected at commit 59f6d21 named base 2133.13 and limit 106.66 in advance and both are exact; the superseded figure 2033.13 appears nowhere in the day, which is the discriminating check. Q8 LIVE FIRING NUMBER THREE, weekend class, and exactly one ALERT for the day against the cadence cap. `seconds_in_state` runs 217637 to 217667 straight across the boundary, so the rollover was absorbed inside ACTIVE with no transition, the same absorption P1-A and P1-M recorded. Day totals for 2026-08-17 to 09:41:35: 1164 LIFE lines, no gap above 31 seconds, DEGRADED 0, RESYNC 0, WARN 0, ALERT 1.

P1-B, stated plainly at owner request. The row reads "one weekend harvest: anchor holds Friday through the freeze, adopts Monday at reopen, no Sat/Sun rollover lines | journal scan, quoted lines" at PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md:251. The PROPERTY is established and the ARTIFACT SET is not clean, and those are different things. Clause by clause. "Adopts Monday at reopen" is fully met and quoted above. "Anchor holds Friday through the freeze" is directly evidenced for all of Saturday, 2880 lines with the anchor pinned and zero non LIFE lines, and for Sunday to 11:13:33. "No Sat/Sun rollover lines" is directly evidenced for all of Saturday and for Sunday to 11:13:33, and for the remaining Sunday window it is established INDIRECTLY but rigorously, from an intact artifact: Monday's reopen reports `previous_high=2026.08.14 01:00:00` and `old_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00` and `jump=259200s`. Q8's latch is a HIGH WATER MARK, so any rollover that had fired on Saturday or Sunday would have raised previous_high to that later value and would have shortened the jump below three days. Neither happened, so no anchor movement occurred anywhere between 2026.08.14 01:00:00 and 2026.08.17 01:00:00, including inside the sleep and inside the deleted window. The property holds. What is NOT clean is the harvest itself, and for two independent reasons only one of which is the executor's fault: a genuine 3 hour 18 minute hole where the machine was asleep and emitted nothing at all, and the deleted remainder of the Sunday file. RECOMMENDATION, and it is a recommendation and not a ruling: re harvest next weekend. It costs nothing, since the machine sits idle through a freeze anyway, it removes a defect the executor introduced, and P1-B is precisely the row whose subject is an uninterrupted observation, so closing it on a reconstruction argues against the thing it exists to test. What this weekend has already proven should stand recorded so that next weekend's run is confirmatory rather than exploratory. P1-B is left OPEN and the owner may overrule this and close it on the inference above, which is sound.
