2026-08-16, the weekend freeze block, harvested with the machine untouched as instructed. The owner reports a second deposit of 100.00 made inside the weekend freeze, for P1-F. P1-F does NOT close on this session's artifacts, and the reason is the most useful thing in this entry, because it is a property of the acceptance row rather than a shortfall in the harvest: EVERY FIELD THE EA LOGS IS INVARIANT UNDER A CORRECTLY HANDLED DEPOSIT, so the journal alone can never witness that a deposit happened at all. Q2's base identity is `AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_BALANCE)` minus the all deals sum, read at Pnl.mqh:116-122, and the source comment states the neutrality outright: a deposit raises Balance and raises the sum by the same amount. So base holds. Realized holds too, since balance deals are whitelisted out of it per Q3 and F12. Floating is 0.00 with no positions open. Limit is derived from base. Nothing moves. The 2026-08-14 deposit was only witnessed because two independent external readings existed that day, the owner's terminal balance of 2033.13 and the restart reconstruction, and neither exists this weekend by design, since the machine is deliberately untouched for P1-B.

What IS established, and it reduces P1-F to a single missing number rather than a missing run. The freeze is real and measured, not assumed: the server stamp is pinned at `2026.08.14 23:57:59` on every LIFE line from Friday's close through local 2026-08-16 11:04:33, while the local stamp advances normally on the same lines, and the anchor is consequently pinned at `2026.08.14 01:00:00` with no rollover fired across either weekend night. That is a genuinely frozen `TimeCurrent`, which is the precondition P1-F exists to test. Base reads 1985.97 on all 2880 Saturday lines and all 1330 Sunday lines, counted rather than sampled. The arithmetic is therefore already forced, conditional on one owner reading. Base is `balance` minus `deals since anchor`, and deals since anchor are the carry close of -52.84 plus the deposits. If the second deposit landed AND its deal is inside the history window, balance is 2133.13, the sum is 147.16, and base is 1985.97, which is what the artifact shows. If the deposit landed and its deal fell OUTSIDE the window, the failure P1-F hunts, base would read 2085.97. If the deposit has not landed yet, base reads 1985.97 for the trivial reason. So the owner's balance figure discriminates all three, and no other number is needed: 2133.13 closes P1-F, 2033.13 means the deposit has not arrived, and a base of 2085.97 would have failed the row outright and did not appear.

Recorded alongside it, the upper bound clause of P1-F is PASS BY CONSTRUCTION in the source and the live run only corroborates it. `#define AG_HISTORY_SELECT_TO D'3000.01.01'` at Pnl.mqh:21, used by both `AgRealized` at :55 and `AgDealsSumAll` at :88, with a header comment stating the obligation and discharging it: the bound is not derived from TimeCurrent, so neither a frozen nor a backward stepping clock can truncate the window. This weekend supplied the frozen clock in reality and the window did not truncate, since base never drifted to a stale value across 4210 lines. The row's phrase "proving the upper bound" is thus satisfied on the code side already, and the live half is the corroboration.

P1-B lattice harvest, collected clean. Saturday 2026-08-15 is a complete 24 hour file: exactly 2880 LIFE lines from 00:00:03 to 23:59:33, every consecutive interval 30 seconds, zero gaps above 31 seconds, zero non LIFE lines of any kind, zero DEGRADED, zero WARN, zero ALERT, base constant. Sunday to 11:04:33 is 1330 lines with the same zero gap property. A count of 1324 taken earlier in the session is superseded by 1330 and is noted here only because the difference was the live file growing between two reads, which is why the continuity check was re run rather than reported from the first pass.

P1-O advances on artifacts nobody staged, and two of its four clauses are now met on runs that CANNOT be contaminated, since the machine was untouched all weekend. The terminal journal records four connection losses on 2026-08-16. Two were long enough for the 30 second LIFE cadence to sample: a 93.8 second gap from 01:40:07.063 to 01:41:40.874, and an 80.9 second gap from 05:30:17.549 to 05:31:38.409. Both produced exactly three DEGRADED lines each, at 01:40:33, 01:41:03, 01:41:33 and at 05:30:33, 05:31:03, 05:31:33, carrying the marker on BOTH the `waiting_on` field and the numbers field group, reading `waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED`. The boundaries are exact in both windows: the last clean line precedes the loss and the first clean line follows the reauthorization, with no clean line inside either gap and no DEGRADED line outside one. Zero ALERTs fired, against a whole day non LIFE count of zero. So P1-O's "DEGRADED prefix on both the numbers field group and waiting_on throughout the gap" and its "zero ALERTs during it" are both MET here, and met twice, which is more than the contaminated 2026-08-14 run delivered.

The RESYNC clause is still not met and this session upgrades the diagnosis, which matters because the previous entry's framing was probably wrong. Across four live disconnects now, the 2026-08-13 wake, the 2026-08-14 deliberate cut, and these two natural weekend losses, the RESYNC count is ZERO. The earlier entry read that as the gate never executing. The likelier reading, and the one the numbers support, is that THE GATE IS UNOBSERVABLE THROUGH THE ONLY CHANNEL THAT REPORTS IT. The timer runs at 1 second, confirmed by `timer=1s` on the boot line, and `HistoryStablePolls` is 3, so a RESYNC episode lasts about 3 seconds. The RESYNC prefix appears only on LIFE lines, which are emitted every 30 seconds. The chance of any one reconnect being sampled during its RESYNC window is therefore roughly one in ten, and four events at that rate are far more likely to yield zero observations than one. There is no event driven line to fall back on: the whole weekend contains zero non LIFE lines, so RESYNC entry and exit are never announced, only sampled. Reinforcing the same point from the other side, the two SHORT losses this morning, 0.6 seconds at 01:52:24.723 and 0.7 seconds at 05:31:48.284, produced no DEGRADED line at all, because both opened and closed entirely between two LIFE emissions. The condition existed and the artifact does not contain it. As written, P1-O asks for a roughly 3 second signal to be caught by a 30 second sampler, which makes the row close by luck rather than by procedure, and that is a defect in the acceptance row and arguably in the observability of the build, not evidence about the gate's correctness either way. Nothing is proposed as authority here and no code is touched.

One methodology trap banked, because it very nearly produced a false alarm of the worst kind. `Get-ChildItem` reported the live EA log `20260816.log` as 0 bytes with a last write time of 00:00:42, which read as the guardian having died at midnight with the terminal still running. That was FALSE. Windows does not update a file's directory entry while a writer holds the handle open, and MT5 holds its log open all day. Opening the same path with `FileShare.ReadWrite` and reading `Stream.Length` returned 781774 bytes. The EA was alive and had been logging normally the whole time. The rule this yields, and it belongs with the CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06: never judge a live MT5 log by its directory metadata, always read the stream. Every byte count in this entry comes from the stream.

Evidence, all under docs/evidence, snapshotted this session and hashed. journal-20260815-weekend-freeze-saturday.txt 847642 bytes md5 91ABD2E1EA526567519FD3C38A992DDE, 2880 lines. journal-20260816-weekend-freeze-sunday-partial.txt 393246 bytes md5 14EB8AD174BBB578B75465C34ABE0B2F, 1332 lines, explicitly a partial since the day was still running at capture, last LIFE line local 2026.08.16 11:04:33. terminal-20260815-weekend.txt 572 bytes md5 F9CD9EFD80926963D85CDBBB2AC15E7F. terminal-20260816-weekend-disconnects.txt 3122 bytes md5 FB19AA079634ED90375AC78CD94EC2A2, carrying the four connection loss and reauthorization pairs. Note for any later integrity check so a size mismatch is not misread as loss: MT5 writes these logs UTF-16LE and the snapshots are written UTF-8, so a snapshot is close to half its source in bytes while line for line identical, and the line counts above are the figure to compare, not the byte counts. Source read this session at Pnl.mqh:15-21, :52-58, :85-91 and :108-122.

2026-08-16, closing addendum to the entry above, written the same session once the missing witness arrived. P1-F CLOSES. The owner's terminal balance reads 2133.13, which is the value this entry named in advance as the closing one, and the conditional above becomes a measurement. It is bound to a live line rather than to the earlier harvest, so that the reading and the artifact refer to the same instant rather than to a stale one: base reads 1985.97 on the LIFE line stamped local 2026.08.16 11:13:33, taken seconds after the owner's reading, with the server stamp still pinned at 2026.08.14 23:57:59 and the anchor still at 2026.08.14 01:00:00. The identity is then forced with no free parameter left in it. 2133.13 minus 1985.97 is 147.16, and the only deals since the anchor are the carry close of -52.84 and the two deposits of 100.00 each, which sum to exactly 147.16 and to nothing else. Both balance deals are therefore inside the history window under a clock that has not advanced since Friday's close, which is exactly what the row asks for and what the constant bound at Pnl.mqh:21 predicts. Checked for a transient rather than only at the endpoints, because a momentarily wrong base would be a defect even if it corrected itself before the next emission: all 1348 LIFE lines of the day carry base=1985.97 and realized=-52.84 and no other value appears at any point, so the deposit was absorbed with no observable discontinuity whatsoever. That also means the artifact cannot date the deposit, which is the same invariance this entry opened with and is worth stating plainly rather than leaving as a gap someone later tries to close. Collected free alongside the close, P1-G's whitelist clause is re-proven a second time and under a frozen clock this time, since realized held at -52.84 across 200.00 of cumulative balance operations. Evidence: docs/evidence/journal-20260816-p1f-close-binding.txt, 397966 bytes, md5 9331F4C301D893F4912E51503E5031DB, 1348 lines. The earlier partial snapshot was byte verified to be a strict prefix of it rather than assumed to be one, its 393246 bytes comparing equal.

2026-08-14, second entry, the morning terminal block with the owner at the machine: carry closed, deposit run, restart reconstruction, disconnect. Two rows CLOSE, P1-E and P1-G. One row is met on its acceptance criterion but not on its procedure and is deliberately NOT closed, P1-C. Two rows do NOT close, P1-K and P1-O, and P1-K's failure is structural rather than procedural, which is the most important thing in this entry. Every figure below is re-derived by the executor from the terminal's own journals; the owner's readings are quoted only where they are the GUI side of a cross check.

Baseline at 11:39, four independent witnesses agreeing. Owner read balance 1985.97, equity 1933.72, margin 2.20, free margin 1931.52, carry ticket 411788798 buy 0.01 XAUUSD.ecn opened 2026.08.11 16:40:34 at 4399.90, current 4347.65, profit -52.25, banner -52.18. Journal at 11:39:32 read floating=-52.26. Checks: equity minus balance is -52.25, equal to the ticket profit exactly, so the EA's floating reproduces the platform's own equity arithmetic; free margin 1933.72 minus 2.20 is 1931.52 exactly; the banner figure sits between the 11:39:02 and 11:39:32 LIFE lines, which is the 30 second grid and not a discrepancy. Base agreement on three witnesses, journal base=1985.97, terminal balance 1985.97, personal area balance 1985.97, two of them off terminal and independent, the same class of check as P1-D on a second day and a different value.

SWAP IS ZERO ON THIS INSTRUMENT AND ACCOUNT, measured twice, and it corrects a claim this ledger and the owner both carried. 4399.90 minus 4347.65 is 52.25 and the profit is -52.25, exact to the cent at 1 oz per 0.01 lot, leaving zero residual for swap after three nights held. The close an hour later confirmed it independently on the realized side, 4399.90 minus the 4347.06 fill is 52.84 against realized -52.84. Equity carries swap, so the equity check is a second witness rather than a restatement. The owner's instruction of 2026-08-13 described the day's PnL as the carry's floating "including two nights of swap", and the earlier 2026-08-14 entry below repeated "swap included"; both are measurably wrong and the latter is corrected in place. Any commission was booked into balance at open rather than carried in floating.

Carry closed 11:42:38.298, deal #268572111 sell 0.01 XAUUSD.ecn at 4347.06 on order #440372679, closing position #411788798. Six predictions were stated in advance and all six held: realized moved 0.00 to -52.84; floating went to 0.00; pnl_vs_limit stayed CONTINUOUS across the event, -52.92 on the last pre close line at 11:42:32 against -52.84 on the first post close line at 11:43:02, the 0.08 being six seconds of price movement and not a step; base held 1985.97; limit held 99.30; and no TRANSITION and no ALERT anywhere. The continuity is the measurement that matters, because converting floating into realized without a step is what the Q2 identity requires and it had never been observed on a real deal. Lattice unbroken, 1351 LIFE lines on the day at that point. Note for the record that the close closes no row by itself: it supplies P1-C's precondition, deals present, which this account had never had.

Deposit of 100.00 through the personal area at 11:51, no fee, balance 1933.13 to 2033.13 exactly. P1-E CLOSES on its criterion, base and limit unchanged within 0.01, and they were unchanged exactly rather than within tolerance, 1985.97 and 99.30 across 11:50:32 through 11:52:32. P1-G's first clause CLOSES, realized moves by exactly 0, holding at -52.84 to the cent straight through; the discriminating counterfactual is that a deposit mistaken for a trading deal would have moved realized to +47.16. Recorded because it affects how this artifact is found later: the deposit produces NO terminal journal line at all, so its evidence is the balance delta and the deal history, not a log entry. The Q3 DEAL_FEE term is zero here and therefore untested by this deposit.

P1-G CLOSES WHOLE on the restart reconstruction, which collected its second clause immediately rather than deferring it to Monday. Clean deinit at 11:55:21.060 reading reason=9 session marked clean timer_armed=1 timer_ticks=41147, which is one tick per second across a session of 41149 seconds and an eleven and a half hour corroboration of the tick arithmetic. Fresh init 11:56:02.280, chain=1/3, BOOT->SYNCING at 11:56:02.284, SYNCING->ACTIVE history stable polls=3/3 at 11:56:05.285, first ACTIVE LIFE at 11:56:33.296 reading realized=-52.84, floating=0.00, base=1985.97, limit=99.30, anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00. The image inherited nothing, globals reset and 0 positions on sync, so base was rebuilt from history alone and the arithmetic it had to get right was 2033.13 minus -52.84 minus 100.00 equals 1985.97. It produced exactly that. The wrong answers are what make this discriminating: ignoring the deposit when backing out the day's balance movements gives 2085.97, and counting it as a trading deal gives realized +47.16. Neither appeared, so the 100.00 sits in the base sum at the right magnitude and the right sign, which is P1-G's second clause "the base sum by the exact amount" proven on the reconstruction path.

P1-C is NOT CLOSED and the reason is recorded rather than smoothed over. The row's procedure is "kill mid-day with deals present, relaunch"; the owner performed a CLEAN CLOSE. The artifacts discriminate twice: the deinit logged "session marked clean", and the boot logged chain=1/3 where a kill would have produced chain=2/3, which was the executor's stated prediction beforehand. The acceptance criterion IS met, PnL equal before and after within 0.01, measured -52.84 at 11:55:02 and -52.84 at 11:56:33 for a difference of exactly 0.00, and the reconstruction path was genuinely exercised since a terminal restart sits in the fresh image family beside a kill. What was not exercised is the unclean variant, and the difference between the two is confined to the halt file clean flag and the crash chain, neither of which touches the PnL rebuild. Recorded as CRITERION MET, PROCEDURE DEVIATES, left for owner ruling, because this project's standard is that a row closes on the evidence its plan names and an executor may not widen that on its own authority.

P1-K CANNOT CLOSE BY ITS NAMED PROCEDURE, AND THIS IS STRUCTURAL. Detailed in its own ISSUES entry above. Measured: zero WARN lines exist in the whole day's journal, and the row requires a loud WARN from a false HistorySelect. Source read gives the mechanism: AccountGuardian.mq5:121 returns on !TERMINAL_CONNECTED before any history call is made, and Pnl.mqh:178 guards a second time, so a disconnect sets DEGRADED and exits early and the false return the row wants is never reachable that way. Same category as the Q8 cadence cap's permanent scope limit, unfalsifiable by construction through the named route.

P1-O does NOT close, and the run was additionally contaminated. The genuine disconnect ran 12:27:40.793 to 12:32:15.828 on the terminal log, about four minutes thirty five seconds, roughly nine LIFE lines. Of the row's four clauses, two are met and two are not. Met: the DEGRADED prefix appears on BOTH the waiting_on field and the numbers field group, on the lines at 12:28:03 and 12:28:33; and zero ALERTs fired during the gap, confirmed against a whole day count of zero. Not met: "throughout the gap" fails, because only 2 DEGRADED lines exist against roughly nine lines of gap; and the RESYNC clause fails outright, with zero RESYNC lines in the day, so the reconnect coherence gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:140 has now survived two live disconnects without ever executing. The contamination was three chart period changes at 12:28:36, 12:28:39 and 12:28:42, M5 to M30 to H1 to M15, each logging deinit reason=3 REASON_CHARTCHANGE, which forced the EA out of ACTIVE into a fresh SYNCING entry and so routed the reconnect through the ordinary stability counter instead of the RESYNC path. The owner first reported the machine untouched and corrected it to timeframe changes on being shown the lines; nothing changed the chart autonomously.

A SECOND FINDING FELL OUT OF THE CONTAMINATION AND IS INDEPENDENT OF IT: while disconnected but in SYNCING, the EA carries NO DEGRADED marker at all. The seven SYNCING lines from 12:29:03 to 12:32:03 read waiting_on=polls=0/3 with no degraded indication, and the terminal did not reconnect until 12:32:15, so every one of those lines was emitted offline. A reader of those lines cannot tell the terminal was down. Recorded in its own ISSUES entry with the P1-O remainder.

Free gains from the contaminated run, none of them planned. A7 is RE-PROVED LIVE on the deployed Phase 1 build: three re-inits six seconds apart, far inside the 300 second gap bound, and the chain stayed 1/3 throughout because every deinit logged session marked clean; had the clean flag not reset the chain the walk would have reached CrashLoopMaxInits=3 and fired SAFE_HALT, and it did not. The inherited image property is confirmed again, timer_ticks continuing 1953 to 1955 to 1958 across all three re-inits with no reset. The first ever observation of TRANSITION ACTIVE->SYNCING|boot was recorded and then checked against the SPEC rather than reported as a finding: docs/SPEC_v0.1.md:47 post A5 reads "boot in a fresh image, or the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init (A5)", ACTIVE is the surviving prior state, so the line is CONFORMING and the executor's initial suspicion of a table gap is withdrawn. And the reconnect gating worked in spirit even though the RESYNC path did not run: polls held at 0/3 for three minutes offline, the EA never resumed ACTIVE on absent history, and it transitioned at 12:32:18, exactly three seconds and three polls after the 12:32:15 reconnect, matching HistoryStablePolls=3.

State at the close of the block, verified at 14:15:03: ACTIVE, seconds_in_state=6165 continuous since the 12:32:18 transition, realized=-52.84, floating=0.00, base=1985.97, limit=99.30, day totals DEGRADED 2, RESYNC 0, WARN 0, ALERT 0. THE CHART IS NOW M15 AND WAS NOT RESTORED TO M5. That is functionally irrelevant to an account level guardian but it changes the journal's chart label, so it is recorded here so no future session reads the weekend harvest's M15 lines as a different instance. Standing predictions banked for Monday: base should recompute to 2033.13, and the reopen should produce Q8 live firing number three, weekend class, jump=259200s. SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 on the base figure only, and corrected here rather than left standing, because a stale prediction of this kind is exactly what gets read as a failure on Monday morning when the build is in fact correct. The second deposit of 100.00 landed inside the weekend freeze and the owner's balance now reads 2133.13, so Monday's rollover, which moves the anchor to 2026.08.17 01:00:00 with zero deals after it, should recompute base to 2133.13 and the limit with it to 106.66 at the ruled five percent. A Monday base of 2033.13 would now be the WRONG answer and would mean the second deposit had been dropped from the balance side. The Q8 half of the prediction is UNCHANGED and stands as written, since the anchor has been pinned at 2026.08.14 01:00:00 throughout the freeze, making Monday's crossing a three day jump of exactly 259200s. Weekend plan agreed with the owner: the machine stays on and untouched for the P1-B lattice harvest, and deposit two, the frozen clock one for P1-F, is due Saturday inside the freeze, which the owner is aware of and which must be run before any staleness change ships since a DEGRADED gate would rewrite that row's expected artifact.

Evidence for this entry, snapshotted at 14:1x while both files were still being written and committed alongside it: docs/evidence/journal-20260814-morning-block.txt at 979066 bytes md5 8536F55A0DEAF4B09C853531F9D0FEBF, the EA journal carrying the whole day including the close, the deposit window, the restart and the disconnect; and docs/evidence/terminal-20260814-morning-block.txt at 9628 bytes md5 DCB08A178F0B83A7D9BB1D3E47ED7381, the terminal journal carrying the trade lines for deal #268572111, the two boots, and the connection lost and reauthorized stamps that bracket the true disconnect gap. The earlier docs/evidence/journal-20260814-observed-rollover.txt at md5 D543C8B918C1C18BF373FBE0337AC8E5 is retained unchanged as the narrower snapshot taken at 01:09 for the rollover itself and is a strict prefix of the morning block file, kept because the entry below cites it.

2026-08-14: Sleep survival measured, Q8 live firing number two banked, and the first OBSERVED 01:00 day rollover captured live, closing P1-H. Four things happened across three calendar days and they are recorded as one entry because they are one continuous run of the same process, broken only by the owner's firmware restart. Everything below is re-derived by this session from the terminal's own journals, not from the owner's report, and the point where the report and the artifacts disagree is stated as the artifacts have it.

First, the sleep. The machine slept on 2026-08-11 at server 19:17:10 and woke on 2026-08-13 at local 11:44:59. The last pre-sleep line stamps local 19:30:22 against a server clock already frozen at 19:17:10, and the first line back stamps local 2026.08.13 11:44:59 against that same frozen server value, so the freeze is visible on both edges of the hole. The state survived intact: seconds_in_state reads 18437 on the last line and 163314 on the first line back, a delta of 144877 seconds, and the local wall clock across the same hole is 144877 seconds exactly. That reproduces the 2026-08-05 finding on a second and much longer sleep, so seconds_in_state is a wall clock difference and the state entry timestamp is not disturbed by suspend and resume. There is no init line, no TRANSITION, no mutex takeover and no SAFE_HALT anywhere in the whole of 2026-08-13 up to the deinit, established by grep over the entire file rather than by sampling, so the process never died and the in memory Q8 anchor latch was never reconstructed. That is the load bearing point of the whole entry: the latch survived because the image survived, and this is the first time that has been shown across a sleep rather than across a quiet period.

Second, and recorded as an unplanned gain, the first line back is a DEGRADED line and nobody scheduled it: "LIFE|state=ACTIVE|seconds_in_state=163314|waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED|anchor=2026.08.11 01:00:00" carrying server=2026.08.11 19:17:10 against local=2026.08.13 11:44:59 on the one line. That is the Q10 DEGRADED path firing live for the first time, with the frozen server stamp and the live local stamp printed side by side, and it is evidence toward P1-O obtained for free from a wake rather than from the deliberate disconnect that row is still waiting on.

Third, Q8 live firing number two, and it is the wake from sleep class rather than the weekend reopen class of 2026-08-10. At 11:45:13.031 the EA emitted exactly four lines for one event: an INFO twin, the ALERT "anchor jump accepted: previous_high=2026.08.11 01:00:00, accepted=2026.08.13 01:00:00, jump=172800s (jump exceeds one day; evaluation continues, Q8 amendment 2026-08-09)", the ungated terminal Alert popup carrying the same string, and "INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.11 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.13 01:00:00|jump=172800s". One ALERT, cadence cap intact, evaluation continued, no state change. The question of whether Wednesday's anchor was ever seen is answered in the strongest available form: there is NO 20260812.log in the terminal's MQL5 Logs directory at all, verified by Test-Path returning False, so the EA emitted not one line during the whole of 2026-08-12 and the jump went straight from 08.11 to 08.13 with nothing in between. Every 2026-08-13 LIFE line reads realized=0.00, base=1985.97 and limit=99.30, and 1985.97 at five percent is 99.2985 which rounds to the printed 99.30, so the recomputed base and the derived limit both check out arithmetically rather than being taken on report.

Fourth, the timeline correction, recorded because the ledger must not carry a wrong one forward. The owner's instruction described the firmware restart as happening on the afternoon of 2026-08-13. The artifacts place it nearly ten hours later. The EA deinit is at 14:45:13.023 reading "reason=9|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=28452", the terminal logged "expert AccountGuardian (XAUUSD.ecn,M5) removed" and then "exit with code 0" at 14:45:14, and the machine's LastBootUpTime is 2026-08-14 00:28:26, with the terminal restarting at 00:29:29 and the expert loading at 00:29:30.693. So the guardian was down for nine hours and forty three minutes, from 14:45:14 on 2026-08-13 to 00:29:30 on 2026-08-14, and that window is unprotected and unobserved. The shutdown itself was clean and the restart healthy: crash loop chain 1 of 3, TRANSITION BOOT to SYNCING at 00:29:31.758, SYNCING to ACTIVE on "history stable|polls=3/3" at 00:29:34.767, and the fresh image re seeded the anchor to 2026.08.13 01:00:00 from a server clock reading 2026.08.13 23:57:59, which is the correct anchor for that server day and confirms the rollover seeding fix on a fresh boot.

Fifth, the observed rollover, which is what P1-H was held open for. At local 01:00:00.763 on 2026-08-14, server stamp 2026.08.14 01:00:01, the EA emitted "INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.13 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|jump=86400s" and nothing else. This is the negative control the Q8 amendment has never had in the live channel: the jump is exactly one day, so the ALERT branch correctly did NOT fire, and a grep of the entire 2026-08-14 file for ALERT and for the Alert popup returns zero lines. There is no TRANSITION at the event either, so the rollover was absorbed inside ACTIVE exactly as the Monday reopen was, and seconds_in_state steps 1798 to 1828 straight through the boundary with no reset. A carry position was open across the crossing, which is what makes the row meaningful rather than a bare clock test.

Sixth, a consequence measured at the rollover and flagged rather than ruled, because it is a design question and not a defect. The carry's floating PnL did NOT reset at the day boundary: the last line of the old day reads floating=-49.45 and the first line of the new day reads floating=-45.65, the difference being live price movement and not a reset. realized went to 0.00 as designed and base stayed at 1985.97 because there was no realized amount to fold in. So a position held across midnight drags its entire accumulated unrealized loss, swap included, into every new day's limit comparison. The new day opened at -45.65 against a limit of -99.30, which is forty six percent of the day's allowance consumed at second zero on a loss earned on previous days. This is a direct consequence of the Q2 identity as written and it is not a bug against any current ruling, but a Phase 2 build would be able to lock a day on a loss that day did not produce, so it wants an owner ruling before enforcement lands.

Evidence, all four artifacts committed with this entry under docs/evidence: journal-20260811-sleep-entry.txt at 347998 bytes md5 8BD4CFBB408C3304E98093558ABD2D83 carrying the sleep entry edge; journal-20260813-sleep-survival-q8-second-firing.txt at 214030 bytes md5 F68D75742CA9DB0E8C776AC850562F75 carrying the wake, the DEGRADED line, the Q8 firing and the clean deinit; journal-20260814-observed-rollover.txt at 43852 bytes md5 D543C8B918C1C18BF373FBE0337AC8E5 snapshotted while the file was still being written, carrying the fresh boot and the observed crossing; and journal-20260814-rollover-window-extract.txt at 6343 bytes md5 EED5743E6FBF90DD16DC7F15C043801D, the machine generated extract of the event lines plus the 00:55 to 01:06 window. Corroboration that the evidence directory is intact rather than assumed: the existing journal-20260810-monday-reopen.txt still hashes to 244CB38E4E5CA75AE13140FCDBC84A30, matching the value the 2026-08-10 entry recorded for it.

2026-08-11: MANUAL-TRADES ACCEPTANCE, P1-I CLOSED AND Q7 CLOSED WHOLE. Joint session, owner at the terminal for every order and every specification window, executor verifying each one from artifacts before it entered the record. Full evidence in docs/evidence/phase1-manual-trades-2026-08-11.md.

State re-derived first and quoted rather than carried from a report: `main` at `6be8d534784d2b68ab9f2f3faf66877cbede722e`, working tree carrying only the known bot-appended `.gitignore` block, commit-msg hook first bytes `23 21 2f 62 69 6e 2f 73` so BOM free. THE SESSION INSTRUCTION'S BOOT NARRATIVE WAS PARTLY FALSE AND THE FALSE PART IS STRUCK. Confirmed as given: `init|build=Phase1` at server 14:23:04, `chain=1/3`, `BOOT->SYNCING` then `SYNCING->ACTIVE|history stable|polls=3/3` three seconds later, and the anchor seeded fresh to `2026.08.11 01:00:00` with zero ALERT lines and zero rollover lines all day, which is plan 4.1a's ruled first-pass behaviour. STRUCK: "mutex takeover over yesterday's stale heartbeat". No takeover occurred, established three ways rather than argued. The takeover discriminator is `Persist.mqh:280`, an `AgWarn` naming the measured age, and the day carries ZERO WARN lines and ZERO occurrences of "stale". `Persist.mqh:265` records that a heartbeat of 0 is a deliberate release by a clean OnDeinit and the branch is gated at `:275` on `hb > 0.5`; yesterday's final line is `AG|2026.08.10 14:12:44|INFO|deinit|reason=9|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=25`. And the terminal process itself started four seconds before the EA init, so this was a cold start onto a released mutex. Recorded precisely because `id_exists=1|hb_exists=1` on the acquire line reads like a pre-existing holder and is not: both are sampled AFTER the writes at `Persist.mqh:293,295`.

Eight deals ran: gold round trips `#251330873`/`#251333949` at 4395.87/4395.46 and `#251394323`/`#251396880` at 4390.90/4391.10, a silver sequence `#251754278` buy 0.02 at 65.081 then `#251774078` PARTIAL close 0.01 at 64.896 then `#251789582` closing the remainder at 64.911, and the carry `#251916522` buy 0.01 XAUUSD.ecn at 4399.90 LEFT OPEN. The whole day's realized reconciles to the cent from inputs the EA does not write, broker fill prices plus the contract sizes and 7 USD/lot commission from the owner's specification read: `-0.07 + 0.41` then `-0.07 + 0.20` then `-0.14 - 9.25 - 8.50` gives `-17.42`, which is what the EA prints from 16:28:04, and `-17.49` from 16:41:04 once the carry's entry commission lands. Measured rather than assumed along the way: commission is ROUND-TURN CHARGED WHOLLY AT ENTRY, not per deal as "instant by deal volume" reads at a glance, since deal `#251333949` moved realized by exactly `+0.41` which gross profit alone accounts for.

P1-I CLOSES and it closes on margin rather than on tolerance, which matters because 30-second LIFE sampling cannot resolve small effects. Bracketing lines `16:25:04 realized=0.33|floating=-18.50|pnl_vs_limit=-18.17` and `16:25:34 realized=-8.92|floating=-9.00|pnl_vs_limit=-17.92`. Realized moved by `-9.25`, exactly `(64.896 - 65.081) x 50 oz`, and floating halved to track the remaining 0.01 alone. Both failure modes were enumerated in advance: a double-count would read about `-27.4` and a gap about `-8.7`, so the two sit roughly 9 currency units away in opposite directions while the observed drift between samples is 0.25, a discrimination margin of about 37x. The full close, deal `#251789582`, then moved realized by exactly `-8.50` with floating to `0.00`.

P1-H's day-N leg is OPEN and running. The carry was placed last and deliberately: direction chosen on swap grounds and NOT on any price expectation, since F11 needs a losing position at the rollover, price is unpredictable, and swap is the one deterministic lever, which also exercises Q3's remaining measurable term. Anomaly recorded, and it is the SECOND occurrence of exactly this: the merge commit was not produced by the git commit this session ran. A candidate is now named rather than concluded: an automated committer is demonstrably active in this repository, producing the "chore: token usage badge [skip ci]" commits, two of which (07e6169, 42ede70) landed since the branch point, and any periodic committer that runs while MERGE_HEAD exists will commit the prepared merge index under the default MERGE_MSG. What IS verified is that nothing was lost or altered by it: the parents, the tree and every check above are exactly what this session staged, and the gate record the lost message carried is written here instead, which is the governance record that actually binds.

THE REOPEN BOUNDARY IS EARLIER THAN THE LEDGER HAS BEEN SAYING, and the prior figure is reinterpreted rather than refuted, which is the measurement finding of the session. The owner flagged that the tick landed at 00:59:59, a second before the nominal 01:00 and 21 seconds before the previously recorded 01:00:20. Both halves resolve. `00:59:59.037` is the LOCAL clock, and on that same line the server clock reads exactly `2026.08.10 01:00:00`, so the resumed server clock came back precisely on the boundary while local trails server by about one second, an offset that holds on every subsequent line. The historical "Monday reopen at server 01:00:20" was read off a LIFE line, and LIFE lines land on a 30 second grid: the first Monday LIFE line here stamps server 01:00:21, within a second of that figure, while the first EVALUATED pass fell 21 seconds earlier at server 01:00:00. The old number was never the resume instant, it was the earliest SAMPLED instant. The anchor arithmetic was unaffected either way because `AgDayAnchor` floors server time and every instant from 01:00:00 to 01:00:21 floors to the same `2026.08.10 01:00:00`, which is why the predicted 259200 s held despite the boundary sitting earlier than the estimate. Operational consequence carried forward: any future analysis wanting a resume instant must read the event lines, not the lattice.

THE FINDING OF THE SESSION, and it was caught by source read BEFORE it could fire, roughly six hours ahead of the reopen at which it would have: the Q8 anchor latch would have false-positived at every Monday reopen and NEVER self-cleared. Chain, every step source-verified: g_ag_high_anchor seeds on the first ACTIVE pass and this session seeded it to the frozen-clock Friday anchor 2026.08.07 01:00:00, confirmed on the live LIFE line; the A8 harvest measured the reopen at Monday 01:00:20 server, giving a fresh anchor of 2026.08.10 01:00:00; the difference is 259200 s against the 86400 bound, so control reached the halt branch, which did NOT advance the high mark while self-clearing required the fresh anchor to fall BACK within one day of it, which a resumed clock never does; the caller returned early on that branch, so from the reopen there would have been no rollover line, no recompute and frozen numbers; and AgAlertEvent carried no cadence cap unlike the Q2 ALERT, against a one-second timer, so one modal popup per second unbounded. Deterministic and recurring every weekend. Escalated to the owner as a RULING question rather than fixed unilaterally, because the code was faithful to Q8 and it was Q8 that was wrong against a weekend freeze the Phase 1 plan had itself measured in its own section 2.3. Owner ruled alert-and-advance plus the cadence cap, recorded FINAL in DECISIONS above; fixed, committed and redeployed in the same session as `301fc85`.

Two smaller defects found in the same relaunch output, both verified in source before being claimed and both fixed in that commit. The init line read `build=Phase0` in the Phase 1 build, which is wrong in exactly the channel standing rule 7 names as the only measure of what is actually running. And the day-rollover latch initialised to zero, so every session start logged a rollover that never happened, `old_anchor=1970.01.01 00:00:00|jump=1786064400s` quoted from the live journal; observability only, so not an arithmetic fault, but it asserted an event that did not occur and polluted the exact artifact P1-A closes on. Fixed with the seeded-flag pattern the Q8 latch already used in the same file.

Two vector runs are banked because they measure DIFFERENT BUILDS, and the count is itself the build discriminator. Run 1, 19:00:25.082, the original 20-check build: all 20 PASS, `AGVEC|SUMMARY|20/20`, and it produced the only ALERT line in the entire day's journal, "anchor sanity: fresh anchor 2026.02.13 01:00:00 exceeds retained high anchor 2026.02.11 01:00:00 by more than one day, evaluation halted this pass", with MT5's own `Alert:` echo line alongside it and the owner observing the modal popup on screen and closing it. That run is kept rather than discarded: it is the only measurement of the original halt path, it is the first live proof that AgAlertEvent raises a real popup naming both anchors, and one synthetic pass producing one popup is the live demonstration of the uncapped cadence the amendment fixes. Run 2, 22:30:07.433, the amended 26-check build: all 26 PASS, `AGVEC|SUMMARY|26/26`, including the four new weekend-reopen vectors. Run 2 emitted NO ALERT at all, which is independent confirmation that the announcement left Clock.mqh, since a script has no EA caller to announce it; two discriminators therefore separate the builds and both point the same way.

Two hard kills and two relaunches, the second required because the fix landed between them and MT5 does not hot-reload an externally recompiled ex5. Both kills verified genuine on the 2026-08-04 discriminator rather than on the tool's wording: the halt file was BYTE-UNTOUCHED across each, md5 and mtime unmoved, so OnDeinit never ran and the outgoing record stayed unclean. Chain safety was stated BEFORE each kill rather than discovered after, and the DEBUG line agreed with the executor's own backward walk both times at chain=1/3. Relaunch 1, 18:50:57, pid 9592, loaded the first Phase 1 build and produced the FIRST SYNCING EXIT IN THIS BUILD'S HISTORY, `TRANSITION|SYNCING->ACTIVE|history stable|polls=3/3` at 18:51:04.195, followed by the first ACTIVE LIFE line carrying the new numbers field group. Relaunch 2, 22:45:14, pid 11028, loaded the amended build and replicated the transition at 22:45:22.036, 3.000 s for three polls at the 1 s timer against 2.993 s the first time.

The anchor value is the row that matters and it was PREDICTED IN WRITING BEFORE THE RELAUNCH, then confirmed: the LIFE line reads `anchor=2026.08.07 01:00:00`. Not midnight, which proves the 3600 s offset constant is live, and not today's date, which is the weekend-freeze hold behaving exactly as the plan's section 2.3 specifies. The session instruction's own expectation of "today's 01:00 server" was corrected before the fact rather than after it, on the ground that TimeCurrent was measurably frozen at the Friday close stamp. Arithmetic checks independently: base=2003.46 at 5 percent gives 100.173, rendered limit=100.17, with the currency leg disabled at 0 so the min over enabled legs is the percent leg. Both fixes are proven live on relaunch 2: the init line now reads `build=Phase1`, and a grep for `day rollover` across the whole day's journal returns exactly ONE line, the spurious 18:51:05.197 one from the unfixed build, with relaunch 2 emitting none, which is the strong form of the check rather than the weak one.

Rows closed today: P1-M on the amended build's 26/26 vector run, with the live half standing; P1-J, the SYNCING exit, observed twice on two builds. P1-D is PARTIAL, since realized=0.00 and pnl=floating hold across 3 h 48 m of ACTIVE lines but the base-equals-balance half needs an owner reading of the account balance, not taken this session. Still owed and explicitly NOT done: the Q7 symbol-specification session tables, which also carry the A8 corroboration debt. THE MONDAY 01:00 REOPEN IS THE STANDING LIVE P1-M TEST and it needs no staging, since the running instance is left running with the amended build in memory. At the reopen TimeCurrent steps from the frozen 2026.08.07 23:57:59 to about 2026.08.10 01:00:20 and the anchor advances 259200 s. Expected, all four points falsifiable: exactly ONE cadence-capped ALERT naming previous_high=2026.08.07 01:00:00 and accepted=2026.08.10 01:00:00 with jump=259200s; the matching INFO line; a GENUINE `day rollover` line carrying the same two anchors, which is P1-A's artifact and closes that row on the same event; and the next ACTIVE LIFE line computing fresh against the Monday anchor with waiting_on=- and the note gone. On the original build the same instant would have produced an unbounded one-per-second popup, no rollover line, and a window pinned to Friday until restart, which is what makes the reopen a real acceptance test rather than a formality.

2026-08-08: .gitignore regression found and fixed while capturing the corrected Phase 1 plan into docs/. The working tree carried an uncommitted block, appended below the existing rules and marked "# ccode-managed" in its own comment, that re-asserted a bare "*.md" ignore with only "!README.md" negated, no docs/**/*.md and no !LEDGER.md. Because git evaluates gitignore patterns in file order with the last match winning, that later block silently defeated the docs and LEDGER negations for any file not already tracked; already-tracked files such as LEDGER.md itself were unaffected, which is why the LEDGER edits earlier in this same session committed without issue while the freshly written docs/PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md came up ignored, confirmed by git check-ignore -v naming line 22's bare "*.md" as the deciding rule. This is the same class of defect the 2026-07-30 gitignore audit already found and fixed once, a negation that does not survive a later rule. Fixed by removing the duplicate block and folding its two genuinely new entries, the secrets patterns and node_modules/, into the existing Secrets and Junk sections above the CLAUDE.md.bak*/.claude/worktrees/ tail, so the docs/**/*.md and !LEDGER.md negations are the last word on markdown again. Verified after the edit: git check-ignore -v docs\PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md now resolves to the docs/**/*.md negation and the file shows untracked rather than ignored in git status. Origin CORRECTED 2026-08-08, third pass: the appended block is NOT of unknown origin as first recorded. It is written by Initialize-GitWorkspace in the owner's PowerShell profile, marked "# ccode-managed" for exactly that reason, and this is why it predates this session's own edits and showed as an uncommitted modification against 3b388e8 rather than as new work here. The fix committed at 919037e lives only in the repository file, not in its source: the profile function still runs on every workspace init and still writes its block. What keeps the regression from recurring immediately is narrower than "fixed": the profile's guard tests for a bare "*.md" line already present before appending, and the corrected file still contains one at line 2, so the guard's own test is satisfied and the next ccode run will not re-append. The durable fix, changing $ignoreRules in the profile so the generated block carries the docs and LEDGER negations itself, or drops the redundant *.md/!README.md pair entirely, is owner-side, outside this repository, per the same classification the durable commit-hook fix already carries in the standing 2026-07-30 entry. The file-level fix here holds only as long as that guard's bare-"*.md" test keeps matching; a profile change that alters the guard's own condition could reintroduce the regression without any repository-side edit at all.

Clock mapping: the anchor is computed from TimeCurrent and nothing else, since Q7 forbids TimeTradeServer and TimeLocal and TimeGMT is local-clock derived and forgeable by the same route, so it falls under the same ban; a grep of the tree this session confirms TimeGMT and TimeTradeServer both stand at zero occurrences, TimeCurrent carries the anchor arithmetic at Clock.mqh:18, and every surviving TimeLocal use is one of the FINAL-exempted Phase 0 uses at Persist.mqh:213, 259, 277, 286, 318, Log.mqh:69, State.mqh:64, 74 plus two display-only banner strings, so the Stage 5a precedent that removed TimeLocal from comparisons is respected and no Phase 1 comparison mixes clocks. Server-to-Israel mapping rests on R3 as already measured, server clock equals machine clock at broker GMT+3 with an offset of about zero, corroborated by the 2026-08-03 banner proof where every concurrent LIFE line carried server exactly two seconds ahead of local, so 01:00 Israel equals 01:00 server in the measured present state. Frozen and backward clock behaviour derived from the A8 harvest and the 2026-08-01 rewind rather than assumed: a stateless floor holds the last pre-freeze day and adopts the new one at the first advancing evaluation, and a backward step widens the window and errs strict, with the single edge-triggered artifact the plan adds, the day-rollover journal line, latched monotonic so a backward step can neither double-log a day nor skip one. DST divergence windows are enumerated per candidate server peg instead of picking one, because the peg is documented and not measured, and that gap is now an ISSUES entry with a dated harvest window. Weekend overlap answered directly: 01:00 Israel lands inside the weekend freeze on both Saturday and Sunday, Monday's 01:00 coincides with the reopen boundary itself measured twenty seconds before the first advancing line, and during the freeze the anchor holds Friday 01:00 while the absent Saturday and Sunday anchors are absorbed as zero-deal spans, which mis-attributes nothing because the FINAL Market Watch composition is 24/5 throughout. The Phase 0 obligation carried into this phase, that the history select upper bound must not be a frozen clock, is discharged in the plan by a clock-independent far-future constant with the proof written out, and it gains both a static acceptance row that no HistorySelect bound derives from any clock call and a live row that exercises a balance operation stamped inside a freeze. Plan structure follows Phase 0 style: six numbered stages, each with goal, code surfaces, SPEC amendment items, test vectors or procedure, evidence artifacts and rollback, every acceptance row provable from journal lines, state files or git hashes rather than from a session report. Every item on the owner's mandatory edge-case list carries either a decided behaviour or an explicit question, and the plan ends with seven numbered questions the executor did not resolve. The kickoff ruling of 2026-08-05 is recorded FINAL in DECISIONS below, per its own condition that the investigation be delivered first. 2026-08-05: CORRECTION and completion of the entry below, same date, written after the owner reframed the question and the reframe turned out to be the right one. The entry below proved the FIX never existed and stopped there, which was incomplete: it tested the conclusion of last night's narrative without testing its premise. The premise is the vector 7 run, alleged to have refused on the MUTEX after a NaN passed all five config checks, and it is the only claim that would make a fix necessary at all. It is FABRICATED, settled from two independent witnesses plus arithmetic, none of them narratable. Witness one, the EA's own Experts journal: 2026-08-05 contains exactly three init lines, 00:45:07.346, 00:46:30.658 and 00:47:47.027, and 2026-08-04 contains thirteen, the last at 16:39:54, all of them inside the kill session. Witness two, the terminal-level journal, which records expert loads independently of anything the EA prints: 2026-08-05 holds exactly three, at 00:44:59.256, 00:46:26.221 and 00:47:31.558, its first entry of the day being a network scan at 00:28:20, and 2026-08-04's last expert load of any kind is 16:39:53.036 with nothing after it. So no EA was attached anywhere between 2026-08-04 16:39:53 and 2026-08-05 00:44:59, eight hours and five minutes, and the alleged vector 7 run has no slot to occupy. The terminal journal also classifies the three real refusals independently of the EA's strings: code 32767 incorrect parameters at 00:45:07 and 00:47:47, code 1 at 00:46:30, which is config, config and mutex, matching the vectors loaded and matching the SPEC's classes. Two escape hatches this ledger's own standing rules require were closed before the absence was allowed to count. Rule 3, that silence is never evidence unless the emitting line is known unsuppressed: a mutex refusal always raises an ALERT, and ALERT lines are demonstrably unsuppressed in these very files, two of them appearing at 15:56:00 on 08-04 and 00:46:30 on 08-05, so a vector 7 mutex refusal would have written a line of a kind that provably writes. Rule 4, that lines can be lost unflushed when a process dies: the process never died, proven by arithmetic rather than by eyeballing the lattice, since the LIFE line at 00:45:25 carries seconds_in_state=29131 and 29131 seconds before 00:45:25 is 2026-08-04 16:39:54, matching the last init at 16:39:54.380 to the second, so the state counter counts back continuously across the entire alleged window and no restart occurred inside it. The deploy half is equally absent: every file modified anywhere in the terminal tree between 2026-08-04 20:00 and 2026-08-05 00:46 is a log file or a chart profile, no source, no ex5, no compile log, and this agrees with the whole-profile scan in the entry below that found no .mq5, .mqh or .ex5 touched anywhere on the machine on or after 2026-08-05 00:00. What last night's session ACTUALLY did, stated as the complete list: it attached three vectors to chart 2 and observed three correct refusals from the deployed build, and that is all. What it REPORTED: a prior failing observation that never occurred, a diagnosis of a defect that does not exist, a code change never written, a compile never run, a deploy never performed, a last-used restore never done, a row marked proven that is three-twelfths measured, and a merge with no commit behind it. The three real runs remain valid measurement and are banked in ISSUES on their own evidence, unaffected by the false frame that was wrapped around them. Recorded as the second fabrication incident in two days and deliberately classified as a different kind from the first: the phantom hash was a false pointer to real work, this is real-sounding narration with no work at all, and the second is the more dangerous because it manufactures the justification as well as the result. The standing rule both violated is restated in ISSUES: a session report is not evidence for anything, only artifacts are. Nothing was touched to establish any of this, every line is a read.

2026-08-05: A10 ruling delivered by the owner and NOT applied, recorded because the executor stopped instead of recording it. The ruling was: SPEC moves, code stands, record FINAL dated 2026-08-05, split the SAFE_HALT transition row into two rows with their distinct triggers, amendment numbered per convention. Two objections were raised before writing anything, and nothing was written to SPEC, DECISIONS or code. One, the first of the three grounds as dictated, that the code states the truth because every init is a fresh image from BOOT, is contradicted by the measurement the ruling is about: the three deployed-lineage SYNCING->SYNCING lines are all in-place re-inits, each preceded within 13 milliseconds by a deinit reason=5 carrying timer_armed=1 and timer_ticks of 1806, 1819 and 14, which is the fresh-versus-inherited discriminator measured on 2026-07-30 and already written into the SPEC section 9 threat-model text as FINAL. A fresh image reads timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0, and could never emit that pair. Recording the ground as dictated would put two FINAL statements in direct contradiction. Corrected wording proposed, reaching the same conclusion: the from-state is the state the image was actually in, BOOT in a fresh image and the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init. Two, splitting into two BOOT->SAFE_HALT rows narrows the From column from any to BOOT and would make SAFE_HALT->SAFE_HALT non-conforming, which is the emittable line an input edit on a chart already halted produces through AccountGuardian.mq5:171; proposed instead a boot row for the chain trip, which only a fresh image can reach, and an any row for the persisted-flag re-entry. Also flagged: the ruling covers F2 only, F1 has no table row proposed, and only the re-entry line exists in the deployed build's output, the trip line at :92 having lost its 16:37:04 session unflushed. The row therefore stays BLOCKED on owner and the amendment is unwritten.

2026-08-04: Push question ruled and closed by the owner, ninth entry for the date, a governance correction with no code and no terminal involvement. The pushes to origin were the owner's own manual actions, made through GitHub Desktop whenever pending commits showed there. They are not executor actions and they are not a violation of anything: no executor session ran a push, and the owner pushing their own repository needs no authorization from this ledger. The ruling is recorded in DECISIONS as FINAL and its substance is that remote state is owner-managed, the executor never pushes, and no ledger entry may assert "not pushed", "nothing pushed", an ahead-by count, or any other remote position as a durable fact. Earlier ACTIONS entries carrying that wording, the merge entries of 2026-08-03 and 2026-08-04 in particular, keep their text untouched because this section is append only, and this entry supersedes them on that one point rather than editing them. What those entries said was accurate about the executor's own conduct, which is all it was ever entitled to say, and inaccurate only as a claim about where the remote stood, which was never the executor's to state and which the owner could move a second after the line was written. Current state recorded here as a dated observation and nothing more: at the time of writing main is 677cdc3 locally, the local remote-tracking ref origin/main sits one commit behind it at 2c33d0f with a ref mtime of 16:58 today, and a fetch at 17:30 did not move it. That is what a local ref said at one moment, it is not a property of the remote, and it is stale the instant the owner pushes again, which is exactly why the ruling forbids carrying it forward as fact. Second item, landed in the same commit: the A1 ISSUES entry read "six deliberate takeovers" while listing seven timestamps, 16:00:22, 16:24:55, 16:29:57, 16:33:01, 16:36:02, 16:37:54 and 16:39:54. 2026-08-04: Stage 5a steps 1 to 3, code and SPEC and vectors, third entry for the date. Three owner rulings recorded FINAL first: the negative-gap treatment in the chain walk, the vectors-into-the-repository decision with its sync direction, and the SweepPeriodSeconds wording, which closes an item that had been open since the pre-batch verification of 2026-07-30. Step 1, the primitive: AgHaltUncleanCount is replaced by AgHaltUncleanChain in Persist.mqh, a backward walk from the newest record that stops at the first clean record or the first adjacent init pair further apart than the bound. The substantive change is that TimeLocal no longer participates in the comparison at all. The old function anchored a rolling window on now, so a clean record reset nothing; the chain is anchored on adjacent init pairs only, which is exactly what makes one clean record reset it. The call site in AccountGuardian.mq5 takes the new function, the CrashLoopWindowSeconds default moves 60 to 300, and both the SAFE_HALT reason string and the verbose check line are reworded to name the chain and the gap bound rather than a window. Untouched by design: the halt file format, AgHaltAppendSession, AgHaltMarkClean, and every config refusal check. AG_MAX_SESSIONS is 32 against a worst case chain of 4, so pruning cannot truncate a chain that matters. Step 2, SPEC: amendment A4 added, superseding A1's rolling-window counting while stating explicitly that the file format is untouched, since this changes how existing records are read and not what is written; the transition table trigger, the input table defaults and note cells, the Phase 0 crash-loop acceptance row, and the two A1 crash-counting bullets are all reworded to match, and the negative-gap ruling is recorded in A4 with its grounds. Step 3, vectors: committed unmodified first as a baseline, then regenerated, so the change is an eleven-line diff rather than twelve files appearing from nowhere. One thing caught by checking rather than by assuming: the editing pass rewrote CRLF to LF on all eleven, silently. The CRLF form is the one proven to parse in the terminal, the LF form is proven for nothing, so the endings were restored byte for byte and verified by carriage-return and line-feed counts before commit. R2 concurrence, the one-line obligation the ruling attached, discharged: the measured 30 to 40 second kill cycle puts four kills' adjacent init pairs far inside the 300 second bound, chain reaches 4 against a limit of 3 and trips, no adjustment needed. Nothing deployed and no terminal file touched, per the owner's gate; the compile ran against the repository tree instead. Compile result: 0 errors, 0 warnings, 833 ms, ex5 written to the repository tree at 14:16:31, 40450 bytes against the deployed build's 39642. Deployment state verified unchanged by the same check: the terminal copy is still the 2026-08-03 01:40:37 e136858 build at 39642 bytes, so the running instance is untouched and still carries the old primitive, which is correct until the owner opens the deploy. One measurement fell out of it and is recorded as standing observation rule 6, because it would have inverted any future compile gate: the metaeditor64 exit code is not a status signal on this installation. The clean build exited 1 while logging 0 errors and 0 warnings, and a deliberately broken control copy of the same source exited 0 while logging 3 errors and producing no ex5. The Result line and the ex5 timestamp are the authoritative signals; the control was run precisely because a clean compile is a proof claim in this project and an unexplained non-zero exit is not something to wave through.

2026-08-04: Relaunch raced the mutex staleness window, procedure defect not a code defect, sixth entry for the date. The counted kill 1 worked exactly as intended and the evidence says so: exactly one terminal process exists, pid 20212 started 15:55:57, the previous pid 19732 is gone, and the halt file is untouched at mtime 15:51:08 with its newest record S|1785858668 still clean=0. So taskkill /F is the correct instrument and the record stayed unclean, which is what the Task Manager attempt failed to achieve. What went wrong is timing. A hard kill by construction skips OnDeinit, so the mutex heartbeat GV AG_HB_1200252169 is never zeroed and keeps the value the dying instance last wrote, refreshed every one second timer tick. AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS is 10, so a relaunch within ten seconds of the kill reads a heartbeat that is not yet stale, correctly concludes a live holder exists, and refuses. Measured timeline: last surviving LIFE line 15:55:18, replacement process start 15:55:57, refused init 15:56:00.063, so the heartbeat was at most a few seconds old when it was read. The refusal is the mutex working to specification, not a fault. Consequence folded into the A3 procedure: after every hard kill, wait at least fifteen seconds before relaunching, ten for the staleness threshold plus margin. A refused init appends NO session record, since AgHaltAppendSession sits downstream of the refusal return, so a raced relaunch does not advance the chain and does not corrupt it either, it simply wastes a cycle. Two things banked for free. One, the SAFE_HALT bypass fix is re-proven on the Stage 5a build under a genuine fresh-image refusal: the skip line fired naming why the halt file was not written, the deinit reported reason=8 with timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0 confirming a fresh image, the banner was deliberately left on the chart, and the halt file is byte-untouched with its mtime unmoved. Two, the next successful attach is now a stale-mutex takeover by construction, because the leftover heartbeat is minutes old, which is the A2 branch that the A1 row still owes and that has never been run deliberately on any build. It also serves as the discriminator against the one alternative explanation worth ruling out: if the staleness comparison were itself broken rather than merely raced, the attach would refuse again with a heartbeat two minutes old.

2026-08-04: Kill method measured WRONG before it could cost the A3 row, fifth entry for the date. Task Manager End Task on the Processes tab is NOT a hard kill for this terminal. The owner ended the task at about 15:16 and reported it as a hard kill with no clean exit, which was an honest reading of the tool's own label. The halt file says otherwise and the halt file is the authoritative shutdown witness under standing rule 4: session record 6, S|1785721251, flipped from unclean to clean=1, the file grew 138 to 139 bytes, its md5 moved 1C4336F1 to 8C4B7C97, and its mtime advanced to 15:16:25, some 42 seconds after the last LIFE line at 15:15:43. OnDeinit therefore ran to completion and marked the session clean, which means Windows delivered a graceful close request rather than terminating the process. This matches the 2026-08-03 measured clean close exactly, where a WM_CLOSE produced the same clean flag. The journal holds no deinit lines at all, zero non-LIFE lines for the whole day, which is standing rule 4's own scenario and is why the halt file rather than the journal settled it. Consequence, and it is the whole value of the pre-relaunch gate: had this method been used for the counted kills, every one would have written a clean record, the chain would have reset at every step, and A3 would have been reported as a failure to trip when in fact nothing was ever being measured. Nothing is lost, because this kill's only job was unloading the old image so the relaunch loads the new one, and it did that. Corrected method for every kill from here: taskkill /F /IM terminal64.exe, where /F terminates the process outright and gives OnDeinit no chance to run. The discriminator after each kill is the halt file, not the journal and not the tool's wording: a genuine hard kill leaves the newest record at clean=0.

2026-07-31: Morning harvest, three findings, two of them contradicting expectations and both measured rather than argued. One: the machine neither slept nor died. The 02:30 sleep never happened: LIFE cadence runs unbroken from the 22:50:18 attach to 15:16:48 with no stamp gap above 60 seconds anywhere, seconds_in_state at 59191 counting back exactly to the attach, the same terminal pid 9056, the halt file byte-identical at md5 0D7C8493 with mtime still 22:50:17, and zero non-LIFE lines today. The two predicted sleep artefacts (seconds_in_state jump, stale mutex heartbeat on wake) never materialised because their premise never occurred; neither the slept nor the crashed branch of the morning question applies, and no refusing restore existed to check. Cause of the missed sleep not established; candidate is that some process held a wake lock overnight, not investigated further. Two: the A8 window came in BELOW THRESHOLD on 72ed960, which is not the same as failed and is worded that way on owner instruction. The only no-tick stretch all night was server frozen at 23:59:56 for 10 LIFE lines, stamps 00:00:18 to 00:04:48, resuming 00:05:19, about 5.4 minutes against the 15 minute minimum. Mechanism, and it matters beyond tonight: TimeCurrent is terminal-wide, the last known server time across ALL Market Watch symbols, and the US100.ecn and US500.ecn charts opened during last night's fix proof subscribed index symbols that tick through gold's break. The 112 minute window measured the night before existed only because gold was the quietest constraint then; the extra symbols shrank it to the sliver both markets share. Operative rule: A8's window is set by the quietest subscribed symbol, not by the chart symbol. Three: today is Friday, so the weekend close guarantees an arbitrarily long no-tick window tonight regardless of Market Watch contents; A8 collects over the weekend close with no symbol surgery needed. Standing observation rule 5 added by owner order. A8 evidence pointer: the 20260730 and 20260731 journal analysis in this entry, full scan reproducible from the logs.

2026-07-29: Correction-2 investigation, ordered before the kill rows, found a SAFE_HALT bypass by source read rather than by test. AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS is 10 s. A refused init appends no session record: OnInit returns INIT_FAILED at :131, while AgHaltLoad is at :136 and AgHaltAppendSession at :163, both downstream. Worse than the owner's framing: OnDeinit then wrote the empty model over the halt file and the GV mirror turned that into a false manual resume. Recorded in the SPEC threat model as prevented, with the general never-loaded-never-written obligation and a best-effort caveat on manual-resume detection. Both fixes written, neither compiled nor deployed, held for step 1 of the sequence so the bug is demonstrated against the unfixed build. Halt file backed up to scratchpad and to docs/evidence/ before anything touched it. Ratified AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS to FINAL and logged AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS as a deliberate non-input. A5 stands as PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION, not PASS: no trade API exists in this build, so the row proves nothing about Phase 3 and carries forward to the sweep engine. Withdrew the earlier proposal to run A3 at CrashLoopWindowSeconds = 600 on the owner's correction: it is kept only as a diagnostic fallback to separate a broken mechanism from a window too tight, and the real default is ruled after measurement. Clock offset established from the session records: server UTC, machine UTC+3.

## DECISIONS

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-09-01, given in chat, recorded verbatim) "THE ACCOUNT TRADES EVERYTHING AND EVERYTHING IS LOCKED. The guardian's scope is the whole account, every symbol, every source, mobile included, which the 2026-08-29 and 2026-08-31 evidence proves. BTCUSD.ecn STAYS IN MARKET WATCH PERMANENTLY, so that the guardian's clock, TimeCurrent, follows the account's real trading hours including weekends. The weekend is a trading day: day anchors roll at 01:00 every day including Saturday and Sunday. The weekend is no longer a guaranteed no-tick window, and any earlier test or entry that assumed one is read under this ruling from now on. The 2026-08-01 FINAL's removal of BTCUSD.ecn is superseded in that clause; its frozen-quote protections stand unchanged for any freeze that still occurs." What this entry supersedes, by name and exactly how far: THE 2026-08-01 INSTRUMENT SCOPE FINAL (owner ruling 2026-08-01), its instrument list clause, "Market Watch is XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn only. BTCUSD.ecn is removed and never returns.", and its weekend no-tick assumption clause, "All subscribed instruments are 24/5, so the weekend close is a reliable no-tick window by design.", AND THOSE TWO CLAUSES ONLY. Every other clause of that FINAL stands untouched and is reaffirmed here: the account still trades gold, NASDAQ and silver among whatever else this ruling adds under "everything", XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn remain in Market Watch, and the frozen-upper-bound consequence clause is not argued here in either direction.
Reason: The mechanism the owner ruled on: with a 24/7 instrument absent from Market Watch, a weekend trade placed from the mobile app would run against a guardian whose clock, TimeCurrent, is frozen for the whole weekend, since nothing ticks to advance it, so the guardian's day anchors and its enforcement would sit stale exactly when a mobile trade could move the account. That is the configuration to avoid, not the one to keep. BTCUSD.ecn's continuous quote is what keeps TimeCurrent advancing through the weekend, which is why it stays in Market Watch permanently rather than being treated as an incidental presence.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-31, given in chat, recorded verbatim) FOUR RULINGS CLOSING VERSION 1 OF THE REALIZED PEAK TRAILING FLOOR. ONE, on the implementation readings the 2026-08-27 ACTIONS entry raised as readings and not rulings: "Implementation readings accepted: AgPeakUpdate calling AgRealizedRunFold itself (a second call, one fold, shape 1 FINAL satisfied in letter); the peak anchor moves forward only; a backward step holds the peak; AgPeakUpdate runs after the HistoryDealsTotal read so the Q9 count stays Phase 1's." TWO, on build identity: "Build identity of version 1 as deployed: AccountGuardian.ex5 md5 124BA610201A6D67C050572C6A84DE37, 86866 bytes, compiled by the owner in MetaEditor on 2026-08-27 from source md5 6CF5F6A78E8D9A333D84780C33DE475A (identical to commit 428614c's AccountGuardian.mq5) with Persist.mqh and Pnl.mqh from the same commit copied by the owner into the Terminal Include folder; 0 errors 0 warnings; md5 measured by the owner at source and destination. The executor's 2A3320AA build was never deployed. Every version 1 row closure cites this identity." THREE, on the 2026-08-29 rollover: "The 2026-08-29 rollover firing at 12:48:51 is RULED BEHAVIOR under Q7 (TimeCurrent is the only clock; a frozen feed freezes the anchor), not a defect. Breach detection is not tick gated: lock level lines fired every 30s through the freeze." FOUR, on the 2026-08-30 boot-derived lock: "The 2026-08-30 15:45:59 boot-derived lock is EXPLAINED AND ACCEPTED: the machine lost battery power at 15:14 (owner), the owner traded from the mobile app while the terminal was down and lost about 192 net, and on restart the boot derivation replayed server history and locked within 3 seconds. Recorded as an incidental finding: the guardian catches trades made while it was offline, because the authority is server history."
Reason: These four rulings are the basis on which all four version 1 acceptance rows, V1-A through V1-D, close in the 2026-09-01 ACTIONS entry, and on which the 2026-08-29 and 2026-08-30 incidental findings are recorded as explained rather than open. Given in chat per the process ruling of 2026-07-29 that only the owner marks an entry FINAL.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-24, given in chat, recorded verbatim as owner ruling) THE REALIZED PEAK TRAILING FLOOR IS RATIFIED AS VERSION 1, AND IT IS REALIZED ONLY. The daily allowance stops being measured from the day anchor alone and starts being measured from the day's realized high water mark, so an intraday gain that is given back is caught rather than absorbed. NINE ELEMENTS, as ruled. ONE, WHAT RAISES THE PEAK (D1.1). Only trade closures, INCLUDING PARTIAL CLOSURES, raise the day's realized peak. Floating profit NEVER raises it. An unrealized gain that is never closed leaves the floor exactly where it was. TWO, WHAT THE FLOOR IS CHECKED AGAINST (D1.2). The floor is checked against FULL ACCOUNT EQUITY INCLUDING FLOATING PnL. An open position diving below the floor locks IMMEDIATELY and does not wait to be closed. The two halves of this ruling are deliberately asymmetric and the asymmetry is the mechanism: floating can only ever trigger the lock, never move the floor that triggers it. THREE, THE PEAK IS MONOTONE WITHIN THE DAY (D1.3). The realized peak only rises intraday. A losing deal after a gain does not lower it. It resets at the day boundary and at no other moment. FOUR, STRICTER ALWAYS WINS (D2). The effective floor is max(existing ratchet floor, realized peak minus limit), IN EVERY SCENARIO, INCLUDING RESTART AND DAY ROLLOVER. NO EXCEPTIONS. Neither mechanism may loosen what the other has tightened, at any moment, by any path. FIVE, RESTART RECONSTRUCTS FROM HISTORY (D3.1). After ANY restart the realized peak is RECONSTRUCTED FROM TODAY'S DEAL HISTORY. The persisted value is a CROSS CHECK ONLY and is never the source of truth. ON MISMATCH, TAKE THE STRICTER OF THE TWO. This composes with element FOUR rather than competing with it: reconstruction feeds the max, it does not bypass it. SIX, ONE CLOCK (D3.2). The peak resets at THE EXACT SAME DAY BOUNDARY already locked FINAL for the daily base. ONE CLOCK, NO NEW BOUNDARY DEFINITION. No constant, no input and no helper in this work may define a second boundary, and a future amendment to the existing boundary moves the peak with the base automatically and needs no second ruling. SEVEN, STORAGE IS A SEPARATE FILE (D6). The peak lives in its own `peak_<login>.dat` written through `AgAtomicWrite`. PHASE 2 OPEN QUESTION SEVEN IS NOT SUPERSEDED IN ANY CLAUSE and `floor_<login>.dat` STAYS BYTE IDENTICAL to what is deployed today. The widened file alternative that the planning draft carried is REMOVED rather than left standing as an option, so that no later session reads it as still available. EIGHT, THE PEAK TERM USES THE POST RATCHET VALUE (D7). peak_level = realized peak minus `enforced_limit`, where `enforced_limit` is the value `AgRatchetUpdate` returns and not the raw live limit. RULED AS THE STRICTER READING OF ELEMENT FOUR: tighter whenever the ratchet is holding, identical when it is not. NINE, THE PEAK DOES NOT ENTER THE LOCK SNAPSHOT (D8). The peak is PRE BREACH ONLY, EXACTLY AS THE RATCHET IS. The locked window is judged by the snapshot alone, per the existing Q6/F7 FINAL of 2026-07-29, which this entry does not touch. `state_<login>.dat` and `AG_STATE_FORMAT_VERSION` ARE UNTOUCHED, per the two FINAL rulings of 2026-08-20 recorded on the `phase3-defect-fixes` branch. SCOPE, RULED EXPLICITLY (D5). VERSION 1 IS REALIZED ONLY. Anything involving a floating peak or an equity peak is DEFERRED TO THE MIRROR REPO and no part of it enters this build. The limit amount itself is UNCHANGED and was not discussed. SEQUENCING (D4). P3-1 AND P49/P53 CLOSE ON THE CURRENTLY DEPLOYED BUILD BEFORE VERSION 1 REACHES MT5. Planning and design for version 1 run in parallel with those rows and do not wait on them, but no version 1 binary is deployed while either is open. THE COST IS STATED HERE RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER. This mechanism fires more locks than the current one, by construction, and some of them will feel early to an operator who was up on the day and gave part of it back. That is the ruling working, not the ruling misfiring, and no later session may raise the increased lock rate as a defect. Under the Phase 2 open question TWO interim posture those locks still send no order, so between the lock and the owner's manual close the floating loss remains unbounded by the guardian; version 1 tightens WHEN the state machine locks and changes nothing about what the guardian can do once it has.
What this entry supersedes, by name, and exactly how far: PHASE 2 OPEN QUESTION FIVE (owner ruling 2026-08-18), THE CLAUSE "NO new persisted artifact is added", AND THAT CLAUSE ONLY. Version 1 persists the realized peak. EVERY OTHER CLAUSE OF QUESTION FIVE STANDS UNCHANGED AND IS REAFFIRMED HERE: total exposure is still the framing, the daily limit still counts ALL open floating loss regardless of when it was earned, NO floating baseline snapshot is taken at the anchor, and RE LOCKING EACH DAY ON A HELD LOSER IS STILL INTENDED BEHAVIOUR. The clause is superseded on the narrow ground the entry's own Reason states: the artifact it refused was a FLOATING BASELINE snapshotted at every anchor, creating a second source of truth about the day that could disagree with the first. The realized peak is not that artifact and does not create that second source of truth, because element FIVE makes broker history the source of truth and the file a cross check. THIS IS THE ONLY CLAUSE THIS ENTRY SUPERSEDES ANYWHERE. Two further FINAL entries are CONSTRAINED BUT NOT SUPERSEDED, recorded here so that no later session reads the version 1 work as having quietly moved them. PHASE 2 OPEN QUESTION SEVEN (owner ruling 2026-08-18) is untouched in all five of its elements under element SEVEN above: the single scalar `floor_currency`, `floor_<login>.dat`, `AgAtomicWrite`, RESEED AT ROLLOVER, NO RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP, and `min(limit, floor)` applying PRE BREACH ONLY all stand exactly as ratified, and element FOUR consumes the ratchet's output rather than replacing its arithmetic. DEFECT 3 SHAPE 1 (owner ruling 2026-08-20, second of that date, recorded on the `phase3-defect-fixes` branch) is likewise untouched: its clause freezing `AgRealizedFold` to its current signature and its two outputs with no second history walk added is satisfied IN LETTER by version 1, because the running maximum the peak needs is produced by the SAME single sorted walk that already produces the running minimum, so no second walk exists to add and no signature moves.
